# qwark wire protocol, version 1

Revision 1.1 (2026-09-07): SessionInfo grew to 164 bytes (16 readouts), Feature is 48 bytes with a `readout` field, LEVELFLAGS_SET added.
Revision 1.2 (2026-09-08): Feature flags bit2 SAVE_ASIDE and bit3 LOAD_ASIDE mark the savefile-helper actions so a client can drive the save-file manager without matching labels.
Revision 1.3 (2026-09-08): Feature flags bit4 LIVE marks a TOGGLE whose state qwark reads back out of game memory, and UNLOCK_LIST now carries four `UnlockFieldDesc` rows that name and type its four per-entry value slots.
Revision 1.4 (2026-09-09): the autosplit event stream. AUTOSPLIT_EVENTS and AUTOSPLIT_DESCRIBE in the 0x00A0 block, and a 20-byte `QE` datagram pushed to every telemetry subscriber the moment a run event happens. See section 8.
Revision 1.5 (2026-09-09): autosplit timing. The Event's second word is `time_ms` rather than a tick count, the kinds gained 6 LOAD_START and 7 LOAD_END, and EventDesc is 32 bytes with a `param_us` and two new flags, FLAT and NORMALISE, that carry the game-time adjustments the old ASL scripts made. See section 8.
Revision 1.6 (2026-09-09): SessionInfo `flags` gained bit1 EMULATOR and bit2 NO_CODE_PATCHES, so a client can tell that qwark is driving RPCS3 through PINE rather than a console and that every WRITES_CODE feature is refused there. See section 3.2.
Revision 1.7 (2026-09-09): signed VALUEs. The first of Feature's two pad bytes is now `bits`, the width in bits of the field behind a VALUE, and Feature flags bit5 SIGNED says that field is two's complement in that width. See section 5.3.2.
Revision 1.8 (2026-09-09): COMBO_SUSPEND, which holds every stored combo off while a client captures a new one, so the buttons being recorded do not also fire the combos already there. See section 5.9.
Revision 1.9 (2026-09-10): the savefile block. SAVEFILE_INFO, SAVEFILE_READ and SAVEFILE_WRITE in the 0x00B0 block. A save no longer travels as a file: qwark embeds one helper per game, installs it invisibly on first use, and the helper parks the save in a RAM buffer these three ops stream. See section 5.12.
Revision 1.10 (2026-09-10): the savefile library moves onto the console. SAVEFILE_CATEGORIES, SAVEFILE_LIST, SAVEFILE_STORE, SAVEFILE_RESTORE and SAVEFILE_CATEGORY at 0x00B3, FILE_RENAME at 0x0079, and a SAVEFILE_INFO grown to 20 bytes that reports the copy qwark now runs between a file and the aside buffer. See section 5.13.
Revision 1.11 (2026-09-15): `QWARK_MAX_PAYLOAD` is **16384**. One frame, request or reply, carries at most that, so every op that used to name 65536 — MEM_READ, FILE_READ, FILE_WRITE, SAVEFILE_READ, SAVEFILE_WRITE — is capped there too. No opcode, structure, status or field moved: this is the size of a frame and nothing else. The console holds one 16 KB request buffer and one 16 KB reply buffer for every connection to share, instead of allocating 192 KB of the machine's own memory per request in flight, which is what a user running qwark beside other VSH plugins actually feels. A client that sends chunks of 16000 bytes is safe against this build and against every older one.

Revision 1.12 (2026-09-16): COMBO_ENABLE at 0x0083, the user's own on/off for every combo. It is kept in the console's config as `combo.enabled`, so it survives a client restart and a console reboot the way the five masks do, and SessionInfo `flags` gained bit3 COMBOS_OFF, set while it is off, so a client draws the checkbox out of the info block it already reads. See section 5.9.

Revision 1.13 (2026-09-22): POS_EDIT at 0x004A and POS_STORE at 0x004B, the two ways a client writes a position slot without the player standing in it. POS_EDIT rewrites the three coordinates of a slot that is already filled and leaves the rest of that slot's blob - the rotation, and whatever else the game keeps in it - untouched, so a coordinate typed into a box does not throw away a facing captured in the game. POS_STORE replaces a slot's whole blob and will only take one of the running game's own length, so a blob of the wrong shape can never be stored for POS_LOAD to misread. Both act on the current planet and both persist immediately, as POS_SAVE does. See section 5.5.

Revision 1.15 (2026-09-29): the savefile helper under RPCS3. SAVEFILE_PATCH at 0x00B8 hands out the running game's helper as the (address, value) words a patch writes to put it there and the request bytes it zeroes, with a CRC stamp, and on a platform that refuses code patches the savefile block now works once an RPCS3 patch has put the helper in the game at load: qwark looks for it once a session and, finding every byte of it, behaves exactly as a console does after an install. SAVEFILE_INFO answers on that platform either way, where it used to be UNSUPPORTED, and says in `installed` whether the patch is in. Revision 1.14 is reserved for opcodes 0x0056 and 0x0057 on a parked branch and is not in this build. See sections 3.2 and 5.12.1.

Revision 1.16 (2026-09-29): mods under RPCS3, the way the savefile helper got there in 1.15. MOD_PATCH at 0x0066 hands out one mod as the (address, value) words an RPCS3 patch writes - every cave as consecutive words, then the mod's own words - and the tail bytes of any cave that is not whole words, in SAVEFILE_PATCH's layout and with the same CRC stamp. On a platform that refuses code patches MOD_LIST's `flags` bit0 LOADED now means the mod is in game memory, every cave byte and every word of it, which is how a patch RPCS3 applied at load shows up, and the new bit5 CHECKING says qwark has not looked yet this session; a console never sets bit5 and its LOADED is unchanged. MOD_LOAD, MOD_UNLOAD, auto-apply and the previous-session reapply behave there exactly as before. Separately, `flags` bit3 PREVIOUS was documented and never set: it is now set on a mod whose bit is in the previous-session record while it is not loaded again. Module build 49. See sections 3.2, 5.7 and 5.7.1.

Revision 1.17 (2026-09-29): code switches, the nine WRITES_CODE toggles under RPCS3. SWITCH_PATCH at 0x0025 hands out the running game's code switches - a trampoline in a code cave for every instruction those toggles patch on a console, and the branch that replaces each such instruction - as the (address, value) words an RPCS3 patch writes, with every flag byte the trampolines read as a byte entry at 0, in SAVEFILE_PATCH's layout and with the same CRC stamp. On a platform that refuses code patches qwark looks for them once a session, and while they are in the game SessionInfo `flags` bit4 CODE_SWITCHES is set and every WRITES_CODE toggle of the game works, as a write of its flag byte: FEATURE_SET, the read-back into `toggle_state`, auto-apply, PREVIOUS_REAPPLY and the fast loads RaC2's and Deadlocked's loads force. RaC2's, RaC3's and Deadlocked's fingerprints accept the branch at their site. Separately, RaC2's savefile helper keeps its request bytes at 0x1317728..0x131772A since this build, out of the executable's code segment, which RPCS3 makes read-only (section 5.12.1). Module build 50. See sections 3, 3.2 and 5.12.2.

Revision 1.18 (2026-09-29): the PS3 module carries nothing that only RPCS3 needs. The three patch ops, SAVEFILE_PATCH, MOD_PATCH and SWITCH_PATCH, and the looks for a savefile helper, a mod or the code switches an RPCS3 patch put in the game exist only in the emulator builds, `qwark-rpcs3.exe` and the `qwark-host.exe` simulator; they are compiled out of `qwark.sprx`, which runs where memory is tight. The PS3 module answers SAVEFILE_PATCH, MOD_PATCH and SWITCH_PATCH with `UNSUPPORTED`, where until now it answered them for information, and never sets SessionInfo `flags` bit4 CODE_SWITCHES, as before. A client only asks for the three under its RPCS3 target, so nothing a client does has to change; no opcode, structure or other status moved. Module build 51. See sections 3.2, 5.7.1, 5.12.1 and 5.12.2.

Revision 1.19 (2026-10-06): MOD_PATCH answers a page at a time, so a mod bigger than one frame can still be written as an RPCS3 patch. Until now a reply was one 16384-byte frame, at most 2046 rows, and a bigger mod (a 9.3 KB cave and a few words, say) answered `FULL`. The request is now `u8 index, u8 pad, u16 page`, still four bytes; a client before this revision sends three zero pads, which is page 0. The reply keeps its layout, with the pad after `n` now `u16 pages`. A page carries at most 2046 rows, word rows and byte rows together: page k carries word rows 2046·k up to 2046·(k+1) or the last, and the last page carries every byte row after its words. `pages` and `stamp` are the same on every page, the stamp being the CRC-32 of one unbounded reply after its stamp, so a mod that fits one page answers exactly what it did before with `pages` 1 where the pad was 0. A `page` at or past `pages` is `BAD_ARG`, and `FULL` now means only that a mod's byte rows alone would not fit one page. Module build 52. See section 5.7.1.

Revision 1.20 (2026-10-15): SAVEFILE_INFO's `error` gains 7, NO_SAVE: there was no save to act on. RaC1, RaC2 and RaC3 have no save until the game has saved or loaded once in the process (on a new game, the first autosave), and a set-aside before then - and a load with nothing set aside, which fills the aside buffer from the game's save - is refused with `BUSY` and error 7 rather than copied from wherever the game's pointer points, address 0 in RaC2; so is a load whose aside buffer holds something that is not a save of the running game, and a RESTORE whose file is not one stops with error 7 after the copy, without raising the load. No opcode, structure or field moved, and a client that does not know the value still gets the `BUSY`. Module build 53, which also makes RaC1's and RaC2's loads wait for the game's pause, moves RaC1's request bytes and aside buffer and RaC2's helper, and so changes both games' SAVEFILE_PATCH words. It moves RaC2's code switches too, out of the same routine planet Joba runs every frame, so RaC2's SWITCH_PATCH words change as well; RaC2's fingerprint goes on accepting the branch an earlier build's patch left at its site, so such a game is still recognised and reads as having switches from another build. See sections 5.12 and 5.12.2.

Revision 1.21 (2026-10-16): manip categories. MANIP_LIST, MANIP_SELECT, MANIP_OPTIONS and MANIP_APPLY at 0x00D0..0x00D3. A game describes groups of the RAM writes speedrunners call manips, each for one or more leaderboard categories; the client picks one, and qwark applies its setup once per game boot and its per-run writes on every file load (a save loaded or a new game started) while auto-reset is on, after checking the file's challenge-mode counter both ways. The options live in qwark's config.txt, so they work without a PC. The old manip buttons and toggles are retired, ids and all: RaC1 7, 8, 10, 11 and 16; RaC2 15 to 22; UYA 16, 18, 19 and 20; Deadlocked 12. MANIP_LIST also carries a list of headers, features that belong beside the manips: Deadlocked's "Fixes" (Crash patches and Fix reset softlocks, ids, behaviour and auto flags unchanged) and UYA's "Trophies" (Reset trophies, 21, and a new toggle, 39 Reset trophies on Veldin load, off by default), each also a DESCRIBE group of the same name. UYA's reset deletes the console's trophy data for the game and then has the game re-register its trophies on a thread of the savefile helper's, while the game goes on running; it is refused under RPCS3. The same build fixes Deadlocked's Fast loads, which hung the game on a black screen whenever the flight had a movie to play, and reworks them on RaC2's model: the screen goes black at the start of the load and stays black to the level's first frame, no flight is flown or shown, the level is read with no wait between its steps, and a queued movie plays first, at once, instead of after a segment of flight. Nothing on the wire changes, but the code switches behind that checkbox under RPCS3 changed, so a patch written by an earlier build is asked for again (section 5.12.2). RaC1's PLANET_LOAD and LOAD_ASIDE, and RaC2's LOAD_ASIDE as before, force fast loads on for the length of the load and put the checkbox's setting back when it is over (sections 3.2 and 5.12). RaC2 also gets two crash guards that are no feature: a planet change qwark makes to or from Dobbo Orbit, Damosel Orbit or the Jamming Array, with an in-space story cutscene queued, crashed the game. qwark writes the guards into the game itself as the session enters RaC2 on a console, and under RPCS3 RaC2's SWITCH_PATCH carries them as two more trampolines with no flag byte, so a patch from an earlier build is asked for again there too (section 5.12.2). UYA's Fast loads (38) become a code patch and gain `WRITES_CODE`, as RaC1's and RaC2's are: the old data words hung the game on a black screen on a trip that owes one of Aquatos's story scenes, and the patch lets the scene play and, as RaC2's does, keeps the screen black from the start of a load to the new level's first frame with no ship clip shown, so UYA's PLANET_LOAD forces fast loads for every planet now, Aquatos included, and its LOAD_ASIDE forces them as RaC1's and RaC2's do; under RPCS3 they are a second code switch, so UYA's SWITCH_PATCH words change and a Freeze-ammo-only patch reads as one from another build (sections 5.12.2 and 5.12.3). Same module build, 53, which is unreleased. See section 5.14.

Revision 1.21, the toggle tidy (2026-10-16, same build 53): RaC1's Max ammo, all weapons (17), RaC2's Weapon insta-upgrades (4) and UYA's One-hit KO (2) are retired, ids and all (section 3.1). Freeze health in all four games (RaC1 2, RaC2 2, UYA 1, Deadlocked 4) holds the hit points at the game's own maximum as it is now, health upgrades and all, rather than at a fixed number: it keeps a freeze entry on them, which FREEZE_LIST shows like any other, moves its value as the game's maximum moves, and while the game has no maximum yet holds nothing. RaC1's is id 2, which was Infinite health, the code patch at the fingerprint word 0x7F558: it keeps its id, so its combos, auto flag and previous-session record carry over, and loses `WRITES_CODE`; the Freeze health row RaC1 had for a while beside it, id 28, is retired. UYA's Freeze ammo (0) is labelled Infinite ammo, its patch unchanged. The four cheats every game has lead the Cheats group in all four, in one order, Fast loads, Ghost Ratchet, the ammo cheat (Infinite ammo, but Deadlocked's Refill ammo, which fills the ammo at a mission's start on purpose) and Freeze health, each game's other cheats after them; only the rows moved. A toggle's config.txt auto flag set under its old label still counts (section 3.1). And the first application of a manip group in a boot is announced whatever it wrote (section 5.14): a group with per-run writes alone, all of RaC1's and RaC2's Any% and 100%, never was. No opcode or structure changed.

Revision 1.21, loads during a cutscene (2026-10-17, same build 53): RaC2's savefile helper takes the game into its pause by writing the mode word, as RaC1's does, and loads in the same call, rather than asking the game's mode change for the pause and waiting a frame for it, which still failed during a cutscene. Before it writes the mode it closes a movie that is playing and empties the cutscene's queue, as the Start button's skip does, and drops the callback the cutscene's end would have run. A RaC2 load is now also refused while a trip in the ship is queued, and while the console holds the game in its own freeze, and no longer in debug mode. RaC1's and RaC3's loads still wait for a frame of the pause, and their wait now runs out after ten seconds of the console's clock rather than after 600 calls of the helper. No opcode or structure changed; RaC1's, RaC2's and RaC3's SAVEFILE_PATCH words and stamps did, so a patch written before this change is asked for again. See section 5.12.

Revision 1.21, the review's follow-ups (2026-10-17, same build 53): a load of anything qwark itself put in the aside buffer - a RESTORE's file, or SAVEFILE_WRITE - is refused with error 7 when its first word is 0, in all four games, rather than handed to the helper, which takes that word for an empty buffer and loads the game's own last save; a RESTORE of such a file used to report success having loaded the game's save. SAVEFILE_INFO's `error` describes the last request: a set-aside or a load clears it as it starts, as a STORE or a RESTORE always has. A set-aside or a load asked for while a STORE or a RESTORE runs is `BUSY` with error 5, where it used to go out over the buffer the transfer was copying. A load's fast-load force belongs to that load: a request that fails takes back its own force and no earlier load's (RaC1's, RaC2's and Deadlocked's PLANET_LOAD, and RaC1's and RaC2's set-aside loads); a set-aside load the helper drops puts back only its own force, and a RESTORE's dropped load none; RaC1 watches for the end of the load its force was for, so a set-aside load asked for in a planet's fly-in is no longer taken for that planet load's end; and under RPCS3 the end of a load nothing forced writes nothing, and a fast-load flag a force finds up is the user's, as RaC3's already were (sections 5.12 and 5.12.2). Deadlocked's file-load trigger acts only if the tutorial byte still reads 0 on the tick after its drop (section 5.14). Freeze health (the toggle tidy, above) leaves a freeze the user set on the same hit points alone, value and all, also when it goes off, and puts its own entry back once the user takes theirs out. UYA's Reset trophies (21) and its toggle (39) are left out of DESCRIBE where they can never work, under RPCS3 and with `savefile_helper = 0` (section 5.14). Under RPCS3 without the code switches' patch, which carries RaC2's crash guards there, RaC2's PLANET_LOAD refuses a trip the guards are for with `UNSUPPORTED` (sections 5.5 and 5.12.2). No opcode, structure or status changed, and no patch word.

Revision 1.21, planets (2026-10-17, same build 53): PLANET_LIST carries one more byte per planet after the names, the order a menu lists the planets in, which is the order the game visits them rather than the order it numbers them; the index a name sits at is still the planet's id, and every request still carries the id. UYA's and Deadlocked's orders are the old client's combo boxes, the others' are their ids in turn, and a client before this build reads the names and stops, so it sees no change. UYA's PLANET_LOAD now acts on bit1 of the planet flags, reset special bolts, which it ignored: it takes back the titanium bolts collected on the destination planet, as RaC1's takes back its gold bolts, so a client offers the box for UYA against a module of this build or later and not against one before it. UYA's Unlock all titanium bolts (24) and Reset all titanium bolts (25) cover the game's whole bolt table, 296 bytes from 0xECE54D, where they wrote 128 bytes from sixteen bytes before it and missed every planet from Koros on. No opcode, id or status changed. See section 5.5.

Revision 1.21, the planet selection per game (2026-10-17, same build 53): the planet PLANET_SELECT picks and the planet flags beside it, the two reset boxes, are one game's rather than one setting for the console, as the manip options are. config.txt keeps them as `selected.<game>.planet` and `selected.<game>.planet_flags`, `<game>` being `rac1` to `rac4` as in the manips' keys, and a game with no key of its own yet reads the console-wide `selected.planet` and `selected.planet_flags` an earlier build wrote, which are never written again, so a selection made before this build is not lost. SessionInfo's `selected_planet` and `planet_flags` are the running game's, PLANET_SELECT sets the running game's, and a PLANET_LOAD of 0xFF, the load-planet combo's among them, loads the running game's: a box ticked in RaC1 is no longer ticked in UYA. Outside a game - at the XMB, through the next boot, and in a title no game table claims - both fields and PLANET_SELECT are the last game's that was INGAME, which is the game a client goes on drawing there; the first block that says a game is INGAME carries that game's selection. Before any game has run since the module loaded both fields are 0 and PLANET_SELECT answers `UNSUPPORTED`. No opcode, structure or field moved; only what the two fields and the two ops mean did. See sections 3, 5.5 and 5.10.

Revision 1.21, the trophy status (2026-10-18, same build 53): an ACTION's `readout`, always 0xFF until now, may name a **status readout**, a word that says what the action is doing and how it last ended (section 5.3). UYA's Reset trophies (21) is the only one, and names readout 12, "Trophy reset", new and last in UYA's list: whether a reset is running and at which step (starting, deleting, registering, and which registration), whether the trophies are ready and how many seconds the reset took, or why it failed, with a count of the resets that have ended so a client can tell a new result from an old one (section 5.14). A boot puts it back to "no reset this boot". Where the reset can never work, under RPCS3 and with `savefile_helper = 0`, DESCRIBE leaves the readout's name out with the two rows, so UYA describes twelve readouts there and thirteen elsewhere. A reset that ends well no longer shows "Trophies reset: ready to win (N s)" on the console's screen, which would distract a runner mid-attempt; the failures still show theirs. A client before this revision takes a readout only from a VALUE, an ENUM or a COLOR, so it reads Reset trophies' row as it always did and sees one more readout name. No opcode or structure changed.

Revision 1.22 (2026-10-18, module build 54): the console menu, a menu on the TV for a console with no PC beside it (section 9). Combo action 5, OPEN_MENU, opens it, and closes it when it is up. It is the first combo with a mask of its own, L1 + R1 + L2 + R2 + L3 + R3 (0x060F), the one combo COMBO_ENABLE does not hold off (revision 1.23 holds it like the rest), and COMBO_LIST's sixth entry (section 5.9). Two config.txt keys come with it, `combo.menu` and `menu.enabled` (section 5.10). The menu is drawn by a renderer qwark writes into the game a few seconds into a session, which also makes the game's own Help row in its pause menu the menu's way in, and the two talk through a mailbox in game memory; neither is on the wire, and section 9 describes both for information. All four games have renderers in the same build, Deadlocked's first, and only on a console. No opcode, structure or status changed. A client that predates this revision reads the sixth COMBO_LIST entry as an action it does not know.

Revision 1.22, the console menu's round 3 (2026-10-19, same build 54): MANIP_SELECT writes nothing any more, whatever is in play: a group's setup goes in at the next start of a run that passes its check, with its per-run writes, or by MANIP_APPLY (section 5.14). Build 53 put the setup in at once when a compatible file was in play, so stepping through the categories on the console menu applied, or refused, each group on the way. On the console menu, which is not on the wire (section 9): the root and the pause-menu row that opens it say Trainer; the menu opens on its root, the cursor on its first row, whenever it was closed; a choice shows its bare value; a game may put the Unlocks page's actions on a page of their own (UYA's Collectables); a VALUE row with bounds carries its numbers for a native slider; and the renderer's core keeps two text sets so a screen going keeps its own text to its last frame. No opcode or structure changed.

Revision 1.22, the VSH's memory (2026-10-19, same build 54): VSH_MEMORY at 0x0007 and SessionInfo `flags` bit5 VSH_MEMORY. The module reads how much of the VSH's own memory is free - the memory its image and stacks, every other plugin and the XMB come out of - at module start, at each game's arrival, when the console menu's renderer goes in and every ten seconds, keeps the latest reading, the lowest since it started and the reading at start, and logs them to qwark.log. VSH_MEMORY hands them to a client; bit5 says the module has a reading to hand, so a client asks only then, and a module before this one (which answers `UNKNOWN_OP`) and RPCS3 (`UNSUPPORTED`) are never asked. It is a control op, answered through a launch. Nothing else changed: SessionInfo is still 164 bytes and bit5 was always 0. See sections 1.1, 3 and 5.1.1.

Revision 1.23 (2026-10-20, module build 55): two settings of the console's own, and a change to the menu combo. **MENU_HELP** at 0x0092, `u8 on`, writes config.txt's new key `menu.help` (1 when the file has never named it): at 1 the console menu's Trainer row takes the place of the game's Help row in its pause menu, as it did in build 54; at 0 the Help row is the game's own in every game while the rest of the renderer stays in, so the menu combo still opens the menu while the combos are on. A change swaps the Help row's words alone, in the running game, at the first moment nothing can be reading the pause menu's rows, and waits for it otherwise (section 9.2). SessionInfo `flags` bit6 MENU_HELP_OFF is set while the key is 0, so a client draws its box from the info block as it does the combo switch; a module before this one never sets it, which reads right, since its menu always took the row. MENU_HELP answers in any session state, `BAD_ARG` for anything but 0 or 1, and `UNSUPPORTED` where code cannot be patched, which has no menu. **Combo action 6, TOGGLE_FAST_LOADS**, flips the running game's Fast loads toggle through FEATURE_SET's own code, as a client's checkbox does, and the console says "Fast loads on" or "Fast loads off", or why not; it has no mask out of the box (key `combo.fastloads`), the COMBO_ENABLE switch holds it off like the first five, and under RPCS3 it is refused, with a notification, until the code switches are in, as the checkbox is. COMBO_LIST therefore has seven entries. **The OPEN_MENU combo now obeys the COMBO_ENABLE switch**: revision 1.22 spared it, as the quick way into the menu that would switch the combos back on, but the pause menu's Trainer row is there to open the menu from, so the user's Combos switch holds every combo off, this one too; the combo keeps its default mask and stays rebindable, and COMBO_SUSPEND held it all along. With `menu.help` at 0 and the combos off the menu has no way in until one of the two is back on. No structure changed. See sections 3, 3.2, 5.9, 5.10 and 9.2.

Revision 1.23, the fixes (client 1.4.1): Deadlocked's PLANET_LOAD unlocks a locked planet only in a save file's game held still, and is `BUSY` for one anywhere else (the front end, multiplayer, a load), with nothing written; Unlock all planets (feature 11) is `BUSY` there too. In co-op it loads co-op's level, the planet's id plus 20, as the game's own travel does, where it loaded the single-player level with co-op's rules on (section 5.5). The console menu, which is not on the wire: a row's refusal goes when its two seconds are up or its page goes, a save library page reads each folder once per build, and a save row's press is refused as stale if the category's listing has changed since the page was built (section 9). No opcode, structure or status changed.

Revision 1.23, ui.pak (client 1.4.1, same build 55): the console menu's four renderers leave the module for a file on the console's HDD, `/dev_hdd0/qwark/ui.pak`, which `make` writes beside `qwark.sprx` and a release ships with it; the module reads the running game's renderer from it as the menu goes into the game, and takes only the pak built with it (section 9.10). SessionInfo `flags` bit7 UI_PAK_BAD is set while that file is missing, damaged or another build's, so a client can write the right one: FILE_OPEN, FILE_WRITE and FILE_CLOSE to `ui.pak.new`, FILE_DELETE of `ui.pak`, FILE_RENAME into its place, and the module looks at it again within a tick of a file op on its path (section 5.8). A pak left as `ui.pak.new` by an update is taken at the next module start. Separately, the console keeps one 16384-byte frame for every connection's bulk requests where it kept two, a request and a reply; nothing changed for a client that sends what this document describes, but a request longer than 937 bytes is answered from the connection's own buffer, so its reply is `FULL` (or `BAD_ARG`, where an op checks its length against the room) past 937 bytes, and no op takes a request that long and answers more (section 1.1). No opcode or structure changed. Without its pak a module still works in full but for the console menu; RPCS3 never needs one.

Revision 1.24, settings profiles (2026-10-22, module build 56): a set of settings per console user, behind a console-wide switch that is off out of the box (section 5.10.1). Three ops: **PROFILE_INFO** at 0x0093 (the active profile and the signed-in user, 64 bytes), **PROFILE_ENABLE** at 0x0094 (`u8 on, u8 setup`: the switch, and the signed-in user's profile made as it goes on, a copy of the console's per-user settings or empty) and **COMBO_OVERRIDE** at 0x0095 (`u8 action, u8 set, u8 pad[2], u32 mask`: one combo's own mask in the active profile, or none). SessionInfo byte 29, a pad byte until now, is `flags2`, whose bit0 is the USB mods' and whose bits 1 to 3 are the profile's: bit1 PROFILES, the capability bit (the module has profiles and the console has users; never set by qwark-rpcs3), bit2 PROFILES_ON, bit3 PROFILE_ACTIVE; byte 30 is `profile_seq`, which moves whenever the profile behind the settings changes. The byte after each COMBO_LIST action, a pad byte until now, is that row's flags, bit0 OVERRIDDEN. With a profile active the per-user keys of config.txt, the position slots, which mods and toggles apply themselves and the overridden combos are the user's; the rest is the console's (section 5.10.1 lists every key). The module looks for a new user once a second at the XMB and never in a game. Since build 56 config.txt and the position files are written as `<file>.new` and then renamed into place.

Revision 1.24, mods on a USB drive (module build 56): the console reads mods off its USB drives, at `/dev_usb00N/qwark/mods/<TITLEID>/<dir>/` and `/dev_usb00N/mods/<TITLEID>/<dir>/` for N in 0, 1, 2, 3, 6 and 7, and lists them as available rather than installed. **MOD_USB_LIST** at 0x0067, no request, answers `u8 n, UsbMod[n]`, 120 bytes a row: the drive, how the USB copy stands against the console's own (AVAILABLE, SAME, NEWER, OLDER, CONFLICT or BROKEN), whether a NEWER copy waits because the mod is loaded, which of the two places it is in, and the hash, folder name, name, version and author. SessionInfo `flags2` (byte 29, beside the profiles' bits) bit0 USB_MODS says the module reads USB drives and answers the op; it is never set by qwark-rpcs3, which answers MOD_USB_LIST `UNSUPPORTED` and never reads a USB folder. **MOD_LOAD** of a folder only a USB drive has copies it into the console's library and loads it, and MOD_LOAD of an installed mod first takes a newer USB copy of it, and of anything it depends on that is not loaded, as do the auto-load at boot and PREVIOUS_REAPPLY; a mod that is loaded is never replaced, and its update waits for its next load. **MOD_SET_AUTO** on of a USB-only folder copies it in and sets the flag. **MOD_RESCAN** reads the drives again too. A newer copy is one whose `#- version` is greater as dotted numbers; with no version to compare, or an equal one and other contents, the console's copy stays. A copy is built in `/dev_hdd0/qwark/usbtmp/`, read back, and swapped in by rename, so a drive pulled mid-copy leaves the library as it was. Separately, MOD_RESCAN keeps every mod that is still there at the index it had, where it used to number the mods afresh in folder order, so a mod's bit in `mod_loaded`, `mod_auto` and `mod_previous` and its MOD_PATCH index name the same mod across a rescan. No other opcode or structure changed. See sections 3, 3.2 and 5.7.2.

Revision 1.24, Deadlocked's HUD colour (same build 56): no opcode or structure changes, only Deadlocked's DESCRIBE. It gains a sixth group, "Cosmetics" (index 5, after Fixes, so the other groups keep their indices), a ninth readout, "HUD colour" (index 8), and feature 18, "HUD colour", a COLOR in Cosmetics mirrored by readout 8. FEATURE_SET writes `0x00RRGGBB` into the game's team colour table (GuiMain_TeamColors, 0x9E87CC, eleven teams of four words `{ 0x80, B, G, R }`): all four words of the team the HUD draws in, the colour and the three variants the game works out from it (each channel times 1.5 capped at 255, halved, and halfway to 255). That team is the one the game's own HUD colour cheat picks (its byte at 0xB1F47A, less one) or, with the cheat at Default, team 1, the hero's; the readout is the colour of the same team, so it follows the cheat. The words are data, so the feature works under RPCS3 as on the console, and the game rewrites them only at boot, so a colour stays until the game restarts. The health bar takes it on its next frame and the rest of the HUD when it is next built (a load). A client that does not know the feature draws it as any COLOR, as RaC2's and UYA's chargeboot colours are.

Revision 1.24, the HUD colour's shades (same build 56): feature flags bit6 **SHADE**, a COLOR the console works out from another COLOR, named by the row's `aux`, until the user overrides it (section 5.3.3). Its readout carries bit 24, **AUTO** (`0x01000000`), while it is worked out; FEATURE_SET with bit 24 set puts it back to automatic, and any value without it overrides it. Deadlocked's DESCRIBE gains three readouts, "HUD vivid shade", "HUD dark shade" and "HUD light shade" (9, 10 and 11), and three features, 19 "HUD vivid shade", 20 "HUD dark shade" and 21 "HUD light shade", COLORs in Cosmetics flagged SHADE with `aux` 18, mirrored by readouts 9 to 11: the three variant words of the team the HUD draws in. All three are read by the game; light, the fourth word, is the top corner of the HUD's prompt and pickup boxes, what the radar's cursor pulses to and part of the bot HUD's flash. Feature 18 now changes only the shades that are automatic and leaves an overridden one as it is. qwark keeps no override state of its own: a shade is automatic while its word is 0 or exactly what the game's sums give from the colour, so the state survives a reconnect and a restart of qwark, follows the team when the game's cheat changes it, and goes with the colours when the game restarts. Nothing is re-applied at boot, as nothing is for any COLOR. A client that does not know the flag draws three more COLORs, each of which overrides its shade, and ignores bit 24 of their readouts, as it ignores everything above `0x00FFFFFF`.

Revision 1.24, the HUD colour as it is drawn (same build 56): feature flags bit7 **STOCK**, a COLOR with the game's own colour to go back to (section 5.3.4): its readout carries AUTO while it is the game's own, and FEATURE_SET with bit 24 puts it back. Deadlocked's feature 18 is flagged STOCK. The PS3 port draws every HUD colour twice as bright as its word says (each channel doubled, capped at 255; the health bar as much as the EXP bars), so the four Deadlocked readouts 8 to 11 are now the colours as the HUD shows them, each channel of the word doubled and capped, and FEATURE_SET on 18 to 21 writes the word that shows as the value sent, each channel halved and rounded up: the HUD draws what was picked, to the even value. The stock words are untouched, so the stock HUD draws as it always did and reads as it looks (team 1's red `0x6D1315` reads `0xDA262A`). While the team's colour is its stock word an automatic shade is still the game's own sum on the word; otherwise it is the same sum on the colour as shown, halved back. A set also writes the copies the HUD made when it was built (section 5.3.4), so the whole HUD changes at once, not at the next load. A client before this draws the HUD colour as any COLOR, without a way back to stock; the values it sends and shows are those of the drawn colour either way.

Revision 1.24, the weapons' colours, the HUD's brightness and colours kept for the boot (same build 56, 2026-10-26). **FEATURE_SET_AUTO on a COLOR**: the request may carry a `u32 value` after `auto` (6 bytes); `auto` 1 keeps that FEATURE_SET value, AUTO bit included, as the colour to set again by itself, and puts the row's bit in `toggle_auto` (and DESCRIBE's flags bit0), `auto` 0 forgets it. The console sets every kept colour again when the game's colours are there to be set: RaC2 and UYA as the game boots, Deadlocked at the first tick of each level and of each team the HUD takes, once a save has said which team that is (section 5.3.5). The value lives in config as the feature's `<TITLEID>.auto.<slug>` key, so it is per user with settings profiles on; a 2-byte request on a COLOR keeps black. Deadlocked's DESCRIBE gains three readouts, "Weapon colour V11", "Weapon colour V99" and "Gadget colour" (12 to 14), three STOCK COLORs in Cosmetics, 22 to 24 of the same names, mirrored by them, and a TOGGLE, 25 "Correct HUD brightness" (section 5.3.4): the weapons take their colour from three words of the team table at fixed places, which a HUD colour leaves alone unless the HUD colour cheat picks a team they are in, and the brightness correction of the HUD's colours is the user's choice rather than always on. Off, the HUD colour and its shades are written and read as given, the stock words untouched, and the HUD draws them twice as bright, as it draws the stock HUD; on, the HUD's team is held halved, the game's own colours too, and reads as picked. RaC2's and UYA's chargeboot COLORs are flagged STOCK: AUTO puts the game's own words back, and their readouts carry AUTO while the words are the game's. A client before this sends the 2-byte FEATURE_SET_AUTO only for toggles, draws the new COLORs and the toggle as any, and with the toggle off sees the HUD's colours as the words are rather than as the HUD draws them.

Revision 1.24, test builds (same build 56, 2026-10-26): every build between two releases reports the same `qwark_version`, so a client could not tell the test module on a console from the newer one it ships, and never replaced it. A module now says which build of its number it is. SessionInfo `flags2` bit4 **DEV** is set by a test build, which a plain `make` is (`QWARK_DEV`); `make release` builds the module without it. HELLO's reply is SessionInfo followed by a `u32 build_id`, the CRC-32 of the module's sources (`QWARK_BUILD_ID`, tools/buildid.py), 0 for none; GET_STATE and telemetry carry SessionInfo alone, as before. A client compares a DEV module's ID with the ID of the module it ships, which `make` writes beside qwark.sprx as `qwark.build`, and replaces one whose ID differs exactly as it replaces an older build; a module without the bit is judged by its build number alone. `qwark-rpcs3` never sets the bit and sends ID 0. A client from before this revision reads the first 164 bytes of HELLO and ignores the rest, and a module from before it sends 164 bytes, which reads as ID 0. See section 3.1.1.

Revision 1.24, Deadlocked's HUD theme as code, and its brightness in two (same build 56, 2026-10-26, later). Deadlocked's HUD colour, its shades, the weapons' colours and the brightness are qwark's own code in the game now rather than writes into the team table: GuiMain_GetPlayerColor's last word and GuiMain_GetGadgetColor's three loads branch to trampolines that hand out qwark's own colour words, seven in BSS no code of the game names (0x15CD788..0x15CD7A3), wherever one is not 0, and halve what they hand out by one of two flag bytes (0x15CD783, 0x15CD784) (section 5.3.4). So a HUD colour shares no word with any team the HUD colour cheat can pick, nor with the menus and the vehicle that name a team outright; a weapon colour shares none with the HUD; and a brightness correction changes no colour word at all. Deadlocked's DESCRIBE flags features 18 to 25 **WRITES_CODE** and gains feature 26, "Correct gradient brightness", a WRITES_CODE TOGGLE in Cosmetics: 25, "Correct HUD brightness", corrects the HUD's flat parts (the radar, the quick-select slots, the prompt and pickup boxes, the end screen, the bot HUD) and 26 its gradients (the health bar and the two EXP bars). Readouts are the colours picked, the game's own (the table's words for the HUD's team) where a word is 0, whatever the corrections. On a console qwark writes the code as the session enters the game and never takes it out, the way RaC2's crash guards go in; under RPCS3 it is part of the code switches' patch, rows with no flag byte of their own, so the HUD features need the patch and a restart there like every WRITES_CODE feature (section 5.12.2). Deadlocked's SWITCH_PATCH grows to 225 rows and 5 flag bytes, 1852 bytes, and its stamp changes, so a patch written before this reads as not present and the client offers to write it again. FEATURE_SET on a WRITES_CODE COLOR works while SessionInfo bit4 is set, as on a toggle, and a COLOR kept for the boot waits for the look for the switches as a toggle does. Kept colours are set again once, as the session enters the game: nothing in the game writes qwark's words, and they are zero in a new process. A client before this draws 26 as any toggle, and under RPCS3 without the patch sees the HUD's COLORs refused one at a time rather than greyed.

Revision 1.25 (2026-10-27, module build 57): build 56 shipped with client 1.4.2 as a release module, without the preview features of section 3.1.2, so what follows is build 57. The console menu's Mods page (section 9.1) is one of those preview features, in test builds only.

Revision 1.25, the savefile helpers in ui.pak (module build 57, 2026-10-27): no opcode or structure changes. The four savefile helpers leave the module for `/dev_hdd0/qwark/ui.pak`, beside the renderers, which saves the module about 4.6 KB of code segment (the test module back under three 64 KB pages). The pak is format 2, with the helpers as entries of kind 2 and the renderers' and the helpers' identities in its header, and a module takes only the pak built with it, as before (section 9.10). A console installs the helper out of the pak at the first action that needs it; with no usable pak that action answers `BUSY` and SAVEFILE_INFO `error` is the new **8, SAVEFILE_ERR_NO_PAK**, nothing is written into the game, and SessionInfo `flags` bit7 UI_PAK_BAD is set, as it already was for a bad pak, so a client can send the right one (section 5.12). SAVEFILE_PATCH and RPCS3 are unchanged: the emulator builds still link the helpers' bytes, and the patch is byte for byte what it was.

Revision 1.26, game modules (module build 57, 2026-10-27): a test build's games are SPRX modules of their own, each named for its game, `/dev_hdd0/qwark/rac1.sprx` (RaC1) to `rac4.sprx` (Deadlocked), loaded while their game runs once the VSH has the memory for them and unloaded when it quits (section 9.11). SessionInfo `flags2` bit5 GAME_MODULE_BAD and bit6 GAME_WAITING say when a game's module is not in, and GAME_MODULE (0x0008, section 5.1.2) says why, with the memory gate's figures; it is a control op (section 1.1). A game whose module is not in reads as `game` 0, the session of section 3.3. Deadlocked's quit and loading hooks and the PAUSE and RESUME around its quit to the XMB are the core's now, in every build, so they keep their timing whenever a module loads. A release module links its games in as before: it never sets either bit and answers GAME_MODULE with state 1. No other opcode or structure changes. The file names are the game's, not its number: nothing on the wire carries a name (GAME_MODULE's `game` byte is the game id, as SessionInfo's is), and a client maps the id to the name itself, from a table, so a later game takes a name of its own (Tools of Destruction's will be `tod.sprx`) whatever its id. The first test builds of this revision named them `game-1.sprx` to `game-4.sprx`; nothing with those names shipped, and a core takes them for no game's.

Revision 1.26, config files (module build 57, 2026-10-27): no opcode or structure changes. A write of config.txt or a profile's config.txt is the file it replaces with the module's keys put in, never the module's keys alone: every line the module does not hold (a comment, a key it does not know, a value or key longer than it keeps, a key a load left out) is written back as it was, so no build from this one on takes a setting out of the file that it did not set itself. A file that is there but will not open is `IO_ERROR`, no longer read as no file at all, and a write whose old file will not read, whose bytes do not all go to the disk or whose close fails leaves the old file in place (section 5.10.1, Files).

Revision 1.27, Tools of Destruction (module build 57, 2026-10-27): game 5, Ratchet & Clank Future: Tools of Destruction, the PSN EU executable NPEA00452 v01.00 alone. It is a game module of its own, `tod.sprx` (section 9.11), in a test build and in the PC builds (`qwark-rpcs3.exe`, the simulator); a release module has no ToD at all and treats its boot as a process it does not know (section 3.3). SessionInfo's `game` byte and GAME_MODULE's may now be 5. Three opcodes in the 0x00E0 block run a chunk of the game's own Lua on its own thread, through a helper qwark puts in the game (section 5.15): LUA_EXEC (0x00E0), LUA_STATUS (0x00E1) and, in the emulator builds alone, LUA_PATCH (0x00E2), the helper as RPCS3 patch words in SAVEFILE_PATCH's layout. A console installs the helper on the first LUA_EXEC of a game boot that asks for one in a level, never before; under RPCS3 the client writes the patch, which takes at the next boot. A module without a Lua game (a release, or any game but ToD) answers all three UNSUPPORTED. Everything else ToD offers is the ordinary ops on plain data: DESCRIBE, the unlocks, positions, planets, Die. No other opcode or structure changes.

### Revision 1.28 (2026-10-27, module build 57)

Revision 1.28, ToD's level flags: two opcodes in the 0x0050 block, in the builds that have Tools of Destruction (`QWARK_HAS_TOD`: a test build and the PC builds; a release module answers both `UNKNOWN_OP`). LEVELFLAGS_BIT (0x0056) sets or clears one bit of the region LEVELFLAGS_GET returns, the console reading and writing its byte on the tick, so a flag the game sets in the same byte meanwhile is not undone by a client's stale copy; it works for every game that answers LEVELFLAGS_GET. LEVELFLAGS_NAMES (0x0057) names the bits of that region for a game whose flags are named one by one, grouped by planet; ToD alone answers it, every other game `UNSUPPORTED`. Tools of Destruction answers LEVELFLAGS_GET, LEVELFLAGS_SET and LEVELFLAGS_RESET, which it did not before (section 5.6). `struct game_api` gained `levelflag_names` at its end. No other opcode or structure changes.

SessionInfo `flags2` bit7 **NO_MENU** (ToD small fixes, 2026-10-27): set while INGAME with a game whose code is in and which has no console menu, Tools of Destruction today (its `game_api.menu` is NULL), so a client hides the console menu's combo and the In-game menus switch for that game rather than offering two controls that do nothing there. Clear outside a game, while a game's module is not in, and in every earlier build, which is what a client already shows both for. No opcode or other structure changes. The number is the lead's to settle at merge.

Revision 1.28, ToD's autosplitter: no new opcode and no wire structure changes. Tools of Destruction answers AUTOSPLIT_DESCRIBE with six rows and emits START, RESET, SPLIT, LOAD_START and LOAD_END, and PAUSE and RESUME around a quit to the XMB (section 8.5). An EventDesc may now set NORMALISE with `param_us` 0, which takes the whole interval off game time; ToD's level load is the first row that does (section 8.2). The title index (`src/games/titles.h`) gained `resume_watch` and `resume_watch_alt`: a pause pair's RESUME can wait on the game's own memory, read and never written, where there is no boot patch, so it times the same on a console and under RPCS3.

Revision 1.28, ToD's save files: no opcode or structure changes on the wire. Tools of Destruction answers the savefile block (sections 5.12 and 5.13) with no helper of qwark's in the game: its aside buffer is the game's own save image and a load is data writes into its save system (section 5.12.4). SAVEFILE_INFO says `installed` 1 on every platform, RPCS3 included, and SAVEFILE_PATCH answers `UNSUPPORTED` for it. Its DESCRIBE gains a fourth group, "Savefile" (index 3), with feature 40, "Set aside file" (SAVE_ASIDE), and 41, "Load set-aside file" (LOAD_ASIDE). In ToD these two are a STORE into and a RESTORE of the title's set-aside file, `/dev_hdd0/qwark/savefiles/NPEA00452/set-aside.sav`, which sits beside the categories and so is in no listing; a load of the set-aside with none there is `BUSY` with error 7. Inside qwark, `struct game_api` gained `savefile_driver` at its end, after `levelflag_names`, in the builds that have ToD.

### Revision 1.29 (2026-10-28 to 2026-11-03, module build 57)

Revision 1.29, ToD's autosplitter after its first live test (module build 57): no opcode and no structure moved. **EventDesc `flags` bit4, HOLD** (0x10): on a LOAD_START row with neither timing flag and `param_us` 0, game time stops at the LOAD_START and starts again at the LOAD_END with the same code, an ASL's `isLoading` held true for as long as the pair is open; nothing is subtracted afterwards (sections 8.2, 8.3). A client that does not know the bit sees a row with no timing and does nothing with it, which is what it did with every untimed load row before. Tools of Destruction's code 4 is the first HOLD row: it is now the old ToD autosplitter's long load, exactly, and no longer the whole-load NORMALISE 0 row of revision 1.28, which no row uses any more (a NORMALISE row may still name 0). Its run start is the level flag MOVIE_METROPOLIS_TACHYON rather than the play time, and its code 2 is Tachyon's health reaching 0 rather than a completed mission; code 2's `arg` is 0 (section 8.5). Inside qwark, ToD's `game_api.on_quit` closes a long load still open before the session's PAUSE for the quit goes out.

Revision 1.29, ToD's save-file loads and why a refusal was (module build 57, 2026-10-28): SAVEFILE_INFO's first pad byte, offset 17, is now **`why`**: which of the game's own checks the `error` beside it came from (section 5.12). 0 says nothing more than `error`, and every game but Tools of Destruction always answers 0; the reply is still 20 bytes, and a client that does not know the byte reads the pad it always did. ToD's loads no longer ask the front end's exit flag, which the game never clears, so build 57 refused every RESTORE and Load set-aside after the title with error 6; they now need what the planet load needs and the title screen gone, and a boot with no slot and an autosave left waiting loads too (section 5.12.4). Every refusal of a set-aside, a load, a STORE or a RESTORE, in every game, now logs the check that failed in qwark.log. Inside qwark, `struct core_api` gained `savefile_why`. No opcode moved.

Revision 1.29, ToD's set-aside after a load (module build 57, 2026-10-28): a SAVEFILE_RESTORE of a library file that ends with error 0, the game having taken its load, now also makes that file Tools of Destruction's set-aside, as a RESTORE in the four original games fills the aside buffer it loads from. So LOAD_ASIDE (feature 41, and the Load-set-aside combo) after such a RESTORE loads the same bytes, and SAVE_ASIDE (feature 40) still replaces them with the game's save. The set-aside file is a copy, so a later STORE over the library file does not change it. A RESTORE that does not end in a load leaves the set-aside as it was; a set-aside that cannot be written is logged and changes nothing about the RESTORE's own result. No opcode, structure, error or `why` changes (section 5.12.4).

Revision 1.29, ToD's Fast loads become a code patch (module build 57, 2026-10-28): no opcode and no structure moved. Tools of Destruction's feature 17, "Fast loads", keeps its id, label, group and kind and gains `WRITES_CODE`, and its SWITCH_PATCH answers, where it was `UNSUPPORTED`: 25 rows, one trampoline of 24 words at 0x3EB578 and its site 0x25E588 = 0x4818CFF0, and one flag byte, 0x108A8560 (section 5.12.2). Until now the toggle wrote 0.0 into `g_levelTransitionMinTime` (0x10073548), which the game reads only on a load that plays a story FMV, so it changed nothing on a planet load. A planet load's screen is cinematics the game plays each to its end: the departure (`g_loadScreenType` 0), long-load screens (2 and up) while the data is not in, and the arrival (1); the load ends once the arrival has played. On a console the toggle now patches seventeen words of LoadScreen_Update (0x25E588..0x25E5C8): once the data is in, the screen goes black at once, the cinematic playing stops, and `g_loadScreenType` goes to 1 with no arrival played, so the load ends at the game's next look, within 0.2 s. Under RPCS3 the same words run from the switch's trampoline while the flag byte is 1, so there, as for every `WRITES_CODE` toggle, the toggle needs the code switches' patch and a restart (FEATURE_SET answers `UNSUPPORTED` until then). `g_loadScreenType` takes the values it took before, in the same order, 0 or a long-load screen, then 1 kept until the next load; with the toggle on a long-load screen ends when the data is in rather than at its own end, and none starts after it. Off, the game's words are back and nothing differs from the game. The TOGGLE_FAST_LOADS combo and the auto flag treat it as they treat the other games' Fast loads. In the same change ToD's Infinite ammo (8) and Infinite health (9) lose `LIVE`: FEATURE_SET_AUTO takes them, the session's arrival writes them back when flagged, and their `toggle_state` bit is qwark's own, no longer read back from the game; God Ratchet (16) stays `LIVE`, as RaC1's goodies are.

Revision 1.29, ToD's run timed as the community times it (module build 57, 2026-10-28): no opcode and no structure moved; Tools of Destruction's DESCRIBE has two more rows and a new default. **The start**: the RESET still goes out as a load into Metropolis begins on a file with MOVIE_METROPOLIS_TACHYON clear, but the START now waits for control, the first tick that Metropolis is up, `g_gameState` is 4 and no realtime cinematic plays or waits (`g_moviePlayer` idle, `g_movieQueue` empty): on a new game that is the frame after `Level_Enter`, the first of its fade in from black, about 10 s after the load began. So a ToD START no longer comes in the same moment as its RESET (a load shorter than a tick still sends both together, RESET first), and a client applies each as it comes (section 8.1). A load into Metropolis out of Metropolis itself, on a file with the flag clear, is now a new run like one out of the title; a death or a checkpoint loads nothing and is still nothing. **The end**: new code 7, "Final fade to black", on by default, is the screen black after Tachyon's last hit, the moment `MoviePlayer_update` finds ScreenFade's progress at 1.0 before the ending cinematic `l4b_s04_dimx_rt`, about 2.5 s after the hit. Code 2, "Tachyon defeated", is unchanged but off by default. New code 8, "Ending FMV", off, is `l4b_s05_end1_fmv` taking the game state, which comes only after the whole cinematic. Both new codes' `arg` is 0 (section 8.5). No row needs a client-side delay.

Revision 1.29, ToD's run start at the fade in, and deaths (module build 57, 2026-10-28): no opcode and no structure moved; Tools of Destruction's DESCRIBE has six rows. **The start** is the fade in that shows Ratchet: the first tick after the run's load that Metropolis is up and the screen fade's target ([0x10740BB4]+0x5B634) reads 0.0, which `ScreenFade_StartIn` puts there as `Level_Enter` starts its one second in from black. That is within a frame of build 57's START at control, which came one frame later (`g_gameState` 4 the frame after `Level_Enter`), and it no longer reads the game state or the movie player. **A death is no load**: the game reloads only its level tier for a death or a checkpoint, a new `lua_State` and a new fade in, which build 57 took for a load into Metropolis and so RESET a fresh file. A load, and with it the RESET, the planet split and the START's wait, is now the tier loader working on state 3 (its job at 0x1073B3A0 reading 0x10073300) with `g_levelLoadTier` (0x1037A908) not loaded, which a planet load makes for seconds, with Fast loads on or off, and a death never does; the `lua_State` only says that a level is up. A quit to the title is no longer a load either, so it never RESETs. The long load (code 4) is unchanged and was never the `lua_State`'s. **The end**: code 7 is labelled "Tachyon defeated" (it was "Final fade to black"), still on by default; codes 2 (Tachyon's health reaching 0) and 8 (the ending FMV) are retired and never reused. A client that keys saved choices by label finds a choice made for the old code 2 on code 7 (section 8.5).

Revision 1.29, ToD's Fast loads after the user's hardware test, no fade on qwark's loads, and Remove cutscenes (module build 57, 2026-10-28): no opcode and no structure moved. **Fast loads (17)** now also ends a load screen cinematic once the data is in, the departure included: the test showed the departure playing in full, because `LoadScreen_Update` runs from `Game_UpdateFrame` and a realtime cinematic plays in `g_gameState` 9, which runs `MovieQueue_update` alone, so the cut could only come between two cinematics, while the data's read starts as the departure does. The player is put in its fading state (3) as its own end and its skip put it there, the cinematic fades to black, and the cut follows; `g_loadScreenType` keeps its values and order (0, then 1 with no arrival; a long-load screen, 2 and up, can still start only if the data is not in at the departure's end, and ends as soon as it is). The toggle also covers the story FMV loads, a load into Metropolis or Cobalia while LVL_FAST_SHIP_FIXED (level flag 106) is clear: `g_levelTransitionMinTime` (0x10073548) is 0.0 while it is on and the game's 5.0 again when it goes off, so the data is read at once, and once it is, the FMV is closed as its own end closes it, the list run out and the screen put at black. Those loads touch no load screen, so `g_loadScreenType` is not written there. The pieces are qwark's own code (src/games/switches/sw_tod.s, own_*): a cave of 63 words at 0x3EB5D8, after the switch's, and three sites, 0x3C5744 = 0x48025E94, 0x3C57DC = 0x48025E5C and 0x3C58C8 = 0x48025DE8, which read the switch's flag byte 0x108A8560 themselves, and the FMV load's stamp, the word 0x108A8564. ToD's SWITCH_PATCH is now 91 rows (the two caves' 24 and 63 words, the four sites) and the same one flag byte; an RPCS3 install of the old patch no longer counts as the switches being in (CODE_SWITCHES clear, Fast loads UNSUPPORTED) until the client writes the new one and the game restarts. On a console the toggle writes the cave once, the flag byte, and the seventeen words and three site words as one patch. **No fade-out before a load qwark starts**: PLANET_LOAD, and a SAVEFILE_RESTORE or LOAD_ASIDE whose save the game takes (its `LoadLevel(level, 1, 0)` writes the same request), set `k_loadLevelFadeTime` (the TOC word 0x8BA51C, 0.5 s) to 2^-10 s just before, so `LoadLevel_ProcessPending`'s fade reaches black in one frame, and put 0.5 back once the request is taken (or the level is gone, or after six seconds, a request the game never took), and as every session enters; the game's own loads keep their fade. `k_loadLevelFadeDone` (1.0) is not touched, and the fade's progress ends at exactly 1.0, as the game's own. **Remove cutscenes**, new feature 18, a toggle in Cheats, neither `WRITES_CODE` nor `LIVE`, so it takes the auto flag: the eight FMVs `l00_s00_attr_fmv`, `l00_s00_attr_fmv_textless`, `l01_s01_ntro_fmv`, `l01_s02_walk_fmv`, `l01_s03_tach_fmv`, `l02_s01_hypr_fmv`, `l7b_s01_end2_fmv` and `l17_s03a_rmid_fmv` play as if their files were missing, which is what the runners' deleted files do: the first letter of each one's file name in the game archive's FMV table (`g_archiveGame` 0x102042AC, `toc` + 0x2C, 0x1F4 bytes a record, the file's path at +4 and the localised one at +0x94) is 0 while it is on and back when it goes off, and each session's arrival puts them back before the auto flags come back. The game then goes on as at the FMV's end (`FmvState_next`, or the load's next FMV). The others, `l4b_s05_end1_fmv` among them, and every realtime cinematic, are untouched. Everything here is data under RPCS3 but the three sites, which are in the switches' patch. The autosplitter's START, the screen fade's target going to 0.0 as `Level_Enter` starts its fade in, comes as before and once: the cut and the FMV's end leave the target at 1.0, a removed intro plays nothing that sets it, the shorter fade-out ends before the load begins, and only `Level_Enter`'s `ScreenFade_StartIn` puts it at 0.0 once the level is up.

Revision 1.29, ToD's story FMV loads' fade-out, the START at half the fade in, and Remove cutscenes on boot (module build 57, 2026-10-31): no opcode and no structure moved. **A story FMV load's fade-out.** A load qwark starts (PLANET_LOAD, and a SAVEFILE_RESTORE or LOAD_ASIDE the game takes) still sets `k_loadLevelFadeTime` (0x8BA51C) just before, but to 5/64 s (0.078125, 0x3DA00000: five frames at 60 Hz, three at 30; 7/64 s since the polish below) rather than 2^-10 s when the load is a story FMV load: `LevelTransition_begin` (0x3C8360) plays its FMV list instead of the load screen when level flag 106, LVL_FAST_SHIP_FIXED (bit 42 of `g_saveData`+0x5530), is clear and the level is Metropolis or Cobalia (LEVEL_ 0 or 1, the names it compares). Such a load has no departure, and with its FMV gone (Remove cutscenes, or a file deleted from the game's copy, which qwark cannot see) nothing comes between the old level's last frame and the black, so a one-frame fade-out flashed into the new level. PLANET_LOAD reads the flag in `g_levelFlags`; a savefile load reads the level (+0x906E8) and the flag in the save image it hands the game, whose bytes `SaveManager_onLoaded` copies in before its `LoadLevel`. A flag that cannot be read counts as clear, and a level that cannot be read as no story load. The longer fade is used for every story FMV load, whether the FMVs play or not: a few frames of fade before an FMV that does play cost nothing, and only that rule avoids a flash however the FMV went missing. Every other load keeps the one frame, and the game's 0.5 s comes back as before. **The run's START** (code 0, section 8.5) now waits, after the fade's target reads 0.0, for its progress ([0x10740BB4]+0x5B62C) to be 0.5 or less, half a second into `Level_Enter`'s one-second fade in, the user's timing (0.55, 0.45 s in, since the polish below); the guards (deaths, the title, another planet's load, Fast loads on or off) are unchanged. **Remove cutscenes (18) ships auto-flagged** (`game_describe.auto_default`, as Deadlocked's crash patches), the user's call, the toggle being run-legal: with no config.txt key `NPEA00452.auto.remove_cutscenes` its bit is set in SessionInfo `toggle_auto` and it is applied as every session enters the game; a key, 1 or 0, always wins, so FEATURE_SET_AUTO off keeps it off. It is the one ToD toggle that comes back on its own, an exception to the opt-in rule for everything else (DESIGN 3.7). Its id, label, group and config key are unchanged.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's console menu is the game's own pause menu (module build 57, 2026-10-31; it replaces the prototype of the same day, its renderer, ui.pak's kind 3 and config.txt's `menu.tod`, which are gone): **LUA_EXEC `flags` bit0** (`LUA_EXEC_MENU`) runs the chunk in the pause menu's Lua VM rather than the gameplay VM, `NOT_FOUND` with no pause up; any other bit is still `BAD_ARG` (section 5.15). **The Lua mailbox is version 2** (`src/core/luamb.h`): byte 0x26 `target` (0 the gameplay VM, 1 the pause menu's) and word 0x3C `menu_beat` (+1 every frame of the pause menu); LUA_PATCH's identity word at 0x8CA004 is `0x00022600`, so an RPCS3 patch from an earlier build no longer counts as the helper (LUA_STATUS `installed` 0, the menu not in) until the client writes the new one and the game restarts. **The Lua helper has four hooks**: besides the VM hook and the error tap, the pause menu's update (`0x242B0 = 0x488430FD`) and the pause test in `Game_UpdateFrame` (`0x4C5ABC = 0x483A1935`); LUA_PATCH lists them first, the pause test, the menu's update, the error tap, the VM hook last, and is about 1,180 words, all in one reply. **SessionInfo `flags2` bit7 NO_MENU** is clear in Tools of Destruction: its console menu is the pause menu's TRAINER row (in place of Help, MENU_HELP's switch as in every game) and the game's Cheats screen showing qwark's pages, served through the console menu's own mailbox (section 9) by the Lua helper and a Lua glue qwark sends the pause menu's VM; the menu combo opens it in play (as a press of Start the game takes) and in the pause, and closes it. **On a console** the Lua helper now goes in a few seconds into a ToD session with the menu (`menu.enabled` 1, the default), not only at the first LUA_EXEC. **Under RPCS3** ToD's menu works once LUA_PATCH's patch is in, the only console menu there: everything but the patch is data writes and requests over PINE. `qwark-rpcs3` therefore answers MENU_HELP (section 5.10) as a console does. No other opcode or structure moved; ui.pak (section 9.10) is again what it was before the prototype. Section 9.12 and `docs/tod-menu.md` have the menu.

Revision 1.29, ToD's pause menu crash (module build 57, 2026-10-31, after the first console test of the menu above): no opcode and no structure moved. Opening the pause menu crashed the game: the glue called a Node method from the pause menu's frame after `AnarkMenuSystem_update` had returned, where the Anark runtime has popped the pause's presentation and every Node lookup reads through a NULL pointer (`docs/tod-menu.md` section 9). **The Lua helper's code changed**, and with it **LUA_PATCH**: the cave's bytes are new (about 1,300 words in all; the hook words and the identity word `0x00022600` are unchanged), so under RPCS3 a patch the client wrote before this no longer counts as the helper (LUA_STATUS `installed` 0, the menu not in) until the client writes the new one and the game restarts, as for any change of the helper. The helper now enters the pause menu's VM (the glue, its frame, a request of target 1, LUA_EXEC flags bit0) only on a frame where the game's own words say the pause's presentation is up, and then only with it pushed, as the game's own `AnarkMenuSystem_reset` calls into it; on every other frame a request of target 1 waits (and times out as before). Three words of the helper's block (section 9.12) are new: 0x8CE024 `why`, 0x8CE028 `waits`, 0x8CE02C `note`; `menu.c` logs a long wait and the glue's note in qwark.log. qw()'s operation 6 sets `note`.

Revision 1.29, ToD's second pause menu crash (module build 57, 2026-11-01, after the console test of the fix above): no opcode and no structure moved. With the fix in, opening the pause crashed in the game's Lua allocator: the pause menu's Lua allocates from a pool of fixed-size blocks that the game sets up afresh at every pause (`g_anarkLuaAllocator`, 18 classes of 4 to 640 bytes, about 0.7 MB), whose allocation, once every class from the asked size's up is used up, reads one chunk past its table and faults instead of using its heap; the glue's load and its first look at the pause in the same frame, a closure made by every field it read, used the pool up (`docs/tod-menu.md` section 10). **The Lua helper's code changed** again, and with it **LUA_PATCH** (about 1,720 words; hook and identity words unchanged): under RPCS3 the client writes the new patch and the game restarts, as for any change of the helper. The helper now counts the pool before it lets the pause menu's Lua run, makes the game's own full collection when it is low, and keeps out while it stays low, taking a glue that is in back out (`qwark_quit`, which puts back everything it wrapped and the Help row). **One new status**, in the Lua mailbox and LUA_EXEC's `lua_status` (section 5.15): **`0x103 NO_ROOM`**, a request for the pause menu's VM (target 1, LUA_EXEC flags bit0, the glue) not run because the pool is short of what a load needs even after a collection. Three words of the helper's block (section 9.12) are new: 0x8CE030 `room`, 0x8CE034 `short`, 0x8CE038 `hold`; `menu.c` logs a shortage once a pause, and the pool's room beside the glue's "in". A pause menu frame that serves a request no longer also runs the glue's frame, so the glue's load and its first look at the pause are never one frame's garbage.

Revision 1.29, ToD's pause menu finds its Trainer row (module build 57, 2026-11-01, after the console test of the fix above): no opcode and no structure moved, and **the Lua helper's code and LUA_PATCH are unchanged**. The test showed no crash, but the glue never found the two behaviours it looked for together, the pause's list and the Cheats screen, so Options kept Help and the combo opened the plain pause (`docs/tod-menu.md` section 11). The glue (sent as before, a request of target 1) now looks for them apart: the TRAINER row needs only the list, found by the `menuData` row whose callback is its own `onHelp` in whichever menu holds it; the Cheats screen is looked for again every frame once the Trainer goes to its slide (90 frames at most); the combo needs neither, only the scene. **The glue's note** (the word at 0x8CE02C, section 9.12) has three more values: 3 the Trainer waited on the Cheats screen's slide in vain and went back, 4 the list in with the Cheats screen not met yet, 5 the Cheats screen met on its slide. After a note 2 or 3 qwark sends the pause menu's VM **one more request of target 1, the helper's look** (`lh_diag.lua`, once a session for each), whose first result, a line of text about what the glue could see, it logs in qwark.log; its answer is never a client's. The glue is sent with the spaces around its operators taken out (`build.sh`'s `lua_min`), which keeps it in one request.

Revision 1.29, ToD's Trainer on the pause's own list and Game Options (module build 57, 2026-11-01, after the fourth console test, build e44961c): no opcode moved. The Trainer row was in, but its links did nothing, its pages sat on the Cheats screen under its CHEATS title and skill-point counter, and the game's own Cheats screen broke. **The mailbox's header** for ToD (section 9.12) now says 9 rows at once on a MAIN page and 6 on a CONFIG page (`max_rows`, `cfg_rows`; the row cap stays 32), so qwark posts a longer page a window at a time, and a module that finds the old header (32 and 32) writes a new one. **The pages are shown otherwise**: a MAIN page (the root, Unlocks) is a menu of the pause's own list, a CONFIG page the game's GAME OPTIONS screen; **the helper's copy of a page** (0x8CE040) has its title and its rows' labels and values in capitals. **qw() has three more operations** (`lh_menu.h`, the glue's alone): 7 a press posted only where the row takes it, 8 the cursor at a window's edge (SCROLL_UP/DOWN), 9 the next row the cursor may stand on. The glue's notes keep their numbers, 3, 4 and 5 now being about Game Options. The Lua helper's code grew (LUA_PATCH about 1,945 words; a reply holds 2,046). Why the links were dead, the new screens and the take-over's scope are `docs/tod-menu.md` section 12.

Revision 1.29, ToD's Trainer sized to its rows, loads from the pause, and a spacer (module build 57, 2026-11-01, after the fifth console test, build 87a143a): no opcode and no structure moved, and **the Lua helper's code and LUA_PATCH are unchanged** (the glue is). **PLANET_LOAD in Tools of Destruction's pause** answers OK where it answered BUSY, when the pause is up with the glue in it (`g_pauseMenuState` 3, `g_gameState` 5): qwark asks the glue to close the pause (as the game's own Quit does, `exit_pause_menu`) and loads the planet on the first tick the game is back in play; the load is cancelled, with a console notice, if the game leaves its pause another way first or the Trainer comes up again in it, and dropped five seconds on as the menu's loads are. A SAVEFILE_RESTORE or LOAD_ASIDE from a client in the pause is still refused BUSY (`why` NOT_GAMEPLAY), since the client follows a RESTORE's transfer to its end; the Trainer's own save files and Load set-aside now close the pause and load. The menu's loads (a planet row, a save file, Load set-aside) wait for `g_gameState` 4 as well as the pause's state 0: the pause says 0 a frame or more before the game asks for play, and a load run then was refused BUSY, which is what the user saw. **The menu**: a MAIN page goes to the pause list's slide for its row count (the game's lists are framed by their component's slides, nine, eight, seven, six, five and three rows), and Game Options' rows past a CONFIG page's end are hidden; ToD's Manips section has a blank row between Remove cutscenes and the cutscene cache's rows (the menu table's MENU_ITEM_SPACER, a row again in the builds with ToD, never a section's first or last). `struct menu_native` has two more words, `play_state` and `glue_loaded`. docs/tod-menu.md section 13.

Revision 1.29, a game's debug pages, Tools of Destruction's level cache (module build 57, 2026-10-31): one opcode, **DEBUG_TEXT (0x00E8)**, in the builds that have Tools of Destruction (a release module answers it `UNKNOWN_OP`). A game may offer read-only pages of text it makes from its own memory, which a client shows as they come: the page list (request 0xFF) gives each page's title and a SLOW flag, and a page (request n) its text (section 5.16). ToD alone answers it, with two pages about the game's level cache (`rewrite/docs/REPORT-tod-level-cache.md`): **Now**, the level warmer, what an opening cutscene waits for and the save thread, a dozen small reads, and **Cache**, the FIOS block cache summed per file, newest first, a 70 KB read, flagged SLOW. Every other game, and a session outside a game, answers `UNSUPPORTED`. Nothing is written to the game. Inside qwark, `struct game_api` gained `debug_text` at its end, after `savefile_driver`, in the builds that have ToD. No other opcode or structure changes.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's cutscene cache (module build 57, 2026-11-01): no opcode and no structure moved; ToD's DESCRIBE has three more rows and a fourth readout. A planet's opening cutscene streams its audio from the planet's `streaming_sound.dat` through the game's FIOS disk cache, which those reads never fill; only the game's level warmer fills it, with the current planet's sound once its gameplay has begun, so an opening cutscene starts cold the first time in a boot and warm after (`rewrite/docs/REPORT-tod-level-cache.md` section 10). **Cache cutscenes**, new feature 19, an ACTION in Cheats, hands the warmer Fastoon's and then Stratus City's `streaming_sound.dat` while it is idle in gameplay, and names **readout 3, "Cutscene cache"**, as its status readout, the second ACTION after UYA's Reset trophies to name one (section 5.17 has the word). **Cache cutscenes on reset**, new feature 20, a toggle, asks for the same as a new run's load into Metropolis begins (the autosplitter's RESET: level flag 185 clear). **Disable cutscene caching**, new feature 21, a toggle, frees that planet's sound blocks in the cache during every level load, so its opening cutscene always starts cold; while it is on, 19 answers `BUSY` and 20 asks for nothing. All three are data writes, neither `WRITES_CODE` nor `LIVE` (20 and 21 take the auto flag, keys `NPEA00452.auto.cache_cutscenes_on_reset` and `NPEA00452.auto.disable_cutscene_caching`), and work the same under RPCS3. Ids 22 and 23 are held for this feature. The console menu's Manips page lists the three after Remove cutscenes, and a page of a game's own (`game_menu.sections`) now shows an ACTION's status row after it as the MANIP_LIST page does (section 9); ToD's is the only one. A client before this revision shows the three as any other ACTION and toggles and ignores readout 3. **`tod.sprx` is two pages** (section 9.11): it was 440 bytes under one page's limit before these three, and is about 67 KB with them, so on a console a ToD session's module now takes 128 KB rather than 64 KB, and the memory gate asks for 64 KB more free memory before it loads it (the core reads the pages from the file, as it always has). The four originals' modules, and `make release`, which has no ToD, are unchanged.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's cutscene cache in a run (module build 57, 2026-11-01, after the user's console test): no opcode and no structure moved; feature 19's status word changes in one field and gains two reasons (section 5.17). The user's console run showed a run's request never starting: it waited for the warmer to go idle, which on Metropolis means its own 380 MiB (its sound, dialogue and textures, then Cobalia's `level_cached`), and a runner leaves Metropolis in about 26 s. **A request now goes ahead of the warmer's own work**, and Stratus City's sound goes first, Fastoon's second. While the warmer reads one of its own slots k (`state` k + 1, `curFile` its `file[k]`) with more than 2 MiB of that file left, qwark puts the planet's `level_uncached.psarc` descriptor into `file[k]` (+0x10 + 4k) and its sound's end into `fileSize[k]` (+4k), writes `state` back to k, and cuts the slot's own read 1 MiB past where it is (`fileEnd` +0x34); at the cut the warmer's step takes ours and reads it from 0. While ours is read, the slot gets the next planet, then the game's own file back, with `state` k again each time, so after ours the warmer reads its own file again and qwark puts its `offset` (+0x30) on to where it was cut; from there the warmer goes on with its own slots as the game set them up. A range that starts more than 1 MiB in (what is left of one, or the `.jp` twin) has its `offset` put on to its start the same way. With the warmer idle the single-range form is used as before. None of these writes can race the warmer: `state` and the slots are read only at the end of a file, and a new `fileEnd` or `offset` keeps every chunk inside the file (a cut the offset has already reached is undone at once). **A level load no longer ends a request**: what was read stays read, what is left (from the 1 MiB block the warmer was in) goes ahead of the next level's own work, and only the title (once the request has seen gameplay), the game going, Disable cutscene caching, a stall or an unreadable game end it. A load into Stratus City or Fastoon while that planet's sound is still to read ends it, failed, with the new reasons 7 and 8 (too late for it). **What the cache holds is skipped**: at the request and at each level load qwark walks the FIOS block table (32 descriptors a tick, as Disable cutscene caching does) and reads only from each planet's first block not in it to its last; a planet wholly in is not read at all, and a request that finds both in is done at once with a rate of 0. **The status word**: DONE's bits 16..23 are now the read rate in tenths of a MiB/s (the bytes qwark's ranges moved over the ticks they were in the warmer's hands with it on, at most 25.5), 0 when nothing needed reading, where they were 100; FAILED's detail 1 is now the title alone. Cache cutscenes on reset is unchanged in when it asks. All still data writes, the same under RPCS3, and no code patch. A client before this revision shows a done run as done and reasons 7 and 8 as a failure it does not name.
