Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's finishing touches (module build 57, 2026-11-01): two opcodes retired, ToD's cutscene cache gains Cobalia, its module is one page again, and its Trainer takes the pause list's Inventory row. **DEBUG_TEXT (0x00E8) is retired** and never reused: every build answers it `UNKNOWN_OP` (section 5.16), `struct game_api` loses `debug_text`, and the four games their NULL for it. **LEVELFLAGS_NAMES (0x0057) is retired** and never reused: every build answers it `UNKNOWN_OP`, `struct game_api` loses `levelflag_names` (the four games their NULL, the structs `levelflag_group`, `levelflag_name` and `levelflag_names` go), and Tools of Destruction's 292 flag names and their 20 groups are the client's (RaCMAN Reloaded's `data/levelflags/tod.json`); qwark keeps only each flag's group, by number, for LEVELFLAGS_RESET, which clears the same flags of a planet as before (section 5.6). A client before this revision, which falls back to the byte grid on `UNSUPPORTED` or `UNKNOWN_OP`, shows ToD's flags as bytes. Both made room: `tod.sprx` is 56,224 bytes in memory, one 64 KB page again (it was two, 76,640 bytes; section 9.11). **The cutscene cache gains Cobalia** (section 5.17): Cobalia's opening cinematic, `l02_s03_wake_rt`, queued by its `main.lua` with `queue_cinematic_start_slow_white` while MOVIE_COBALIA_INTRO is clear, is an in-engine cinematic like Stratus City's, which waits on the same sound pre-roll (its slow white is a cut to white, no wait of its own), and its audio streams from Cobalia's `streaming_sound.dat` (bytes 10,928 to 69,919,408 of its `level_uncached.psarc`, cache blocks 0..66, 66.7 MiB); its FMVs `l02_s01_hypr_fmv` and `l02_s02_zoni_fmv` play during the story load before it and are not this. So a request now caches three planets in the order a run needs them, Cobalia, Stratus City, then Fastoon, 182 MiB in all; caching's detail 3 is Cobalia, and failed's new reason 9 is a load into Cobalia before its sound was in. **A late planet no longer ends the request**: a load into a planet still to read drops that planet alone, the rest goes on, and once it is in the request ends failed with the first late planet's reason (Cobalia's, then Stratus City's, then Fastoon's), where it used to end at the load; a load that leaves nothing to read ends it there. A client before this revision shows reason 9 as a failure it does not name. **The Trainer row takes the pause list's Inventory row** (section 9.12), not Options' Help: the glue finds the list by its rows whose callback is the list's own `onInventory` (the main list's fourth, the arena and space combat lists' fifth) and makes each TRAINER while `menu.help` is 1, the game's own Inventory row while it is 0; Options' Help is never touched again. Triangle at the Trainer's root goes back to the list it came from, in that list's own frame (the main list's slide 3), the cursor on TRAINER. The Lua helper's code and LUA_PATCH are unchanged; the glue is 7,933 bytes. **The autosplitter's START waits for the run's own fade in** (section 8.5): the console's START still came about half a second early after the halfway test above, because the run's Metropolis counts as up (its data in, the `lua_State` made by `levelInit`) while `Level_StartGameplay` still runs, before `Level_Enter`'s `ScreenFade_Set(1.0)` and `ScreenFade_StartIn(1.0, 0)`, and the fade can read clear then (target 0.0, progress 0), which passed the test at once. The START now also needs the fade in seen under way first (its dark half, progress above 0.5 with target 0.0, or the progress going down), so it goes out half a second into `Level_Enter`'s fade in, 0.5 s of the game's clock after `ScreenFade_StartIn`. No row, code or flag changed; the RESET, the death guard and the real-load test are as they were.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's polish (module build 57, 2026-11-02): no opcode moved; one bit of the console menu's mailbox, which is not on the wire. **PLANET_LIST names ToD's planets as the ship does** (section 5.5): the ship's planet selector and galactic map list each level by the game's own name for its planet or place, `get_location_name(level)` (`GalMap_getLocationName` 0x36E20, the text ids at 0x1006E624, US English), so the 19 names, still in LEVEL_ order at the same ids, are Kerwan, Cobalia, Kortog, Fastoon, Voron Asteroid Belt, Mukow, Nundac Asteroid Ring, Ardolis, Rakar Star Cluster, Rykan V, Sargasso, Kreeli Comet, Viceron, Verdigris Black Hole, Jasindu, Ublik Passage, Reepor, Igliak and Fastoon II (it was the LEVEL_ enum's level names, Metropolis, Stratus City and on). The game's texts say "Planet Kortog"; the word is left off. The map shows Fastoon and Fastoon II as one row, whichever is visible, under the same text; both can be loaded here, so the second is "Fastoon II". Nothing else changed: the ids, the menu order bytes (0 to 18), PLANET_LOAD and the autosplitter's planet ids are as they were, and RaCMAN Reloaded's ToD route file already named each planet both ways. **The console menu scrolls a page at a time** (section 9.12): `menubox.h` gains **MBOX_CAP_PAGED (0x20)**, which ToD's header now carries (caps 0x2D); with it a long page's windows are whole screens (starts 0, m, 2m ..., the last one short), SCROLL_DOWN shows the next window with the cursor on its first row the cursor may stand on, SCROLL_UP the one before with the cursor on its last, past either end the page wraps, and the renderer asks as the cursor would leave the window rather than as it comes to its edge. The four original games' renderers do not set it and slide as before. **TRAINER from the first frame**: the glue redraws the pause list (its own `updateAllItems`) as it puts TRAINER in Inventory's row or Inventory back, so the row no longer reads INVENTORY until the cursor moves. **The Trainer's Manips page** shows the cutscene cache's done status as "DONE (104S)", the seconds alone; feature 19's status word, readout 3 and the client's line keep the read rate (section 5.17). **A story FMV load's fade-out is 7/64 s** (0.109375, 0x3DE00000, exact: about six and a half frames at 60 Hz), a frame or two longer than the 5/64 s above, the user's call. **The run's START** (code 0, section 8.5) goes out 0.45 s into `Level_Enter`'s fade in, the progress at 0.55f (0x3F0CCCCD, 0.550000012, the float nearest 0.55) or less, where it was 0.5; seen under way first is now the progress above 0.55, and every other guard is unchanged. `tod.sprx` stays one page.

Revision 1.29, ToD's Fast loads crash at Stratus City's opening cinematic (module build 57, 2026-11-02, after the user's console test of build d3862ae): no opcode and no structure moved; feature 17 keeps its id, label, group, kind and flags. With Fast loads on, a load into Stratus City crashed as its opening cinematic (`l03_s01_rcff_rt`) began, a NULL read at 0x82624 in `Cinematic_updateTransitionProp` (0x82518), which `MoviePlayer_update` runs on every frame of a playing cinematic. That function keeps a prop moby on a joint of another: `Cinematic_setupMoby` (0x81F60) spawns the prop (class 0xBC at half size for "stratus city", 0xBB for "fastoon") beside a cinematic's class-0xBA moby only while a level transition is up and level flag 106 (LVL_FAST_SHIP_FIXED) is clear, and keeps both moby handles in `g_transitionPropHandle` (0x101D60E8) and `g_transitionPropOwnerHandle` (0x101D60E0), u64 words that nothing clears but the game's static constructor. The game counts on a handle going stale with its moby, but each level's `MobyPool_init` (0x5578D8) starts the handles' serial at 1 again, so the handles of a cinematic early in one level can name mobys of the next. A load the game plays to its end leaves the arrival's handles, which name nothing in the planet; with the arrival cut, the departure's or a long-load screen's were left, the planet's opening cinematic found them naming two of its own mobys (side by side in the user's dump), and the joint record of the owner's class, which the function reads without a test, was not there. **The cut now makes both handles invalid**, 0x03FFFFFF_FFFFFFFF as the constructor's `MobyHandle64_invalidate` makes them, after the cinematic's stop and the player's reset; no planet's cinematic wants the prop, which is spawned only while a transition is up, and loads that play the arrival, story FMV loads and the game's own words with the toggle off are unchanged. **On a console** the patch is now twenty-five words of `LoadScreen_Update` (0x25E588..0x25E5E8), the eight new ones over the arrival's own start, which the patched form never reached: `lis r9, 0x101D`, `lis r10, 0x0400`, `addi r10, r10, -1`, `li r11, -1`, then `stw` of r10 and r11 at 0x60E0, 0x60E4, 0x60E8 and 0x60EC of r9; the branches to the return now land on the last word, 0x25E5E8. **Under RPCS3** the switch's trampoline is 32 words (it was 24) and runs the same words, and qwark's own cave moved 32 bytes on, to 0x3EB5F8, so its three site words are now 0x3C5744 = 0x48025EB4, 0x3C57DC = 0x48025E7C and 0x3C58C8 = 0x48025E08: ToD's SWITCH_PATCH is 99 rows (32 and 63 cave words, the four sites) and the same flag byte 0x108A8560, and an RPCS3 install of the earlier patch no longer counts as the switches being in (CODE_SWITCHES clear, Fast loads UNSUPPORTED) until the client writes the new one and the game restarts. Fastoon's opening cinematic (`l04_s01a_fint_rt`) had the same exposure; the other planets' arrivals never spawn the prop. Remove cutscenes and the cutscene cache touch none of this.

Revision 1.29, ToD's Credits split retired (module build 57, 2026-11-02): no opcode and no structure moved; Tools of Destruction's AUTOSPLIT_DESCRIBE has five rows. Code 5, "Credits" (SPLIT, off by default: `g_gameState` becoming 11, `arg` the level the credits came from), is gone with its signal, and is retired like codes 2 and 8: never reused. The run's end is code 7, "Tachyon defeated", which comes before the credits. A client keys saved choices by label, so a choice saved for "Credits" matches no row and is ignored; a client that still lists the row from an older module sees it there and nowhere else (section 8.5).

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's Trainer opens the galactic map (module build 57, 2026-11-02): no opcode, no structure and no mailbox bit moved, and **the Lua helper, its glue and LUA_PATCH are unchanged**. The root's Planets row in ToD's Trainer now opens the game's own planet picker, the ship's galactic map, the way the four original games' Planets rows open theirs (section 9.12, docs/tod-menu.md 16). The row is the same link it was (no PICKER cap or flag: the pause cannot hold the map, so the picker is the core's, not the renderer's): its cross is a CLOSES action, the pause closing as for a load, and once the game plays qwark sends `gmap()` to the gameplay VM as a request of its own (target 0, never a LUA_EXEC's), the gameplay package's call that opens the map, with every level the map lists unlocked for as long as it is up (data writes, put back as it goes). A pick is the game's own load (the map's exit, `LoadLevel`, with its fade and transition), which Fast loads and the autosplitter take as any load; Back opens the Trainer again on its root, the cursor on Planets, which then reads **All planets** and opens the Planets page once (Metropolis, and the Fastoon the map is not showing, are only there). Where the map cannot open (no Lua helper, no level) the row opens the Planets page as before, and a map that never comes up is given up after four seconds, the Trainer opening on the Planets page; both say so on the console. A client sees nothing new: PLANET_LOAD and PLANET_LIST are as they were, and `g_gameState` 6 while the map is up is not play, so a PLANET_LOAD then is BUSY as in any other menu of the game's.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's galactic map lists every planet (module build 57, 2026-11-03): no opcode and no structure moved; one value of the Lua mailbox's `target` and one hook of the Lua helper more, neither on the wire. The map the Trainer's Planets row opens (the paragraph above) now offers every planet, Kerwan (Metropolis) and Fastoon II beside Fastoon among them, as the four original games' pickers were made to, so the **All planets** row Back used to leave is gone: there is one picker. Once the map is up qwark sends it a glue of its own (`src/games/luahelper/lh_gmap.lua`), a request of **target 2, MAP** (`luamb.h`), the map's own Anark VM, which the helper serves from the map's frame (a fifth hook, GalMapState_update's call of the menu update at `0x3633C`, to the pause menu's entry) with the same gate, pool guard and push as the pause's; it is the core's own request, never a LUA_EXEC's (LUA_EXEC's `flags` are as they were), and the ship's own map never gets one. The glue lists Kerwan under its galaxy (Solana) and Fastoon II as a row of its own, every row a pick, and a pick is the level picked (a new `qw` operation of the helper's, 10, writes it where the game's own pick goes), so the game's own load follows as before. Should the glue not go in, the map is the game's own (the save's planets unlocked as before), the console says so, and Back opens the Planets page. LUA_PATCH is 2,045 words (of the 2,046 a reply holds), the map's hook among them. docs/tod-menu.md section 17.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's planet loads fast-loaded, map picks as qwark's loads (module build 57, 2026-11-03): no opcode, no structure and no mailbox bit moved, and **the Lua helper's code and LUA_PATCH are unchanged**. **Fast loads forced for qwark's planet loads**: a PLANET_LOAD in Tools of Destruction (from a client, or the Trainer's Planets page) now forces feature 17 on for that load, as the four original games' PLANET_LOAD always has, and puts the toggle's setting back once the new level is up (the level load seen, then a level's lua_State), or six seconds on if the game never began the load; with the toggle on nothing changes. On a console that is the toggle's patch, written and reverted; under RPCS3 with the switches in, the flag byte 0x108A8560 at 1 and back at 0; both ways `g_levelTransitionMinTime` 0 for the load. The toggle's `toggle_state` bit never moves for a load, and under RPCS3 the toggle's read-back from the game (the flag byte) answers the toggle, not the force; a flag found at 1 that this process did not set is the user's and stays. A SAVEFILE_RESTORE or LOAD_ASIDE is unchanged (its short fade only). **A pick on the galactic map the Trainer opened** is now such a load too: as the map comes up, qwark forces Fast loads and arms `k_loadLevelFadeTime` (0x8BA51C) to 7/64 s while level flag 106 is clear (a pick may be a story FMV load) or 2^-10 s, holds both while the map is up, sets the picked level's own fade as soon as it sees the pick (`g_galMap.selectedLevel`, read after `g_gameState`; the game's fade reads the word every frame), and puts both back at once when the map closes without a pick; after a pick both go back as for a PLANET_LOAD. The ship's own map and a space combat level's end map are untouched. Inside qwark, `struct menu_picker` gained `up`, called once as the picker comes up. **The map's names** are trimmed of trailing spaces, so Fastoon II reads "Planet Fastoon II" (`lh_gmap.lua`, 3,132 bytes). The autosplitter reads nothing new and is unchanged. docs/tod-menu.md 18 has the detail.

Revision 1.29, UYA's Fast loads under RPCS3 (module build 57, 2026-11-03, after the user's report of a black screen that never loads): no opcode and no structure moved; feature 38 keeps its id, label, group, kind and flags, and a console's patch words (rac3.c) are unchanged. With fast loads on, a planet load's transition loop (0x27B360) skips its frame, and so its flip, the one place a pass waits, while it stands still for the loader; the level itself is read meanwhile by a thread of its own, LoadAssetAsync (0x99AAA8, priority 1000, started by the loader's stage 0), which sleeps 50 us after every 128 KB it reads. Under RPCS3 the spinning main thread starved that thread: in the user's RPCS3 log of 2026-11-03 the read took 10 to 17 s on every fast load where the same trips read in 3 to 5 s with fast loads off, the game's sleeps woke at about a fifth of their usual rate, and RPCS3's own 10-second monitors ran 3 to 7 s late, all of it behind the black screen. **The code switch's standing-still pass now sleeps a frame's time**, the game's own inline `sys_timer_usleep` (li r11, 141; li r3, 16667; sc, as the reader does at 0x99A6C0), before it goes on at 0x27B538, where the game's own pass would have waited for the vblank; the first pass, a pass with a story scene owed and the last pass do not sleep, and nothing else changed. The trampoline at 0x980F80 is 42 words (it was 38) and the draw site's moved from 0x981020 to 0x981030, so its site word is now 0x27B550 = 0x48705AE0 (it was 0x48705AD0); the pass site 0x27B49C = 0x48705AE4, the ammo switch, the fingerprint site 0x182A88 = 0x487FE478 and both flag bytes are unchanged. UYA's SWITCH_PATCH is now 62 rows (it was 58) with a new stamp, so an RPCS3 install of the earlier patch (stamp 0x0FCF688C) reads as switches from another build: the game is still UYA, SessionInfo bit2 set and bit4 clear, Fast loads and Infinite ammo refused and planet loads at the game's own pace, until the client writes the new patch and the game restarts (section 5.12.2). A console runs the console's cave, which keeps its spin: lv2 there hands the CPU to the reader and to FIOS (priorities 1000 and 99) as they wake, ahead of the main thread's 1001.

Revision 1.29, Tools of Destruction's Lua helper in ui.pak (module build 57, 2026-11-03): no opcode, no frame status and no structure moved, and **LUA_PATCH is byte for byte what it was** (2,045 words). The Lua helper's code (8,152 bytes) and the three Lua chunks the core sends (the pause menu's glue, 7,949 bytes, the look at the pause, 2,690, and the galactic map's glue, 3,132) leave `tod.sprx`, whose code segment goes from 60,608 to 38,656 of its 61,440 bytes, for `ui.pak`, as the renderers did in build 55 and the savefile helpers in build 56 (section 9.10): four entries of kinds 3 to 6 after the savefile helpers, which only a build with Tools of Destruction packs and reads (a test build and the PC builds; `make release`'s pak is byte for byte what it was, 73,992 bytes; a test build's is 96,163). Their identity, `LH_PAK_ID`, is the CRC-32 of the four entries' CRC-32s, read from the table of contents; a module with ToD takes a pak only when it holds, so a pak without them, or with another build's, is another build's pak (SessionInfo `flags` bit7 UI_PAK_BAD, which a client already answers by sending the pak it shipped). On a console the helper goes in out of the pak, checked whole first, and each chunk is read out of it as it is sent; under RPCS3 nothing changes (the emulator builds keep the bytes, for LUA_PATCH, the presence look and the chunks). Without a usable pak a console's LUA_EXEC answers `UNSUPPORTED` with nothing written and LUA_STATUS `installed` 0, the Trainer is not put in (Inventory stays the game's, the console menu's no-pak state of section 9.10, tried again when a client has sent the pak), a glue that cannot be read is not sent, and qwark.log says why (`lua: no usable Lua helper for game 5 in ui.pak; nothing written`, `lua: no usable Lua chunk N for game 5 in ui.pak; not sent`).

### Revision 1.30 (2026-11-04, module build 57)

Revision 1.30, ToD's Load set-aside fast-loaded (module build 57, 2026-11-04): no opcode and no structure moved. Tools of Destruction's LOAD_ASIDE action (its Load set-aside button, the console menu's row and the combo) now forces Fast loads on for that load and puts the toggle's setting back once the new level is up, as RaC1's, RaC2's and UYA's set-aside loads have since build 53, through the same force and watch as its PLANET_LOAD (revision 1.29): the force goes on once the RESTORE of `set-aside.sav` has been accepted, so a refused one writes nothing, and it never stops the load (under RPCS3 without the code switches the load goes at the game's pace). A SAVEFILE_RESTORE of a library file forces nothing, as in every game; Deadlocked's set-aside load forces nothing either, as before. Both keep the short fade-out of revision 1.29.

Revision 1.30, build 57's release (module build 57, 2026-11-04): no opcode and no structure moved. Build 57's release module (`make release`) has everything its test builds had: the preview features of section 3.1.2 (the settings profiles, the USB mods, the console menu's Mods page), its games as modules behind the memory gate (section 9.11), Tools of Destruction (`tod.sprx`, the Lua ops, LEVELFLAGS_BIT and LEVELFLAGS_NAMES) and the ui.pak with ToD's Lua helper (section 9.10). A release now differs from a test build by `flags2` bit4 DEV alone (section 3.1.1): it never sets it, and is judged by its build number. Build 56's release module had none of these; a client tells by the bits and the ops' answers, as before.

Revision 1.30, run-start warnings (module build 57, 2026-11-04): with the in-game trainer it is easy to leave a cheat on, so as a run starts the console now says, in one VSH notification, what is on that the run should not have (section 5.10.2): **"Warning: N cheats enabled"**, **"Warning: N mods enabled"** for mods whose author marks them not run-legal, and **"Warning: N unknown mods enabled"**, once, for mods that say neither. **RUN_WARN** at 0x0096, `u8 on`, writes config.txt's new key `run.warnings` (1 when the file does not name it), a per-user key like `menu.help` (section 5.10.1); anything but 0 or 1 is `BAD_ARG`, and it answers in any session state and on every platform. **SessionInfo byte 31**, a pad byte until now, is `flags3`: bit0 RUN_WARN, the module has the warnings and answers RUN_WARN (the client's capability bit), bit1 RUN_WARN_OFF, `run.warnings` is 0. **MOD_LIST's flags** gain bit6 LEGAL and bit7 ILLEGAL, a `patch.txt`'s new `#- legal: true` or `#- legal: false` line (section 5.7); a mod with neither is of unknown legality, which is every mod written before this revision. A run start is the autosplitter's START (section 8), which every game's watcher emits once per run and never for a death or another load. Which TOGGLEs count as cheats is each game's own table (`struct game_describe`'s new `cheats` at its end, section 5.10.2 lists them); nothing on the wire says it. Not a preview feature: a release module has it too. No other opcode or structure moved.

Revision 1.30, run-start warnings after build 57's hardware test (2026-11-04): no opcode and no structure moved. UYA's Quick-select pause (feature 5) no longer counts as a cheat: it is one of the game's own options, the client's Values switch, so UYA counts four toggles (section 5.10.2); Fast loads still counts in every game, and RaC2's two on-death toggles still count. The third line of the notification reads **"Warning: N unknown mods enabled"** ("1 unknown mod"), for the same mods as before.

Revision 1.31 (2026-11-07, module build 58): build 57 shipped with client 1.5.0 as a release, so what follows is build 58. No opcode or structure changes: it carries the mod pool fix of section 5.7 (a mod switched off is reverted whole, and the Mods page no longer finds an overlap with a mod it shares no byte with). A client that expects build 58 reads a console still on build 57 as out of date and replaces it.

Revision 1.31, Tools of Destruction's Remove black screens (module build 58, 2026-11-12): no opcode and no structure moved, and the revision number stays; ToD's DESCRIBE has one more row. **Remove black screens**, new feature 24 (22 and 23 stay held for the cutscene cache), a TOGGLE in Cheats after God Ratchet, is a practice cheat: before a realtime cinematic the game's movie player cuts the screen to black and holds it there, in gameplay, until the arrival autosave is done and the cinematic's sounds have pre-rolled, and runners use that hold at a planet's start for setups they cannot see. While the toggle is on, a hold that begins within 15 seconds of a level's (or a death's) lua_State coming up is a lead-in, and qwark keeps the screen fade's drawn alpha at 0 for as long as it lasts, while the fade is still; the fade's progress and target, which the game's own waits and the autosplitter's START and "Tachyon defeated" read, are never written, so the cinematic still starts when it would. A cinematic later in a level keeps its black, as do loads, deaths and Level_Enter's fade in. Off in a hold puts the black back. Data writes alone, neither `WRITES_CODE` nor `LIVE`, off at every boot unless flagged on boot (`NPEA00452.auto.remove_black_screens`), and the same under RPCS3. It counts as a cheat at a run start (section 5.10.2), and the console menu's Cheats page lists it after God Ratchet, seven rows, so two Game Options windows, as the Game page has. A client before this revision shows it as any toggle.

Revision 1.31, Tools of Destruction's positions keep the camera, and its Die respawns at once (module build 58, 2026-11-13): no opcode and no structure moved, and the revision number stays. **A ToD position slot** is now 64 bytes, the 48 of the hero's motion as before and then the follow camera's offset from what it looks at (16 bytes), so POS_LOAD puts the camera back facing where it faced, as Deadlocked's slots have (section 5.5). A slot saved before, or saved while no follow camera followed the hero, is 48 bytes and still loads, the hero alone and the camera as it is; ToD's POS_STORE takes both 64 and 48 bytes, a 48-byte blob being a slot without the camera that stores as it is and loads the hero alone, and refuses every other length. **ToD's DIE** (and its Die button, feature 4, and the DIE combo) no longer sets the hit points to 0, which played the death animation and its fade: it asks the game for the respawn a death's end asks for, at the last checkpoint at once, through the game's own data (section 5.5). From the game's pause menu it waits for the pause to close. A respawn is no load, so the autosplitter emits nothing for it, as for any death. Both are plain data writes, the same under RPCS3.

Revision 1.31, Tools of Destruction's loads from the pause and a cinematic, and the lock-strafe camera (module build 58, 2026-11-14): no opcode and no structure moved, and the revision number stays. **PLANET_LOAD in ToD's pause or during a realtime cinematic** answers OK where it answered BUSY: the game's own `LoadLevel` loads at once wherever it is not in play (`g_gameState` other than 4), which is the pause menu's own Load, and qwark does exactly that there, as data (section 5.5). With the Trainer's glue in the pause, a client's PLANET_LOAD is still taken by the menu as revision 1.29 has it (the pause closed by the glue, then the load in play). **SAVEFILE_RESTORE and LOAD_ASIDE** are accepted in the pause and during a cinematic too (section 5.12.4): the game's `SaveManager_onLoaded` loads the level at once there itself, the pause menu's own Load to the word, so the client follows the transfer to its end as in play. Still `BUSY`: the galactic map, an FMV, a pause whose loading thread is still building it, a state change already asked for. DIE is unchanged (made as the pause closes; `BUSY` during a cinematic). **A ToD position slot now carries the lock-strafe camera too**: with Game Options' Lock-Strafe on the hero's camera is type 26, not the follow camera (type 3), and every slot saved there was 48 bytes, so a load never turned the camera; the camera is now the hero's client's active slot of either type (section 5.5). docs/tod-menu.md section 20.

Revision 1.31 (pending), the input display at the XMB, a probe for the hardware (module build 58, 2026-11-13): no opcode and no structure moved, and the revision number stays. **SessionInfo's `pad_mask` and `analog`** (section 3) now come from whichever of two views of pad 1 has the pad: the VSH's own, which a console reads through the XMB's libpad (sys_io's `cellPadGetData`, as webMAN MOD's XMB combos do), and the running game's, read out of its memory as before. At the XMB, in BOOTING and QUITTING, and in a title qwark has no game for (section 3.3), the VSH has the pad, and its view stands for as long as libpad reports no change, so a button held still stays lit. In a game the game has the pad, except while the PS button's overlay has it, which the module tells by GetInfo2's `system_info` bit0 (INTERCEPTED) once that bit has been seen to differ in a game from its value while the game takes input (either sense), and until then by who last took input (the VSH when its libpad reports a change with something held while the game's pad is empty, the game when its pad changes with something held, the game again after a second with nothing new from the VSH). As the game takes the pad back the VSH's view is dropped, so the overlay's close shows the game's pad in the next packet with nothing of the VSH's left lit. While the VSH has the pad its buttons go out when one is held, else the game's while a game is INGAME with its code in, else 0; its sticks the same way, apart from the buttons, when one is past a dead zone of 32 of 128. So at the XMB the two fields carry the controller where they were 0 or the last game's, and in a game with the overlay open they carry the buttons pressed in it. The module reads the VSH's pad every tick (120 Hz, as it reads a game's pad out of its memory), GetInfo2 once per telemetry frame, and only while a client is connected; telemetry still goes out at 30 Hz in every state. The combos (section 5.9) and the console menu still read the game's own pad alone. qwark-rpcs3 and the simulator until its `vshpad` command have no VSH pad, so the fields are the game's as before. The module logs what each view sees in qwark.log, and a line at every change of who has the pad with what drove it, for the hardware probe (rewrite/docs/REPORT-vsh-pad-probe.md).

### Revision 1.32 (2026-11-14, module build 58)

Revision 1.32, the telemetry rate (module build 58, 2026-11-14): the telemetry packet (section 4), which carries `pad_mask` and the sticks for a client's input display, went out every fourth tick, 30 Hz, in every build until now. **TELEMETRY_RATE** at 0x0097, `u8 hz`, writes config.txt's new key **`telemetry.hz`**: 30 (the default, and what a file that does not name it, or names anything else, reads as), 60 or 120, a packet every 4, 2 or 1 ticks of the 120 Hz tick from the tick after the write; any other byte is `BAD_ARG` and writes nothing. It is a console-wide key (section 5.10.1), not a per-user one: it is how often this console talks to the PCs connected to it, the same for every subscriber, and a user signing in at the XMB does not change it. It answers in any session state and on every platform, qwark-rpcs3 included (section 5.10.3). **SessionInfo `flags3`** gains bit2 TELEMETRY_RATE, the module answers TELEMETRY_RATE (the client's capability bit), and bits 3-4, the rate in force: 0 30 Hz, 1 60 Hz, 2 120 Hz. Nothing else moved: a client before this revision never sends the op and the console keeps sending at 30 Hz unless another client has asked for more, which an older client takes as it takes any packet (it keeps the latest); a module before it sends 0 in all three bits, which a client reads as no switch and 30 Hz. The autosplit datagrams still go out every tick (section 8.4), GetInfo2 is still asked once per 33 ms (revision 1.31), and GET_STATE still answers the last packet published, now at most one telemetry period old.

### Revision 1.33 (2026-11-14, module build 58)

Revision 1.33, Tools of Destruction's revisits (2026-11-14): an autosplit event's `u16 reserved` (section 8.1) is now `u16 flags`, still 0 on every event but the ones below, and an `EventDesc` gains flags bit5 REVISIT (section 8.2). A game whose planet row (code 1) sets REVISIT marks a planet event whose load is a revisit with the event's flags bit0, REVISIT, in AUTOSPLIT_EVENTS and in the `QE` datagram alike; START and RESET never carry flags. Only ToD sets it, on a load into Metropolis or Cobalia with `LVL_FAST_SHIP_FIXED` (level flag 106) set, the game's own test for whether their arrival FMVs play (section 8.5). The client's **Don't split on revisit** takes no split for a marked event. Nothing else moved: an older client skips the two bytes and sees an unknown descriptor bit, so it splits on a revisit as it always has, and an older module sends 0 and no bit5, so a newer client has nothing to offer. `QWARK_BUILD` stays.

Revision 1.33, cheats-only warnings, apply toggles on game reboot, and 60 Hz (module build 58, 2026-11-14): an opcode, two bits of SessionInfo `flags3` and a new default. **`run.warnings` takes 2**, cheats only: a run start says the "N cheats enabled" line and no mod line (section 5.10.2); 1 still means mods and cheats, the default, so every config.txt keeps its behaviour, and any other number in the file reads as 1. RUN_WARN takes 0, 1 or 2 and is `BAD_ARG` for anything else. **Apply toggles on game reboot**, config.txt's new per-user key **`reboot.apply`**, says what a same-game reboot does with the previous-session record (section 4.1): 1 Ask (the default) leaves it for a client to offer, as every earlier build did, but drops it when no client is connected at the reboot; 0 Always re-applies it as the game arrives, with or without a client; 2 Never drops it (section 5.10.4). The toggles and mods flagged on boot are applied at every boot in every mode, as before. **REBOOT_APPLY** (0x0098), `u8 mode`, writes the key, and with no byte reads it (`u8 mode`). **`flags3`** gains bit5 SETTINGS_133, the client's capability bit for both settings (the module takes RUN_WARN 2 and answers REBOOT_APPLY), and bit6 RUN_WARN_CHEATS, `run.warnings` is 2; bit7 stays 0. Bit1 RUN_WARN_OFF keeps meaning exactly 0, so a client before this revision reads off as off and cheats only as on; a module before it sends 0 in bits 5 and 6, refuses RUN_WARN 2 with `BAD_ARG` and answers REBOOT_APPLY `UNKNOWN_OP`. (A test build of the revision had `boot.apply`, which held back the on-boot toggles, with BOOT_APPLY at 0x0098, BOOT_ASK at 0x0099 and `flags3` bit7 BOOT_ASK; none of them is left: `boot.apply` is not read, 0x0099 is `UNKNOWN_OP` and bit7 is 0.) **`telemetry.hz` reads as 60** when config.txt does not name it, or names a value that is no rate (30 before this revision): a console set to 30 by hand or by TELEMETRY_RATE stays at 30, and a module before this revision still sends at 30 without the key.

### Revision 1.34 (pending, module build 59)

Revision 1.34 (pending, module build 59, 2026-11-18): build 58 shipped with client 1.6.0 as a release, so what follows is build 59. A client that expects build 59 reads a console still on build 58 as out of date and replaces it. The changes of this revision are listed below as they land.

Armour and skin pickers (build 59): ENUM rows as UYA's Armour (11) is, group Player on the wire, with a readout each. RaC2 gains 40 Armour (five options, no armor to Carbonox, the big-endian halfword at 0x1329AB0) and 41 Skin (five: none and the Cheats menu's four skin cheats, the bytes at 0x14817F3, 0x14817FA, 0x14817FB and 0x14817FC, one on and the rest off), readouts 9 Armour and 10 Skin; both write -1 into the loaded-armour word 0x147A310 as well, so the next planet load reads Ratchet's model again, which is when it shows (the protection is at once). UYA gains 40 Skin (ten: Current Armor and the skin menu's nine, ids 5 to 13 in the byte at 0xDA519B), readout 13 Skin, with -1 into 0xD99210 for the same reason; where the trophy reset is left out, readout 12 is still sent, with a blank name, so the skin keeps index 13. Tools of Destruction gains 25 Armour (five, ToDSaveData's armour, its owned word, and the hero's damage scale from the game's own table, as Hero_setArmor writes them) and 26 Skin (nine, the skin word Skin_select writes), readouts 4 Armour and 5 Skin. All are data writes, the same under RPCS3, not in the console menu (as UYA's Armour is not), and not counted by the run-start warnings, which count TOGGLEs alone. No opcode, structure or status changes.

Revision 1.34, the Trainer's page combos (section 5.9): combo actions **7 to 13**, one per page of the in-game Trainer's root, the same list in every game: 7 Game, 8 Cheats, 9 Planets, 10 Manips, 11 Unlocks, 12 Mods, 13 Save files. A press opens the Trainer straight on that page, with OPEN_MENU's rules (section 5.9). Config keys `combo.page.game`, `combo.page.cheats`, `combo.page.planets`, `combo.page.manips`, `combo.page.unlocks`, `combo.page.mods`, `combo.page.saves`, console-wide with per-user overrides like the seven before them, no mask out of the box, and **no line while unbound**: COMBO_SET of 0 on one takes its line out of config.txt (a profile's override of 0 stays a line, since it turns the console's combo off for that user). **COMBO_LIST** has fourteen entries, and after them, new, the pages' names: `u8 m, { u8 action, u8 len, char name[len] }[m]`. A client before this revision reads `n` entries and stops, so it skips the names and draws no row for an action it does not know; a client of this revision against an older module finds no names and offers no page combo, and COMBO_SET or COMBO_OVERRIDE of action 7 on is `BAD_ARG` there anyway. No opcode and no SessionInfo bit. The seven combos before them, their ids and keys are unchanged.
Revision 1.34, the weapon bulk actions in every game (module build 59): RaC2's, UYA's and Deadlocked's Max and Reset all weapon levels and Max all weapon ammo now have their equivalents in RaC1 and Tools of Destruction, labelled the same, ACTIONs in group Progress, and in each game's console menu Collectables page after its own buttons; none is a toggle, so none is in a `cheats` mask (section 5.10.2). No opcode moved. **RaC1** gains **29 "Upgrade all weapons to gold"** and **30 "Reset all gold weapons"**: RaC1's one upgrade is the gold weapon, and only ten weapons have one, the ten the gold weapon vendor sells (`g_goldWeaponItems`, 0x811CB0 in NPEA00385: Tesla Claw, Bomb Glove, Devastator, Pyrocitor, Mine Glove, Blaster, Morph-O-Ray, Glove of Doom, Decoy Glove, Suck Cannon). Each sets or clears the gold byte (`goldedItems`, 0x969CA8 + item id) of those of the ten the player owns, and touches nothing else. RaC1 has no Max all weapon ammo: id 17 was that, retired in build 53 for Infinite ammo. **RaC1's UNLOCK_LIST changes with them:** its Gold slot (1) is declared only by those ten rows now, where every weapon, gadget and item row but the five index-less items declared it (the old client's checkbox on every row); UNLOCK_SET of Gold on any other row is `UNSUPPORTED`, and the console menu steps such a row through Locked and Unlocked alone. No vendor writes the other rows' bytes, and a byte set there still costs four of the player's gold bolts in the game's own count. **Tools of Destruction** gains **27 "Max all weapon levels"**, **28 "Reset all weapon levels"** and **29 "Max all weapon ammo"** (25 and 26 are the armour and skin pickers'). Each walks the weapons the player owns (WPN_ 1..15; Max all weapon ammo the devices 16..23 too) and passes over the rest, and reads each weapon's caps out of the game's own weapon.csv as loaded, through `g_weaponDefTable` (0x10385AC4, a `ToDWeaponDef *` per WPN_; its +0x18 flags, & 3 a weapon that cannot level, and +0x48 its `ToDWeaponConfig`: +0x460 numLevels, +0x280 the max ammo per level, +0x464 the 24 mods, +0x6A4 their count). Max all weapon levels takes a weapon to WeaponSlot_getMaxLevel's top, the level byte with the XP the game counts it from (as UNLOCK_SET does): min(numLevels, 4), V5, outside challenge mode, and in challenge mode (`g_saveData`+0x906EC above 0) min(numLevels, 9), V10, for a weapon of more than five levels, the Omega levels the vendor sells there (Vendor_canBuyOmegaUpgrade); it never takes a weapon down, so an Omega weapon keeps its level outside challenge mode. Reset all weapon levels puts each owned weapon that levels back to V1 with no XP, which takes an Omega weapon back below Omega. Max all weapon ammo fills each owned weapon and device to WeaponDef_getMaxAmmo for its level and its mods (the level's max ammo plus each fitted ammo mod's addition, times one plus each fitted ammo mod's factor). A weapon that cannot level, or whose def the game has not loaded, is left alone; with no def at all for the weapons the player owns (no level up) each answers `BUSY` and writes nothing. Raritanium upgrades (mods) are not touched: the three games' actions are levels and ammo alone. All of it is data reads and writes, the same under RPCS3. **UYA's Max and Reset all weapon levels (26, 27)** now pass over a weapon the player does not own, as the others' always did: until this build they wrote every weapon's version byte, so a weapon bought later came at V8.

Revision 1.34, Tools of Destruction's moby table (module build 59): MOBY_TABLE (section 5.5) answers for ToD, and its reply's last halfword, a pad until now and still 0 for the four original games, so their 12 bytes are unchanged, is **`u16 flags`**. **bit0 LAST_INDEX**: `table_end_ptr_addr` names an s32, the index of the last slot the table can have live (negative for none), and the table is that many slots plus one from `[table_ptr_addr]`, not up to a pointer. **bit1 LIVE_TEST**: four more bytes follow, `u16 live_offset, u16 dead_value`, and a slot whose big-endian u16 at `live_offset` is `dead_value` is free and not a moby. ToD's is `g_mobyPool` (0x1066C300, `ToDMobyPool`): the slots' pointer at +0 (0x1066C300), the highest index in use at +0x10 (0x1066C310, `maxIndex`, -1 for none), slots of 0x180 bytes (`ToDMoby`), flags 3, the handle at +0x64 and 0xFFFE: MobyPool_init lays out a level's slots each with the handle index | 0xFFFE0000, MobyPool_alloc takes the first free one and raises maxIndex, MobyPool_processFrees writes a freed slot's handle back to that and lowers maxIndex past free slots at the top, so the free slots below it are the ones whose handle's serial is 0xFFFE. The client's ToD layout (`data/moby/Tod.json` in RaCMAN Reloaded) names the slot's fields: the matrix's three rows (+0x00, +0x10, +0x20), the position (+0x30), the game object (+0x40), the 64-bit handle (+0x48), the class (+0x50, u16), the class's def (+0x54), the parent handle (+0x58), the handle (+0x64), the mode flags (+0x68) and the scale (+0xD0). A ToD moby keeps no state or health of its own; those are its game object's, a class of its own behind +0x40. A client before this revision reads ToD's end word as a pointer below the start and says the table is unreadable; one with it reads the four games exactly as before. Plain memory reads, the same under RPCS3.

Revision 1.34, the five games made consistent (module build 59): **DESCRIBE's Feature byte 7**, the pad after `bits`, is `flags2`, bit0 **RUN_LEGAL**: the module declares the row run-legal, once (`struct game_describe`'s `run_legal`), so a client draws it on its Manips page, the console menu lists it on the Trainer's Manips page, and the run-start warning never counts it, whatever the game's `cheats` mask says (section 5.10.2). Deadlocked's Crash patches and Fix reset softlocks, UYA's Reset trophies and its toggle, and ToD's Remove cutscenes, Cache cutscenes, Cache cutscenes on reset and Disable cutscene caching are flagged; ToD's four leave group Cheats for groups of their own, **Manips** and **Cutscene cache**. Every game with collectable actions has them in a DESCRIBE group **Collectables** (RaC1, UYA and ToD gain it; RaC2 had it), each Unlock all before its Reset all. **DESCRIBE has an extension** after the features (section 5.3): `u8 ext_len` (18), `u8 planet_flags`, the PLANET_LOAD flag bits the game's load acts on, `u8 menu_flags`, bit0 MENU_HERE, the Trainer can come up in this game on this platform, and `char menu_row[16]`, the pause row it takes (Help, ToD's Inventory). **ACTION_STATUS** (0x0099, section 5.3): `u8 id, u32 word`, the line a client draws under an ACTION that names a status readout, worded by the game for that word: `u8 tone` (0 nothing, 1 under way, 2 done, 3 failed), `u8 len`, `char text[len]`. 0x0099 was BOOT_ASK in a test build of revision 1.33 and never released; a request of fewer than five bytes is `BAD_ARG`. **PLANET_LOAD's flags** (section 5.5): RaC2's bit1 takes back the destination's platinum bolts, and ToD's load acts on both bits. **Deadlocked's Load set-aside file** (14) and its combo force Fast loads for that load and put the toggle's setting back once the level is up, as the other four games' do (a set-aside load the player backs out of puts them back two seconds after the load menu has closed); a library RESTORE still never forces. **Labels** (no id moved, and none is a config key, so nothing a user saved is lost): RaC1 Reset skill points is Reset all skill points; RaC2 Reset platinum bolts, Reset nanotech boosts and Reset skill points are Reset all platinum bolts, Reset all nanotech boosts and Reset all skill points; ToD Reset skill points and Reset gold bolts are Reset all skill points and Reset all gold bolts. **Retired**: Deadlocked's Unlock all planets (11; its id answers `NOT_FOUND`, a config line for it is ignored). **ToD's Trainer**: no Die row on its Game page, as the other four have none since round 5; its Manips page is the core's page of the run-legal rows, the same rows in the same place. Compatibility: a client before this revision reads the pad byte as padding, never reads past the features, never sends 0x0099, and lays rows out by its own layout file, which still sends ToD's run-legal rows to Manips by label; the new labels of the collectable actions are not in its moves, and land on its Collectables tab by the new group. A client of this revision against an older module finds no extension and no flags2, and keeps that module's pages from a fallback table of its layout file until it has replaced the module.

This file is the contract between qwark (the PS3 SPRX) and every client. Both sides are written against it; when it changes, `QWARK_PROTOCOL_VERSION` changes with it.

All integers are big-endian. Floats are IEEE 754 single precision, big-endian. Fixed-width string fields are NUL-padded and need not be NUL-terminated when full. Variable strings are given with an explicit length unless stated.

Transport: TCP on port 9673 for commands, UDP telemetry sent to the port the client names in SUBSCRIBE. Up to 4 concurrent TCP clients (8 before revision 1.11); a connection arriving when they are all taken is closed as it arrives, which is what always happened one past the limit.

## 1. Frames

```
Request:  u32 length | u16 seq | u16 opcode | payload[length]
Reply:    u32 length | u16 seq | u16 status | payload[length]
```

- `length` is the payload size, at most `QWARK_MAX_PAYLOAD` = 16384 (65600 before revision 1.11). A larger length closes the connection.
- Every request gets exactly one reply carrying the same `seq`. Replies come back in request order, so clients may pipeline.
- An unknown opcode is answered with `UNKNOWN_OP` and an empty payload; the payload is drained and the connection stays open.
- A payload shorter than the opcode requires is answered with `BAD_ARG`.
- A reply listed as **none** below is a frame with status and an empty payload; every request still gets its frame.

### 1.1 While a game launches

The moment the console reports a game process, qwark leaves it alone for one second, measured by the clock rather than counted in ticks. Nothing reads the process, the XMB is not asked for its title, and nothing moves on the network: existing connections neither receive nor reply, and the telemetry and autosplit datagrams stop. Frames sent into that second are answered, in order, once it ends; nothing is buffered for them beyond what the socket holds. The OS still acknowledges TCP by itself, and other plugins are outside this. Ratchetron and the old autosplitter modules kept the same silence, and the consoles that crashed at launch without it are why. None of this depends on which game is starting, because none of it knows yet: the title is not asked for until the second is over, so a process qwark turns out not to know (section 3.3) is launched through exactly the same silence and reaches INGAME the same way.

For the rest of BOOTING, and through QUITTING, the shared request buffers are not handed out. HELLO, HEARTBEAT, SUBSCRIBE, UNSUBSCRIBE, GET_STATE, since build 54 VSH_MEMORY, and since revision 1.26 GAME_MODULE, with at most two payload bytes, use per-connection fixed buffers and keep working. Any other request is drained without them and answered **BUSY**: retry once the session is INGAME. New connections are closed as they arrive. A connection that is holding the request buffers when a transition starts is closed, and the request in flight may get no reply; reconnect as usual.

Since revision 1.11 there is exactly one request buffer and one reply buffer on the console, 16384 bytes each, and one connection holds the pair for the length of one request: the payload is read into it, the command is run, the reply is sent, and it is handed on. Since build 55 (revision 1.23) the pair is one 16384-byte frame and the connection's own 937-byte buffer: a request of up to 937 bytes is read into the connection's buffer and the reply goes into the frame; a longer request goes into the frame and its reply into the connection's buffer, at most 937 bytes. No op takes more than 937 bytes and answers more than that (the bulk writes, MEM_WRITE, FILE_WRITE, SAVEFILE_WRITE, PATCH_APPLY, LEVELFLAGS_SET, POS_STORE and FILE_RENAME's two paths, answer in a few bytes, and every large reply comes from a small request), so a client that sends what this document describes sees no difference; a request padded past 937 bytes gets `FULL`, or the op's own `BAD_ARG` for a length past its room, for a reply that would not fit. A second client's bulk request therefore waits for the first to finish rather than being refused, and the control ops above never wait at all, because they never touch it. Nothing about this is visible on the wire — the ordering guarantee is unchanged, every request still gets exactly one reply carrying its own `seq`, and a client that pipelines sees its replies in request order — but a client that wants two bulk transfers to overlap should know that the console runs them one after the other.

Connected sockets are non-blocking. A payload receive and a reply send each have a two-second deadline, not counting time paused for a launch, and a connection that misses one is closed and the buffers released. Waiting for the next request header has no deadline and holds no buffers.

## 2. Status codes (u16)

| Code | Name | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | OK | |
| 1 | NOT_INGAME | Session state is not INGAME and the command touches game memory |
| 2 | UNSUPPORTED | The current game does not offer this feature, or no game is running |
| 3 | BAD_ARG | Malformed payload or out-of-range argument |
| 4 | IO_ERROR | cellFs or PS3MAPI call failed |
| 5 | FULL | A fixed table is full |
| 6 | UNKNOWN_OP | |
| 7 | NOT_FOUND | Handle, mod, slot or path does not exist |
| 8 | BUSY | Command ring is full, a save transfer is busy, boot is holding the request buffers back, or (revision 1.15) qwark has not yet been able to look for a patched-in savefile helper (section 5.12.1); retry. Revision 1.21: also MANIP_APPLY's refusal, whose reason is in MANIP_LIST's event (section 5.14) |

## 3. Session info block

Shared by HELLO, GET_STATE and telemetry. 164 bytes.

```
u8   protocol_version   = 1
u8   qwark_version      module build number (see below); a client compares it with the build it shipped beside
u8   state              0 XMB, 1 BOOTING, 2 INGAME, 3 QUITTING
u8   game               0 NONE, 1 RAC1, 2 RAC2, 3 RAC3, 4 RAC4 (Deadlocked),
                        5 TOD (Tools of Destruction, revision 1.27; in a release from build 57)
                        BCES01503, the disc trilogy, reports 1, 2 or 3 depending
                        on which executable is mapped; the client never picks.
                        0 while INGAME is a process qwark does not know (section 3.3)
u32  generation         incremented every time a game enters BOOTING
u32  tick               tick-thread counter, 120 Hz
char title_id[12]       e.g. "NPEA00385"
u8   flags              bit0 PREVIOUS_PENDING: a previous-session record is waiting (section 4.1)
                        bit1 EMULATOR: qwark is driving an emulator, not a console (section 3.2)
                        bit2 NO_CODE_PATCHES: this platform refuses code patches (section 3.2)
                        bit3 COMBOS_OFF: the COMBO_ENABLE switch is off, so no combo fires (section 5.9)
                        bit4 CODE_SWITCHES (revision 1.17): the running game's code switches are in it,
                             so its WRITES_CODE toggles work on a platform that sets bit2 (section 5.12.2).
                             Never set by the PS3 module
                        bit5 VSH_MEMORY (build 54): the module has read the VSH's free memory,
                             so VSH_MEMORY answers with it (section 5.1.1). Set from module
                             start on a console; never by qwark-rpcs3
                        bit6 MENU_HELP_OFF (revision 1.23): config.txt's `menu.help` is 0, so the
                             console menu leaves the game's Help row alone (MENU_HELP, sections
                             5.10 and 9.2)
                        bit7 UI_PAK_BAD (revision 1.23, build 55): the console's
                             /dev_hdd0/qwark/ui.pak is missing, damaged or not the one built with
                             this module, so the console menu cannot go into a game (section 9.10),
                             and since build 56 the savefile helper cannot be installed either
                             (SAVEFILE_INFO error 8, section 5.12).
                             Set only on a console, from the module's first tick; never by
                             qwark-rpcs3, and never by a module before build 55
u8   selected_slot      0..7, used by the save/load combos
u8   selected_planet    used by the load-planet combo. Since build 53 the running game's, and
                        outside a game the last game's (section 5.5)
u8   planet_flags       bit0 reset level flags, bit1 reset special bolts, used by the load-planet combo.
                        Which bits a game's load acts on is in section 5.5. The same game's as
                        selected_planet
u8   current_planet     game planet index, 0 when unknown
u8   flags2             revision 1.24, a pad byte before it.
                        bit0 USB_MODS: the module reads mods off the console's USB drives and
                             answers MOD_USB_LIST (section 5.7.2). Set on a console and in the
                             simulator; never by qwark-rpcs3
                        Settings profiles (section 5.10.1):
                        bit1 PROFILES: the module has settings profiles and the console has users
                             to keep them for. The client's capability bit. Set by the PS3 module
                             from build 56 and by the simulator; never by qwark-rpcs3, which has one
                             user, and never by an earlier build
                        bit2 PROFILES_ON: config.txt's `profiles.enabled` is 1
                        bit3 PROFILE_ACTIVE: a console user's profile is the active one, so the
                             per-user settings are that user's
                        bit4 DEV (revision 1.24, test builds): the module is a test build, so it is
                             told from another build of its number by the build ID after HELLO's
                             SessionInfo (section 3.1.1). Never set by a release build or by qwark-rpcs3;
                             since build 57 the only bit that tells a release from a test build
                        Game modules (revision 1.26, section 9.11), INGAME only:
                        bit5 GAME_MODULE_BAD: the running game's module is missing, is no module,
                             would not load or start, or is another build's, so the session has no
                             game features until a client writes the file or the game boots again
                        bit6 GAME_WAITING: the running game's module is there and is held back
                             until the VSH has the memory for it; it loads by itself when it has.
                             Neither bit is ever set by a module whose games are linked in (build 56's
                             release, qwark-rpcs3). GAME_MODULE (section 5.1.2) has the figures
                        bit7 NO_MENU (revision 1.28): the running game has no console
                             menu, so its combo opens nothing and MENU_HELP's switch has no
                             Help row to take (Tools of Destruction until revision 1.29, whose
                             menu is now its own pause menu). INGAME with the game's code in only
u8   profile_seq        revision 1.24, a pad byte before it: goes up by one, wrapping, whenever the
                        active profile changes or is read again (section 5.10.1)
u8   flags3             revision 1.30, a pad byte before it.
                        bit0 RUN_WARN: the module has the run-start warnings and answers RUN_WARN
                             (section 5.10.2). Set by every build from revision 1.30, release and
                             qwark-rpcs3 included; never by an earlier build, which sent a pad byte
                        bit1 RUN_WARN_OFF: config.txt's `run.warnings` is 0, so a run start says
                             nothing
                        bit2 TELEMETRY_RATE (revision 1.32): the module answers TELEMETRY_RATE
                             (section 5.10.3). Set by every build from revision 1.32, release and
                             qwark-rpcs3 included
                        bits 3-4 (revision 1.32) the telemetry rate in force, config.txt's
                             `telemetry.hz`: 0 30 Hz, 1 60 Hz, 2 120 Hz; 3 is never sent.
                             0 from an earlier build, which sends at 30 Hz
                        bit5 SETTINGS_133 (revision 1.33): the module takes RUN_WARN 2, cheats
                             only, and answers REBOOT_APPLY (sections 5.10.2 and 5.10.4). Set by
                             every build from revision 1.33
                        bit6 RUN_WARN_CHEATS (revision 1.33): `run.warnings` is 2, so a run start
                             says the cheats line alone. Bit1 is clear then
                        bit7 reserved, 0
f32  pos[3]             player x, y, z
u32  pad_mask           controller buttons, OG layout (section 6). Revision 1.31: the VSH's own view of
                        pad 1 while the VSH has the pad (at the XMB, or a game's overlay) and a button
                        held, else the running game's pad (INGAME with the game's code in), else 0; see
                        that revision's line at the top
f32  analog[4]          rx, ry, lx, ly, each -1..1, (v - 128) / 128 of libpad's byte. Revision 1.31: the
                        VSH's sticks while it has the pad and one is past the dead zone, else the game's,
                        else 0
u32  readout[16]        game-defined live values; DESCRIBE names them. readout[0] is the bolt count when the game has one. VALUE, ENUM and COLOR features name the readout that mirrors their current value
u64  toggle_state       bit i set = TOGGLE feature id i is on. A LIVE toggle's bit is whatever game memory says, re-read at 10 Hz (section 5.3.1)
u64  toggle_auto        bit i set = feature id i re-applies itself on game boot. Never set for a LIVE toggle
u64  freeze_active      bit i set = freeze id i is active
u32  mod_loaded         bit i set = console mod index i is loaded by qwark. A mod an RPCS3 patch put in
                        the game is not in here: MOD_LIST's LOADED bit says that (revision 1.16, section 5.7.1)
u32  mod_auto           bit i set = console mod index i auto-applies on game boot
u32  mod_previous       bit i set = mod i was loaded before the last same-game reboot and is neither re-applied nor dismissed
```

### 3.1 `qwark_version`, the build number

`qwark_version` is the build number of the module itself (`QWARK_BUILD` in `src/core/proto.h`), and is independent of `protocol_version`. The wire contract can hold still while what sits behind it moves, so the build number increments whenever the feature tables change — a feature added, retired or relabelled — or whenever any other user-visible behaviour changes. It never decrements and is never reused.

Retiring a feature leaves its id behind for good: ids are the wire contract for the toggle bitmaps, nothing is renumbered, and a client that still knows a retired id simply never sees it in DESCRIBE again.

Build 53 retired these besides the manip rows (section 5.14), and as with those, config.txt auto flags for them are ignored and the previous-session record never offers them:

| Game | Retired id |
|---|---|
| RaC1 | 17 Max ammo, all weapons (Infinite ammo covers it); 28 Freeze health, the row it had beside Infinite health for a while (id 2 is Freeze health now) |
| RaC2 | 4 Weapon insta-upgrades (the Unlocks panel sets weapon levels) |
| UYA | 2 One-hit KO |

A relabelled feature keeps its id. config.txt keeps a toggle's auto flag under its label (`<TITLE>.auto.<slug>`), so a game names the labels its toggles had, and a toggle reads its flag from the old label's key until one is written under the new label's, which wins from then on. Build 53 relabelled RaC1's Infinite health (2) as Freeze health and UYA's Freeze ammo (0) as Infinite ammo.

A client ships knowing the build it was developed against. When the console reports a *lower* `qwark_version` than that, the client warns the user that the SPRX on the console is out of date and should be re-uploaded; DESCRIBE still answers, so the client keeps working against whatever tables the older module actually has. A *higher* number is not an error: the console is newer than the client, and DESCRIBE remains the authority on what exists.

### 3.1.1 Test builds: `flags2` bit4 DEV and the build ID (revision 1.24)

The build number moves once per release, and a release is many test builds, all reporting the same number. So a module also says which build of its number it is:

- **`flags2` bit4 DEV**: set by a test build. A plain `make` builds one (`QWARK_DEV=1`, the makefile's default, so a build nobody marked is a test build); `make release`, or `make QWARK_DEV=0`, builds a release module, which never sets it. `qwark-rpcs3` never sets it.
- **`build_id`**, a big-endian `u32` after SessionInfo in HELLO's reply only (168 bytes in all): the CRC-32 of the module's sources (`QWARK_BUILD_ID`; tools/buildid.py: every `.c` and `.h` under `src/` and the makefile, each after its path, carriage returns dropped), so the same sources give the same ID on any machine and a change to them gives another. 0 is no ID: `qwark-rpcs3`, which the client starts beside itself and replaces with itself, and a module before this revision, whose 164-byte reply reads as 0. A release module carries its ID as well; nothing decides on it.

`make` writes `qwark.build` beside `qwark.sprx`, and `make dist` copies it into `dist/`, so a client's release carries the description of the module it ships: three lines of text after a comment, `build 56`, `dev 1` (or `dev 0`) and `id 0x1A2B3C4D`.

The rule, for a client that knows the build and ID of the module it ships, against a console (not `flags` bit1 EMULATOR):

| Console's module | Client's module, same build | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| lower build | any | stale, as before |
| higher build | any | current, as before |
| DEV | an ID that differs | stale: replaced as an older build is |
| DEV | the same ID | current, so it is not sent again and again |
| no DEV | DEV, an ID that differs | stale: a test build is newer than the release of its number |
| no DEV | no DEV | current: two release modules of one build are judged by the number alone |

A client that has no `qwark.build` beside its module, or one that names another build than the client expects, falls back to the build number alone. Under RPCS3 the build number alone decides too: `qwark-rpcs3.exe` is started by the client from beside itself and goes with it.

### 3.1.2 Features a module may leave out (revision 1.24)

Two features of revision 1.24 are still being tried out, and a module may be built without them (and, with them, the console menu's Mods page, section 9.1, which has no op or bit of its own): the **settings profiles** (5.10.1: PROFILE_INFO 0x0093, PROFILE_ENABLE 0x0094 and COMBO_OVERRIDE 0x0095; `flags2` bits 1 to 3 and byte 30 `profile_seq`; COMBO_LIST's OVERRIDDEN flag) and the **USB mods** (5.7.2: MOD_USB_LIST 0x0067; `flags2` bit0). Their opcodes and bits stay reserved and documented whether a module has them or not, and are never given to anything else.

A module without them never sets their bits (`flags2` bits 0 to 3 and byte 30 are 0, and no COMBO_LIST row is OVERRIDDEN, as the pad bytes of 1.4.1 were) and answers their four ops UNSUPPORTED. It never looks at `/dev_usb00N` and never reads or writes `/dev_hdd0/qwark/profiles`; every setting is config.txt's and the position slots are `positions/`'s, whatever `profiles.enabled` says (the key is kept, as any key is), and a mod only a USB drive has is unknown to it, as in 1.4.1. A client tells by the bits alone, as it does from a module of an earlier build, and offers nothing of either.

Which modules have them is the build's choice (`QWARK_PREVIEW`, src/core/proto.h). Build 56's test builds had both and its release module (`make release`) left both out, compiled out altogether. **Build 57 ships them in its release**: `QWARK_PREVIEW` is 1 unless given itself, whatever `QWARK_DEV` says, so a test build and a release both have them, and only a module built with `QWARK_PREVIEW=0`, which no release ships, leaves them out. A client still tells by the bits alone. The simulator (`qwark-host.exe`) and `qwark-rpcs3.exe` are built with them; `qwark-rpcs3` offers neither all the same (5.7.2, 5.10.1).

### 3.2 `flags` bit1 EMULATOR and bit2 NO_CODE_PATCHES (revision 1.6)

qwark also runs on a PC as `qwark-rpcs3.exe`, driving RPCS3 through its PINE IPC server instead of a console through PS3MAPI. The core, the game tables and every opcode in this document are the same; two things about the platform are not, and both travel in `flags`.

**bit1 EMULATOR** is set when the thing on the other side is an emulator. It is cosmetic: say "RPCS3" rather than "console" and do not offer to upload an SPRX.

**bit2 NO_CODE_PATCHES** is not cosmetic. RPCS3 recompiles PPU code ahead of execution, so writing an instruction word changes memory and the game carries on running the old instruction. It goes further than that once the game is loaded: RPCS3 applies its own patches to the executable and then takes write permission away from every segment of it that has none of its own, the code segment and whatever read-only data shares it. From then on a write into that segment fails, a MEM_WRITE through PINE and a store the game makes itself alike; data memory stays writable. Rather than let a checkbox lie, qwark refuses everything that depends on a code patch:

| Request | Answer when bit2 is set |
|---|---|
| FEATURE_SET or FEATURE_TRIGGER on a feature flagged `WRITES_CODE` | `UNSUPPORTED`, except FEATURE_SET on a toggle while bit4 CODE_SWITCHES is set (revision 1.17, section 5.12.2), and on a COLOR (revision 1.24: Deadlocked's HUD colours) |
| PATCH_APPLY | `UNSUPPORTED` |
| MOD_LOAD of a mod with patch words or code caves | `UNSUPPORTED`, and MOD_UNLOAD has nothing to unload. The mod goes in as an RPCS3 patch instead (revision 1.16, section 5.7.1) |
| SAVEFILE_READ, SAVEFILE_WRITE, and the SAVE_ASIDE and LOAD_ASIDE actions | `UNSUPPORTED` unless an RPCS3 patch put the savefile helper in the game when it loaded (revision 1.15, section 5.12.1) |
| Every op of the savefile library: SAVEFILE_CATEGORIES, SAVEFILE_LIST, SAVEFILE_STORE, SAVEFILE_RESTORE, SAVEFILE_CATEGORY | `UNSUPPORTED` under the same condition (section 5.13). The library is only reachable through the helper, so without one there is nothing to copy out of and nothing to hand a copy to |
| FEATURE_TRIGGER of UYA's Reset trophies (21), and FEATURE_SET of its toggle (39) to on | `UNSUPPORTED` always, patch or not (revision 1.21, section 5.14). The reset deletes the game's trophy data on the console first, and RPCS3 keeps its own where qwark cannot reach it; the game's half alone would change nothing a run can see |
| The OPEN_MENU combo (revision 1.22) | nothing but a notification: the console menu's renderer is code, so `qwark-rpcs3` installs none and there is no menu (section 9). Revision 1.29: Tools of Destruction's menu, the game's own pause menu, works once the Lua helper's patch is in (section 9.12) |
| MENU_HELP (revision 1.23) | `UNSUPPORTED`: there is no menu, and so no Help row of its. Revision 1.29: answered as on a console in a build with Tools of Destruction (`qwark-rpcs3`), whose menu works here |
| ui.pak (revision 1.23, build 55) | never looked at, and `flags` bit7 UI_PAK_BAD never set: there is no menu to read |
| PROFILE_ENABLE and COMBO_OVERRIDE (revision 1.24) | `UNSUPPORTED`: RPCS3 has one user, so there are no settings profiles. PROFILE_INFO answers with every byte 0, and SessionInfo `flags2` bit1 is never set |
| MOD_USB_LIST (revision 1.24) | `UNSUPPORTED`, and `flags2` bit0 never set: a mod is an RPCS3 patch here, and qwark never reads RPCS3's USB folders, so a mod there is neither listed nor copied (section 5.7.2) |
| The TOGGLE_FAST_LOADS combo (revision 1.23) | refused as FEATURE_SET of the game's Fast loads is, unless bit4 CODE_SWITCHES is set, with a notification that says the patches are needed |

SAVEFILE_INFO was in this table until revision 1.15. It answers on this platform now, and its `installed` byte says whether the helper is in the game, which is how a client knows whether to offer to write the patch; SAVEFILE_PATCH answers on this platform (section 5.12.1).

**The patch ops are the emulator builds' alone** (revision 1.18). SAVEFILE_PATCH, MOD_PATCH and SWITCH_PATCH, and the looks that find what their patches put in a game, are built into `qwark-rpcs3.exe` and the `qwark-host.exe` simulator and not into `qwark.sprx`: the PS3 module runs beside other VSH plugins in very little memory, and a console patches code itself. The PS3 module answers the three with `UNSUPPORTED`, where until revision 1.18 it answered them for information. A client asks for them only while bit2 is set, so it never sees that answer.

**Mods work here too, once the client has written them as RPCS3 patches and the game has been restarted** (revision 1.16). A mod is code - patch words, code caves or both - so qwark cannot put one into a running game on this platform, but RPCS3 applies its own patch files as it loads the executable, before it compiles anything. The client asks for each mod with MOD_PATCH, a page at a time since revision 1.19, writes it into RPCS3's per-title patch file, enables it in `patch_config.yml`, and the user restarts the game; qwark then finds the mod in game memory and MOD_LIST reports it LOADED. What does not exist here is everything that changes a mod's state inside a session: MOD_LOAD is refused and MOD_UNLOAD has nothing to unload, as the table says, auto-flagged mods are skipped at boot as they always were, and there is no previous-session reapply for them, because qwark never loaded them and the record never holds them. Taking a mod out is the reverse of putting it in: the client disables the patch and the game is restarted. Section 5.7.1 has the rules.

Everything else works unchanged: memory reads and writes, freezes, watches, positions, planet loads, unlocks, level flags, colours, values, and every toggle whose truth is a data byte rather than an instruction. One difference inside a planet load: on a console, RaC1's, RaC2's, RaC3's and Deadlocked's PLANET_LOAD, and RaC1's, RaC2's and RaC3's LOAD_ASIDE, force fast loads on first, which is a code patch, so here they leave that step out and the load takes the game's own time rather than being refused. With the code switches in (revision 1.17) they force them after all, through the fast-load flag byte. RaC3's fast loads were two data words until they became a code patch (section 5.12.3), and went through here with nothing installed; they need the code switches now, like the others. The resets a planet load's flags ask for are data writes and work here exactly as on a console, code switches or not: the level flags, and the special bolts, RaC1's gold bolts and, since build 53, UYA's titanium bolts (section 5.5).

**The WRITES_CODE toggles work here too, once the client has written the game's code switches as an RPCS3 patch and the game has been restarted** (revision 1.17). The patch gives the game both forms of every instruction those toggles change, and a flag byte in data memory that picks one, so a toggle is a byte write; qwark finds the switches in the game once a session and sets bit4 CODE_SWITCHES while they are there. Section 5.12.2 has the rules. Without them, everything in this section stands.

DESCRIBE is **unchanged**: the WRITES_CODE rows are still listed, with the same ids, labels, groups and flags. A client greys them itself from bit2 rather than discovering one refusal at a time, so the same DESCRIBE parsing works against both platforms and a client built before this revision still functions, it simply sees the refusals instead of predicting them. Since revision 1.17 a client offers them again while bit4 CODE_SWITCHES is set; DESCRIBE is the same then too.

Two side effects a client should know about, both inside qwark rather than on the wire:

- Auto-flagged WRITES_CODE toggles (Deadlocked's crash patches, for instance) are skipped when a game reaches INGAME. `toggle_state` reports them as off, which is the truth. Since revision 1.17 they wait for qwark's look for the code switches instead, and go on through their flag bytes when it finds them.
- The games' own embedded helpers are code patches too, so they are not installed. RaC1's autosplit helper is one, and without it the four collectable reason codes (gold bolt, skill point, item, infobot) never fire; every other RaC1 split is a plain memory read and is unaffected. Deadlocked's quit and loading hooks are the others: the PAUSE still fires when the game goes away, because the session also watches the process itself, and the RESUME then fires on the way back INGAME rather than on the SCE logo. The savefile helper is a third, and the one with a way back in (revision 1.15): RPCS3 applies its own patch files to the executable as it loads it, before it recompiles anything, so a patch that writes the helper's caves and hook words at load, and zeroes its request bytes, is code the game really runs. The client writes that patch from SAVEFILE_PATCH into RPCS3's per-title patch file, `patches/<TITLE_ID>_patch.yml`, enables it in `patch_config.yml`, and the game is restarted; from then on qwark finds its helper in the game and the whole of sections 5.12 and 5.13 works as it does on a console. A patch written by an older qwark build no longer matches this one's helper and reads as not present, so the client writes the new one. Section 5.12.1 has the rules.

### 3.3 INGAME with `game` 0: a process qwark does not know

A title id no game table claims still reaches INGAME. `state` is 2, `game` is 0 NONE, and `title_id` is the id the console reported. It is a real session against a real process: the memory tools work on it exactly as they do on a game.

- **MEM_READ, MEM_WRITE, WATCH_\*, FREEZE_\* and PATCH_\*** work, under the same rules as always. A watch reads live and its value travels in every telemetry packet; PATCH_APPLY is still refused where `flags` bit2 says the platform cannot patch code.
- **Everything that needs a game table answers `UNSUPPORTED`**: DESCRIBE, FEATURE_SET, FEATURE_TRIGGER, FEATURE_SET_AUTO, FEATURE_OPTIONS, PLANET_LIST, PLANET_LOAD, POS_SAVE, POS_LOAD, UNLOCK_LIST, UNLOCK_SET, LEVELFLAGS_GET, LEVELFLAGS_RESET, LEVELFLAGS_SET, LEVELFLAGS_BIT, LEVELFLAGS_NAMES, DIE, MOBY_TABLE, AUTOSPLIT_DESCRIBE and every op of sections 5.12 and 5.13, SAVEFILE_PATCH included (there is no helper to describe). The ops that were never about the game answer as they always do: the listings, the config and combo ops, the file ops and AUTOSPLIT_EVENTS. PLANET_SELECT, and the planet selection in this block, are the last game's that was INGAME, as at the XMB (build 53, section 5.5).
- **No readout, position or pad data is filled in.** `current_planet`, `pos`, `pad_mask`, `analog` and every `readout` are 0 and stay 0, because reading them means knowing where they are. `toggle_state`, `toggle_auto` and `freeze_active` behave as usual; nothing fires the stored combos, since the game's pad is not read. Revision 1.31: on a console, `pad_mask` and `analog` carry the VSH's own view of the pad, which has the pad here as at the XMB.

Everything else about the session is unchanged: the launch is quiet for its second (section 1.1), `generation` advances, QUITTING and the return to the XMB happen when the process goes away, and a same-title reboot keeps its watches and builds the previous-session record (section 4.1) out of what is actually there, which is freezes and client patches.

A client should offer the memory panel and hide the game panels when `game` is 0 while `state` is 2. DESCRIBE answering `UNSUPPORTED` is the same answer it gives at the XMB, so no client is surprised by it.

**Revision 1.26: a game whose module is not in** (section 9.11) is this same session: `game` 0 and `title_id` the game's, with `flags2` bit6 GAME_WAITING while the module waits for memory and bit5 GAME_MODULE_BAD when there is none to be had. When the module arrives `game` becomes the game's id and the session is the game's from then on, as if it had just reached INGAME: a client describes it then (the change of `game` is what it already watches for). GAME_MODULE says which game it is meanwhile. The record of section 4.1 is offered once the game arrives.

## 4. Telemetry packet (UDP)

Sent every fourth tick (30 Hz) to every subscriber; since revision 1.32 every fourth, second or single tick (30, 60 or 120 Hz) after config.txt's `telemetry.hz` (60 Hz since revision 1.33 when the file does not name it), which TELEMETRY_RATE writes and SessionInfo `flags3` bits 3-4 report (section 5.10.3). One packet is a complete snapshot; the client keeps only the latest one.

```
char magic[4]        "QWRK"
SessionInfo          164 bytes (section 3)
u8   nwatch
{ u8 id, u8 size, u8 valid, u8 pad, u64 value }[nwatch]    value is right-aligned: a 1-byte watch is in the low byte
```

`valid` is 0 when the watch could not be read this tick (not in game). Maximum 64 watches, so the packet never exceeds 937 bytes.

GET_STATE returns exactly these bytes over TCP.

The same UDP port also receives 20-byte autosplit datagrams, which start `QE` rather than `QWRK` (section 8.3). Tell them apart by the magic and the length.

### 4.1 Same-game reboot and the previous-session record

When the game exits and the same title comes back:

- Watches are kept and resume automatically; their ids do not change.
