```
              row it is 4 PAUSE, and its RESUME carries the same code
u8  flags     bit0 = a client should enable it by default
              bit1 = "planet route" applies (only code 1 sets it)
              bit2 = FLAT       (section 8.3)
              bit3 = NORMALISE  (section 8.3)
u8  reserved  0
u32 param_us  the timing parameter, in microseconds; 0 when neither timing flag
              is set. A row never sets both flags, and a row that sets FLAT
              always names a non-zero parameter. Revision 1.28: a
              NORMALISE row may name 0, which leaves no allowance and takes the
              whole interval off; every other NORMALISE row names one
              bit4 = HOLD       (revision 1.29, section 8.3), on a LOAD_START
              row only, never with FLAT or NORMALISE, `param_us` 0
              bit5 = REVISIT    (revision 1.33, pending), on the planet row
              (code 1) only: this game marks a revisit with the
              event's flags bit0. A client may offer not to split on one
char label[24]  NUL-padded, what the client shows as a checkbox
```

The list is static per game and never changes within a session.

### 8.3 The two timing rules

A row carries at most one of these, and the client applies it **whenever the autosplitter is on** — they are not user options, and they are not gated on whether the row's checkbox is ticked.

- **FLAT** — when the described event fires, subtract `param_us` from game time. On a SPLIT row the subtraction happens *before* the split is taken, which is the order the old scripts wrote it in.
- **NORMALISE** — time the interval from the described event to the one that closes it (the matching LOAD_END, or the RESUME after a PAUSE) and subtract `max(0, duration - param_us)`. The interval therefore always costs exactly `param_us`, however long it really took. The `time_ms` on the two events is what to measure with.

- **HOLD** (revision 1.29) — game time stops when the LOAD_START fires and starts again at the matching LOAD_END: `pausegametime` and `unpausegametime` in LiveSplit's terms, with game time initialised first (`addloadingtimes 0`) so there is something to stop. Nothing is measured and nothing is subtracted. The two commands go through the same queue as a NORMALISE pause's, so a hold that opens right after a RESUME can never be undone by that RESUME's unpause. A hold is timing like the other two, applied whenever the autosplitter is on, and is not the Pause switch's: that one decides only the quit's pair.

A FLAT **load** row is paid on its LOAD_START; its LOAD_END, if the game sends one, only closes the pair and costs nothing. RaC2's three load rows send no LOAD_END at all, because the script they come from never timed a load — it only paid a toll on entering one.

### 8.4 The UDP push

On the tick an event is emitted, and again on each of the next two ticks, qwark sends a 20-byte datagram to every telemetry subscriber — the same UDP socket and subscriber list telemetry uses. Three sends cover a lost datagram without anyone keeping a timer; a client that sees the same `seq` three times ignores the repeats.

```
char magic[2]  'Q','E'
u8   version   1
u8   reserved  0
Event          16 bytes, section 8.1
```

The magic and the length tell it apart from a telemetry packet, which always starts `QWRK` and is never 20 bytes. An event is **never** carried inside the telemetry packet. Datagrams go out every tick rather than every fourth, because a split has to reach the PC in single-digit milliseconds. AUTOSPLIT_EVENTS over TCP is the catch-up read for anything that was dropped.

### 8.5 Reason codes per game

Ported from racman's `rac1-autosplitter.asl`, `rac2-autosplitter.asl`, `rac3-autosplitter.asl` and `rac4-LC-autosplitter.asl`. "Default" is bit0 of `flags`, and for a split row it matches whether the script's setting defaulted to true. Every timing row is default-on, because the adjustment it carries was never a setting. The rows are listed in the order DESCRIBE returns them.

**RaC1** — START/RESET when the game state goes 6 to 0 while on Veldin (planet 0).

| Code | Kind | Label | Default | Timing | `arg` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | SPLIT | Planet entered | yes (route) | — | the destination planet; never Veldin (0), at either end |
| 2 | SPLIT | Veldin | yes | — | 0. Fires once per run; a latch stops the double split |
| 3 | SPLIT | Drek button | yes | — | 0. The player state reaching 34 within 1.7 of one of the four buttons |
| 8 | LOAD_START | Loading screen | yes | NORMALISE 7 560 000 | the loading-screen id it moved to; 4 on the LOAD_END |

Codes 4 to 7 (gold bolt, skill point, item, infobot) are reserved and neither advertised nor emitted. They counted words that the `gb_sp_as_helper` mod kept, and qwark used to write that mod into the game on every launch; written that early it blanked the screen, so it is gone, and with it the four splits. A client only ever sees the rows DESCRIBE returns, so a saved preference for one of those codes enables nothing.

Code 8 is the script's `isLoading` block. It started a 7.56 s timer when the loading-screen id left 4 and only called the game "loading" once that timer had run out, so the first 7.56 s of every load counted towards game time and the rest did not. qwark emits LOAD_START when the id leaves 4 and LOAD_END when it comes back, and the client subtracts `max(0, duration − 7.56 s)`.

**RaC2** — START/RESET when the player state goes 0 to 98 while on Aranos (planet 0).

| Code | Kind | Label | Default | Timing | `arg` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | SPLIT | Planet entered | yes (route) | — | the planet just entered; never Aranos (0) and never the Insomniac Museum (21) |
| 2 | SPLIT | Protopet defeated | yes | FLAT 116 667 | 0 |
| 3 | SPLIT | Aranos 2 Clank swap | yes | — | 0 |
| 4 | SPLIT | Maktar arena entry | no | — | 0 |
| 5 | SPLIT | Barlow race entry | no | — | 0 |
| 6 | SPLIT | Endako Clank entry | no | — | 0 |
| 7 | SPLIT | Endako Clank exit | no | — | 0 |
| 8 | SPLIT | Tabora caves | no | — | 0 |
| 9 | LOAD_START | Load transition: slide | yes | FLAT 16 667 | 0 |
| 10 | LOAD_START | Load transition: curved | yes | FLAT 150 000 | 0 |
| 11 | LOAD_START | Load transition: wipe | yes | FLAT 350 000 | 0 |

Codes 9 to 11 are the script's `update` block: every time the load-screen byte changed it subtracted a fixed number of frames at 60 fps, keyed on the value it changed *to* — 1 frame for 0, 9 for 1, 21 for 3, and nothing for any other value. One code each, emitted on the change, with no LOAD_END: the script never timed a load, it only paid a toll on entering one. Code 2 carries the seven frames the script took off immediately before returning true for the Protopet split.

**Why Aranos is not a planet entered.** A new game loads Aranos, and the planet byte gets there first: it reads 0 for as long as the load takes, and only then does the player state flip to 98 and the START/RESET above go out. The script split on that, so the run it was about to throw away ended on a segment nobody ran, and LiveSplit kept the gold. Arriving on Aranos is the beginning of a run in this game and never anything else, so it is not a planet change. **In every game the planet the run starts on is excluded from code 1 for this reason**: RaC1's script did it already, by testing `destinationPlanet != 0` where its Veldin is planet 0; RaC3's says in a comment that it does and does not, and qwark's does.

**RaC3** — START/RESET when the game state goes 6 to 0 while on Veldin (planet 1).

| Code | Kind | Label | Default | Timing | `arg` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | SPLIT | Planet entered | yes (route) | — | the destination planet; never Veldin (1) |
| 5 | SPLIT | Biobliterator defeated | yes | — | 0. Armed by an odd Neffyrious phase at full health |
| 2 | SPLIT | LDF entered | no | — | 0 |
| 4 | SPLIT | Koros bolt 2 | no | — | 0. The second titanium bolt of the run on Koros |
| 3 | SPLIT | Tyhrraguise obtained | no | — | 0 |
| 6 | LOAD_START | Long load | yes | FLAT 1 000 000 | the destination planet |

Code 6 is the script's long load: a second off game time when the loading-screen id becomes 1, **unless** the destination planet or the origin planet is one of 20, 26, 27, 28 or 29, whose loading screens are different ones that only look long. qwark tracks the origin the way the script did — the planet you left when a planet change is under way, the planet you are standing on when one is not — and emits the LOAD_START only when both ends pass. LOAD_END follows when the id leaves 1, and only for a load that actually opened.

The script's split route and its strict-order mode are the client's business: qwark emits code 1 for every planet change and the PC filters.

**Why Veldin is not a planet entered.** The script's comment on the planet-change line reads "we are changing levels (but not reloading, and not going to or from veldin)", but the two tests under it — `destinationPlanet != 0 && planet != 0` — are its RaC1 ancestor's, where Veldin is planet 0. In RaC3 it is planet 1 and neither test catches it, so the load that starts a run over split the run the game-state edge was about to reset a moment later. qwark excludes the destination only: leaving Veldin for Florana is the run's first real split, and RaC3 has no Veldin split of its own to carry it the way RaC1 does.

**Deadlocked** — START/RESET when a load starts for DreadZone (planet 1) with the tutorial flag clear.

| Code | Kind | Label | Default | Timing | `arg` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | SPLIT | Planet entered | yes (route) | — | the destination planet |
| 2 | SPLIT | Vox defeated | yes | — | the destination planet, which is 0 by then |
| 3 | PAUSE | Quit to XMB | yes | NORMALISE 14 800 000 | 0 |

Deadlocked is the only game whose detection already ran on the console; codes 1 and 2 replace the old command stream's single "split" whose packet byte the LC script had to read as "0 means Vox". The script's AEC setting, which disables resetting, is the client's business: qwark still emits every RESET.

**Where Deadlocked's origin planet comes from (build 9).** Code 1 needs the planet the player is coming from as well as the one they are going to, and the game's own current-planet word at `0x009C3240` is saved with the file: after a quit to the XMB and a boot it still holds whatever planet the save was on, so the first load out of the main menu read as a planet change and split. qwark keeps its own origin instead. It is planet 0, the main menu, whenever a session is primed, and it becomes the destination of every load that starts after that — so a load back to the main menu puts it back to 0 on its own, and neither that load nor the load out of the menu after it is a split. The word itself is still read, for the Vox split and for the readout.

The origin is also what makes code 1 a planet *change* rather than a load. The SPRX watched the load request word for a plain 0-to-something edge, and one flight can raise it more than once: the second edge carried the same destination and split a second time, so one transition took two splits. By then the origin is already that destination, so **a load whose destination is the origin is not a planet change** and emits nothing.

Code 3 is the quit to the XMB, which some categories use as a strategy. PAUSE goes out when the game leaves the running state — the quit hook fires, or the process disappears underneath qwark. RESUME carries the same code 3, and the LC script's arithmetic (stop game time for the whole quit and reload, then add 14.8 s back on the way in) is NORMALISE 14 800 000: the quit costs exactly 14.8 s however slow the console was.

**Where Deadlocked's RESUME comes from.** qwark reaches INGAME as soon as the process is back and its fingerprint reads, which is several seconds before the game is playable — it sits behind a loading screen and a warning screen first — so resuming there would hand the runner free time the old autosplitter never gave away. qwark therefore installs the old SPRX's **loading hook** alongside its quit hook when Deadlocked enters INGAME: ten words of trampoline at `0x11904`, a `b +0x80` at `0x11884` that reaches it, and a byte at `0x1710000` the game sets to `0xFF` once it has the SCE logo up. Both hooks go in permanently and are never reverted, and both bytes are cleared on entry so a stale one cannot fire. RESUME is emitted on the first tick that byte reads `0xFF`, and the byte is cleared again for the next quit — exactly the gate the SPRX's `STATUS_INGAME_PENDING` used before it sent `CMD_UNPAUSE`.

**Tools of Destruction** (revision 1.28, reworked in 1.29) — START/RESET when a load into Metropolis (0) begins on a file that has not beaten it: level flag 185, MOVIE_METROPOLIS_TACHYON, clear (`g_saveData`+0x5528, five big-endian u64 words, flag *n* is bit *n* & 63 of word *n* >> 6, so byte 0x102113A8, bit 1). The game sets it as the first planet is beaten, so a new game and a challenge mode (`SaveData_prepareChallengeMode` builds the file from a fresh one) have it clear and every save made after Metropolis has it set. It is read as the load begins, by which time the file is in place: the title giving way to it (a new game, or a Metropolis save made before the planet's end), or a level load with Metropolis as its destination (a challenge mode started out of the credits, or, since revision 1.29, a new game or such a save loaded while on Metropolis). A death or a checkpoint is no load (below). With the flag set, a load into Metropolis is an ordinary planet change (code 1, `arg` 0). Revision 1.28 used the play time instead, which counts the new file's setup and so missed new games.

**RESET at the load, START 0.45 s into the fade in (revision 1.29).** The RESET goes out on that condition, as the load begins. The START is 0.45 s into the fade in that shows Ratchet, which is where the community's timing starts (the user's timing: half a second from 2026-10-31, 0.45 s since 2026-11-02; the fade's start was half a second early). `Level_Enter` makes the `lua_State` (`levelInit`), then puts the screen at black and starts a one-second fade in: `ScreenFade_Set(1.0)` writes 1.0 in the fade's progress and target and 0 in its rate, and `ScreenFade_StartIn(1.0, 0)` writes 0.0 in its target and 1.0 in its rate (`ToDScreenFade` at [`g_bigObj5B8F0` 0x10740BB4]+0x5B600: progress +0x2C, rate +0x30, target +0x34). The overlay's alpha is eased, (1 − cos(π × progress)) / 2. So the START goes out on the first tick, once the run's Metropolis is up (its data in and the `lua_State` non-zero), that the target reads 0.0 (or -0.0) and the progress 0.55f (0x3F0CCCCD, the float nearest 0.55) or less (a negative progress counts as less): 0.45 s after `ScreenFade_StartIn`, since the rate is 1.0 from 1.0. The test is "at most", so a tick that misses the crossing still sees it a little late, and a fade the game starts again (`ScreenFade_Set(1.0)`, then `StartIn`) is waited for again; the START still goes out once a run. It is the same moment, within a tick, whether Fast loads cut the load screen (which blacks the screen with `ScreenFade_Set(1.0)` before `Level_Enter`) or not. A new game's intro FMV (`l01_s01_ntro_fmv`) plays inside the load, from `LevelTransition_begin`'s FMV list, so it is over before any of this. Build 57's START came one frame later, at control (`g_gameState` 4, which `GameState_ApplyPending` applies the frame after `Level_Enter`). A death before the START (a new `lua_State` with no load) drops it, a load elsewhere or the title coming back drops it, and a Metropolis load again sends a new RESET and waits again. While a START waits the fade is read every tick. **Seen under way first** (revision 1.29's finishing touches, after the console's START still came half a second early with the test above): the `lua_State` is non-zero from `levelInit`, inside `Level_StartGameplay`, which then sets up every system before `Level_Enter` reaches its two fade calls, and in that window the fade still reads as the screens before it left it, which on a run's load can be clear (target 0.0, progress 0) and so passed the test at once, half a second before the real halfway point. So the START now also needs the fade in seen under way since the level came up: the target 0.0 with the progress above 0.55 (its dark part, 0.45 s of the game's clock, which no tick misses), or the progress going down from one tick to the next; a target of 1.0 (black, or a fade out, `ScreenFade_Set(1.0)` between `Level_Enter`'s two calls) starts that look again. A fade that only ever reads clear and still is never the START. `ScreenFade_update` (0x27DF1C) moves the progress linearly, by min(the rest, dt × rate) of the game's clock (`g_gameClock`+4), so the START is the fade in's start + 0.45 s of game time, the same moment the user times.

**A death is no load (revision 1.29).** The game's top level is a stack of tiers (`ToDGameTier`: the vtable at +0, `loaded` at +6, `entered` at +7), and each of its states is a list of them (`g_gameStateStacks` 0x10073168, `ToDGameStateStack`, 0x88 bytes each: the state at +0, the count at +4, the tiers from +8). State 3, a level, is the core tier, 0x1037AA38, the level transition's (0x1037AB30, `LevelTransition_begin` its enter), `g_levelLoadTier` (0x1037A908: `LevelTransition_waitLoad` its load, `LevelLoad_onComplete` its enter) and `g_levelTierState` (0x1037A620: `Level_Enter` and `Level_Exit`). A planet load, however it is asked for, goes through `RequestLevelLoadState`, `GameState_Request(3, 3)`: the top three tiers are marked, `GameState_Update` unloads them, and `GameState_startTierLoads` hands the loader thread (`GameState_tierLoaderThread`) state 3's list as its job (0x1073B3A0 = 0x10073300), which it puts back to 0 once the stack is that list. A death or a checkpoint is the hero's death states fading to black and then `Hero_respawnWhenBlack` (0x1F7508) asking `GameState_Request(3, 1)`: only the level tier goes and comes back, so `Level_Exit` and `Level_Enter` run, with a new `lua_State` and a new fade in, but `g_levelLoadTier` stays loaded. A quit to the title asks for state 2. So qwark takes a level load to be under way while the job is 0x10073300 and `g_levelLoadTier`'s byte at +6 is 0: from the frame the old level goes until the new one's data is in, which is seconds with Fast loads on or off. The rising edge of that is the load's start, for the RESET, code 1 and the START's wait. Both words are read every second tick, on the same tick. Build 57 used the `lua_State` going to 0, or changing, which a death makes too, and so it RESET a fresh file on every death on Metropolis.

| Code | Kind | Label | Default | Timing | `arg` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | SPLIT | Planet entered | yes (route, REVISIT) | — | the destination, a LEVEL_ id; flags bit0 on a revisit to Metropolis or Cobalia |
| 7 | SPLIT | Tachyon defeated | yes | — | 0 (revision 1.29; "Final fade to black" in build 57) |
| 6 | SPLIT | Mission completed | no | — | the level in the high 16 bits, the mission's id (its label's text id) in the low 16 |
| 4 | LOAD_START | Long load | yes | HOLD | 0, on both halves |
| 3 | PAUSE | Quit to XMB | yes | NORMALISE 19 950 000 | 0 |

**The long load (code 4) is the old autosplitter's, exactly.** The ToD autosplitter SPRX (`tod-autosplitter-src`, `ratchetron.c`) polled two bytes every 8.3 ms, `savePlanetID` at 0x1029C55B and `loadScreenType` at 0x102034FB, and sent PAUSE on the rising edge of `is_loading = savePlanetID == 0 && loadScreenType > 1` and UNPAUSE on the falling edge; the LC script's `isLoading` returned that paused byte, so game time stopped while it held. The two bytes are the low bytes of `g_currentLevel` (0x1029C558) and `g_loadScreenType` (0x102034F8). qwark reads both every tick and emits LOAD_START and LOAD_END on the same two edges, which the client turns into a hold.

What the condition means, from the executable. `g_loadScreenType` is the movie the loading screen plays. `LoadScreen_Init` puts 0 there for a load that flies from one planet to another (`LoadScreen_SetupFlight`), or a `LoadScreen_PickType` pick when there is no level to fly from; `LoadScreen_Update` picks another each time one ends while the load is still going, and puts 1, the arrival, once `LoadScreen_LoadDone` (from `LevelTransition_waitLoad`) has said the load is done. The 1 stays until the next load screen. The picks are 2 and up and never 3 (`g_loadScreenTypeCount` of them), so "above 1" means the long load screens, the ones a slow load cycles through.

Its quirks are kept, not fixed:
- Only a load whose level byte reads 0, a load into Metropolis, stops game time, because `g_currentLevel` already holds the destination while the screens play.
- The condition knows nothing else, so it holds with a level up or on the title too if both bytes say so.
- Each value is judged by its low byte alone: a level of 0x100 is 0, a screen type of 0x201 is 1.

The old SPRX judged the condition only between its boot gate and a quit, from a "not loading" start each time, and qwark does the same: nothing until this process has shown its front end or a level (the gate of the quit's RESUME, below), then an edge on the first tick the condition holds. It sent the edge before a split found in the same poll, and so does qwark. The old SPRX kept one paused byte for both the long load and the quit; qwark keeps two pairs, so a quit in the middle of a long load sends the long load's LOAD_END and the quit's PAUSE in the same moment, in that order, which leaves game time stopped exactly as the one byte did. Nothing in the old splitter took a whole load off game time, so revision 1.28's NORMALISE 0 load row is gone.

**Planet changes and planet resets.** Code 1 goes out as a load begins, with the destination `g_currentLevel` holds by then, and only when the destination is not the level the player was on. qwark keeps that origin itself, like Deadlocked's: the level each load went to, and nowhere while the title is up.
- A planet reloaded (the same level loaded again, by the game or a save) is never a second split.
- A death or a checkpoint is no load (above) and emits nothing, though it makes a new `lua_State`.
- The load out of the title, a save's or a new game's, has no origin and never splits, and neither does the quit to the title, which loads no level.
- The level word alone moves nothing: `Lua_set_save_level` writes it without a load.

**Revisits (revision 1.33, pending).** A planet change into Metropolis (0) or Cobalia (1) carries the event's flags bit0, REVISIT, when `LVL_FAST_SHIP_FIXED` (level flag 106: `g_saveData`+0x5530, bit 42 of the big-endian word, byte 0x102113A2, bit 2) is set as the load begins, and no other planet ever does. That flag is the game's whole test for the two planets' arrival FMVs: `LevelTransition_begin` (0x3C8360), the transition tier's start on every planet load, plays `g_transitionFmvsMetropolis` (0x10073608: `l01_s01_ntro_fmv`) or `g_transitionFmvsCobalia` (0x100735FC: `l02_s01_hypr_fmv`, `l02_s02_zoni_fmv`) in place of the load screen when `g_cmdLevelTransition` is on, bit 42 is clear and the destination's name is "metropolis" or "cobalia", and every time it is so; once the ship is repaired on Fastoon (the flag's name; the level script was not read) neither plays again. A new file and a challenge mode (`SaveData_prepareChallengeMode` builds its file from a fresh one, flags and all) have it clear. So the bit is clear on the first arrivals, and on any load of those planets before the ship's repair, which plays the FMV again; it is set on every load of them after. The flag is read at the load's start with the destination, as the run's flag is, so a save loaded over a level is judged by its own flags. Neither Fast loads nor Remove cutscenes moves it: the first cuts the FMV short and the second blanks its file's path, and `LevelTransition_begin` still takes the FMV path. The game's seen-movies list (`g_saveData.seenMovieHashes`, +0x8600, `LevelFlags_markMovieSeen`) is not used: `LevelTransition_begin` adds the FMVs to it before they play, which can come before qwark's first look at the load. Only those two planets have such an arrival; every other planet's load is the flight, whatever the save says, and its own arrival cinematic is the level script's, after the load. A death, a reload and the load out of the title still emit nothing, so the bit never makes a split of something that was not one.

A level is up while the gameplay Lua VM's `lua_State` (0x102043D0) is non-zero: `levelInit`, at the end of `Level_StartGameplay(…, 0)` under `Level_Enter`, makes it, and `Level_Exit` puts it back to 0, on every planet load and every death. The title is `g_inFrontEnd` (0x1037A459).

**The end of a run (revision 1.29).** Code 7, "Tachyon defeated", is the screen black after Tachyon's last hit, which is where the community's timing ends. Once Tachyon's death has played out, `TachyonEnding_update` (0x4018E4, the ending's controller, vtable 0x10074AB8) queues the realtime cinematic `l4b_s04_dimx_rt` with a fade out, then the FMVs `l4b_s05_end1_fmv` and `l7b_s01_end2_fmv`, then asks for the credits. `MoviePlayer_update` (0x84974) starts the fade in its state 1 and stays there until `ScreenFade_GetProgress`, the float at [`g_bigObj5B8F0` 0x10740BB4]+0x5B62C, reads 1.0; then it asks for `g_gameState` 9 and plays the cinematic (state 2), fades back in (3) and is done (4). qwark reads the player's state and movie (+0x58, +0x64) every tick while a level of Fastoon II is up; the movie is an index into the level's `g_movieRecords` (0x101D50C4, rebuilt at every level load by `MovieTable_build`), whose entry's name is the `char*` just before it, so the index is named once and then compared. Code 7 goes out on the first tick that the player plays `l4b_s04_dimx_rt` and is either in state 1 with the progress at 1.0, the game's own test, or already past it (state 2 to 4, for a tick that missed state 1). Nothing earlier fades out for that cinematic: the boss fight's own cinematics are other movies. It goes out once per level up, and a first look that finds it already under way (a module that came in late) learns it.

Codes 2, 5 and 8 are retired (revision 1.29) and never reused. Code 2 was Tachyon's health reaching 0 on Fastoon II (a `vtbl_TachyonBoss` object, 0x10060CB0, in `g_mobyPool` 0x1066C300, its health at +0x24, which `TachyonBoss_OnDamage` takes to 0 only on the killing hit), about 2.5 s before code 7; code 8 was the ending FMV `l4b_s05_end1_fmv` taking the game state, after the whole cinematic. Build 57 called code 7 "Final fade to black" and code 2 "Tachyon defeated"; a client keying saved choices by label finds a choice made for the old code 2 on code 7. Code 5, "Credits", off by default, was `g_gameState` (0x10387F4C) becoming 11, which only `Lua_show_credits` asks for (`GameState_RequestCredits`), outside the title, with the level the credits came from as its `arg`; the watcher no longer reads the game state for it, and a choice a client saved for "Credits" matches no row and is ignored.

Code 6 is every mission completed (`g_saveData`+0x10AF8 + level × 0x7C: ten 12-byte entries, label text id, description text id, flags, then a u32 count), off by default; the first look at a level only learns what is there.

**The quit to the XMB.** Code 3 is the title index's pause pair, as Deadlocked's is. PAUSE goes out as the session leaves the game: the game's quit byte `g_quitRequested` (0x1037A8E8, which the old SPRX also read, through the pointer at 0x008BD908), or the process going. The old ToD autosplitter patched `Memory_InitHeaps` to set a byte and unpaused when it did, and the LC script added 19.95 s to game time on the QuitPending-to-InGame edge. `FrontEnd_Enter`, which calls `Memory_InitHeaps`, sets `g_inFrontEnd` in the same frame, so the RESUME goes out on the first tick that byte reads non-zero, or the `lua_State` does, should the title come and go unseen. Neither is written, so a console and RPCS3 time a quit the same way, and NORMALISE 19 950 000 makes every quit cost the old script's 19.95 s.

**Under RPCS3** the pair behaves as Deadlocked's does there. RPCS3 has no XMB: stopping the emulated game (or the game's own exit) is the quit, and PAUSE goes out as the game goes. Booting it again is the way back, and the RESUME waits for its front end as on a console. Pausing the emulator is not a quit (a paused game is still a game present), so it emits nothing. A long load open at the quit closes before the PAUSE, as on a console. As with Deadlocked, a PINE link lost for good (5 s of silence) also reads as the game going.

Revision 1.28's mission split, play-time start and whole-load row came from the NPEA00452 executable alone. Revision 1.29's long load is the old SPRX's code, and its Tachyon signal was read on a running game under RPCS3. Revision 1.29's control start and fade-to-black end come from the executable and the game's own scripts and movie tables (the Fastoon II movie list read under RPCS3: index 6 is `l4b_s04_dimx_rt`), and were timed against the user's console measurements of the community's timing (control about 10.5 s after the load began, black about 2.45 s after the last hit).

## 9. The console menu (revision 1.22)

A menu on the TV that gives a console with no PC beside it what the client offers. qwark owns it: the pages, what is on them, what a row does, where the player is. A small **renderer** qwark writes into the game draws the current page with the game's own menu widgets and posts back what the player pressed, through a **mailbox** in game memory. Nothing of this crosses the network: a client sees what the menu changes the way it sees any change, in telemetry, and this section is for information.

Deadlocked (section 9.5), Up Your Arsenal (section 9.7), Going Commando (section 9.8) and Ratchet & Clank (section 9.9) have renderers in build 54, and only on a console; Tools of Destruction (revision 1.29, section 9.12) has its own pause menu as its menu, which is no renderer of qwark's and works under RPCS3 too. Under RPCS3 there is no other menu (section 3.2).

### 9.1 What the menu shows

Pages come in two **types**, which the mailbox carries with each page (section 9.4):

- **MAIN**, a short list of links and buttons, drawn with the game's own list of links. Each row is one line, "label: value".
- **CONFIG**, a list of labels and controls in two columns, drawn with a two-column screen of the game's own (Deadlocked's Options > GAME SETTINGS, UYA's Options > AUDIO / VISUAL, RaC2's Special > Cheats, RaC1's Goodies > Cheats). Every game's CONFIG look scrolls, so a CONFIG page shows more rows at once; no game's MAIN look does (round 4), so a list that can be longer than a MAIN look holds is a CONFIG page (below).

A renderer says which it has; one with only MAIN is sent every page as MAIN.

The pages, for a game with a menu table (all four games; `struct game_menu` in src/games/game.h):

| Page | Type | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| The root | MAIN | titled Trainer (round 3; it was RaCMAN): the game's sections (Game, Cheats), then Planets, Manips, Unlocks and Save files where the game has them. The grey last row with the module's build ("qwark build 54"), which the testing had, was built out for client 1.4.0 and is gone since 1.4.1 |
| Game | CONFIG | the quick block of the client's Game panel: the position slot, Save position and Load position, then Challenge mode as a switch (round 6, the user's call, "not really a cheat": RaC2, UYA, Deadlocked; round 5 had a blank row there; RaC1 has no Challenge mode, so its Game has the Goodies menu there instead, its equivalent, a switch that used to be on Cheats), then Set aside file and Load set-aside file (RaC1: and Force autosave). Round 5 left out Die, which the combos and the client have, and the game's values, which Cheats has as buttons; round 4 had Die first and the values last (UYA: Bolts, Challenge mode, Health XP, Health, File time) |
| Cheats | CONFIG | the four cheats every game has, then the game's other cheats (UYA: the quick-select pause; RaC2: Reset bosses on death, Reset platinum bolts on death; RaC1 has none since round 6, its Goodies menu having moved to Game); round 5: then the buttons: a Max button for each of the game's values (Max bolts in every game; RaC2: Max raritanium; Deadlocked: Max Dread points), then the game's other actions (none since build 59, which retired Deadlocked's Unlock all planets). Round 5 had Challenge mode between the toggles and the buttons; round 6 moved it to Game. RaC2's Set respawn point and Store Swingshot are left out (round 5) |
| Planets | CONFIG (round 4; MAIN before) | the planets in the game's order, each a button that loads it once the menu has closed: every planet PLANET_LIST names, in its menu order (UYA's vid comics last), the placeholders left out. Deadlocked listed only the planets the save had unlocked until build 54's planet load learnt to unlock a locked one first (section 5.5). Where the renderer opens the game's own planet picker (mailbox version 3, below), the root's Planets row opens that instead, and this page is the fallback |
| Manips | CONFIG | the category (None or a group), Auto-reset (round 5: Off, On or On boot, MANIP_OPTIONS' auto-reset and remember as one row; Remember had a row of its own) and Apply now, as MANIP_SELECT, MANIP_OPTIONS and MANIP_APPLY have them (section 5.14); then, where MANIP_LIST's headers have features DESCRIBE has (Deadlocked's Fixes, UYA's Trophies), a blank row (round 5) and those features, an ACTION with a status readout followed by a grey row of that status in the game's words, labelled with the readout's name (UYA: Reset trophies, then "Trophy reset: Ready, 13 s", then Reset trophies on Veldin load). RaC1 and RaC2 have no headers, so no blank row |
| Unlocks | MAIN | UNLOCK_LIST's categories that have rows, each a link, then the game's actions on the whole table: on this page as buttons where the game's menu table names no page for them (no game since round 6; Deadlocked's two were here until then), or, where it does (`unlock_actions_title`, round 3), one link to that page (every game: Collectables) |
| The Unlocks actions' page | CONFIG | round 3: the game's actions on the whole table as buttons, their labels in the label column (UYA's Collectables: Max and Reset all weapon levels, Unlock and Reset all skill points and all titanium bolts; RaC2's: Max all weapon levels and ammo, Unlock and Reset all platinum bolts, nanotech boosts and skill points; RaC1's: Unlock and Reset all gold bolts and all skill points, and since revision 1.34 Upgrade all weapons to gold and Reset all gold weapons; round 6, the user's call, Deadlocked's: Max all weapon levels and Max all weapon ammo; revision 1.34, ToD's skill point and gold bolt buttons, then Max and Reset all weapon levels and Max all weapon ammo) |
| An unlock category | CONFIG | each unlock a selector: LOCKED or UNLOCKED, or for a row with the game's level field the game's own values, its version shown as the game numbers it (Deadlocked LOCKED, V1, V10, V99; UYA LOCKED, V1 and the weapon's own last version, V8 or the R3YNO's V5; RaC2 Locked, V1 and the weapon's own last version, V4, or V2 for the Clank Zapper and the five RaC1 carry-overs; round 5, the user's call: UYA and RaC2 said UNLOCKED or Unlocked and MAX or Max); a gadget or an item, which has no level, LOCKED or UNLOCKED in the game's casing; RaC1 Locked, Unlocked and Gold on the gold flag, an item with no gold version Locked or Unlocked. A version between shows as itself ("V3") and steps to the next on the list; an owned item whose version cannot be read back (UYA's GameCube weapons) shows UNLOCKED, and Right on it writes its last version, which it goes on showing as UNLOCKED |
| Mods | CONFIG | build 56, a preview feature (section 3.1.2; in a release from build 57, and a module built without the preview features has no such row or page): the running game's mods, below |
| Save files | CONFIG (round 4; MAIN before) | the console library's categories (section 5.13), each a link, its name the label |
| A save category | CONFIG (round 4; MAIN before) | its saves, each a button, its name the label and its value blank: SAVEFILE_RESTORE once the menu has closed, which leaves the save in the aside buffer, so Load set-aside file loads it again. A row is the save in its place in the folder; 1.4.1: a press counts only while the category lists what it listed when the page was built, and after an upload, a rename or a delete is refused as an event on an old page is, and the page built again shows the change |

Cosmetics are left out, and so are combos for now; mods have a page (below; build 56's test builds, and every build from 57). Every game has a menu table; round 1's root of DESCRIBE groups, each a page of its features, for a game without one, is gone from the module (the dead-code cleanup, build 56), and its page ids stay reserved.

**The Mods page** (build 56, a preview feature: `QWARK_PREVIEW`, section 3.1.2; in a release from build 57; a module built without the preview features, build 56's release among them, carries none of it, and its root is the six rows of 1.4.1). The root's Mods row, after Unlocks and before Save files, is there wherever a mod can go into the game, so never under RPCS3, where there is no menu anyway. Opening the page reads the console's library and its USB drives again, as MOD_RESCAN does (the drives only when the last look is over a second old), so a mod copied in by FTP or a drive put in since shows. One row a mod, as MOD_LIST and MOD_USB_LIST list them: every installed mod by its index, then every mod only a USB drive has (section 5.7.2), each labelled with its `#- name` (the folder's name without one), in the casing of the game's CONFIG rows. A row is a checkbox of the Manips page's Auto-reset words: **Off** not loaded, **On** loaded, **On boot** loaded and auto-loaded at every boot (MOD_SET_AUTO's flag, written into config.txt by the same code). A mod not loaded reads Off whatever its auto flag says, as a toggle does. The cross and Right go forward, Left back, wrapping, through MOD_LOAD's, MOD_UNLOAD's and MOD_SET_AUTO's own code: Off to On loads, On to On boot sets the flag, On boot to Off unloads and clears it, Left from Off loads and sets it. A long list is windowed like any CONFIG page (`cfg_rows` at once, 16 to 32, and up to 128 rows; QWARK_MAX_MODS and MODUSB_MAX are 32 each). A client sees every change as it sees its own: MOD_LIST's LOADED and AUTO and SessionInfo's `mod_loaded` and `mod_auto`.

A load from the page keeps MOD_LOAD's rules - a mod whose `patch.txt` qwark cannot parse is refused (`IO_ERROR`), as is one that needs (`#- depends`) a mod the console does not have (`NOT_FOUND`), and a mod only a USB drive has is copied in first, all or nothing - and adds two (`mods_load_guarded`, src/core/mods.c). **No part of a mod goes over anything already in the game**: not over a word of a code patch qwark has applied (a loaded mod's words, a feature's, a client's PATCH_APPLY), not over a loaded mod's cave, and not over qwark's own helpers (the menu's renderer, its caves with their zeros and its hook words, and the savefile helper's caves and hooks), which are noted as they go in (`mem_own`, src/core/mem.h). Every word of a mod is looked at before anything of it is written, and each piece of a cave before it is written, so a refused mod has written nothing anywhere something was. This is the rule the client keeps only under RPCS3 (its patch file); MOD_LOAD from a client does not keep it. **A load that fails takes back what it put in**: the dependencies it loaded first are unloaded again. A refusal shows in its row for two seconds, as any row's does ("not found", "failed", and "refused" for an overlap), and as a console notification with the reason: `Trainer: <name> would write over <what>`, `Trainer: <name>: a mod it needs is missing`, `Trainer: <name>: its USB copy can't be used`, or `Trainer: <name>: <the status>`. Unloading is MOD_UNLOAD's, as the client's is: nothing stops a mod another loaded mod depends on from going.

**Round 5** (the user's hardware notes on round 4, build 54 as before). A game's menu table (`struct game_menu`, src/games/game.h) can list a VALUE feature as something other than a stepper (`struct menu_value`, `MENU_ITEM_VALUE(i)`): a **button** that writes one value, or a **switch**. Every game's values are listed so, and none is a stepper on its pages any more (round 1's pages of DESCRIBE groups stepped them; since the cleanup in build 56 the module has no stepper at all):

| Game | Switch | Buttons, and what they write |
|---|---|---|
| Ratchet & Clank | - | Max bolts 9,999,999: the game keeps no cap, and the HUD's bolt counter is registered with 9,999,999 as its limit wherever it shows (0x0C8444's last argument, from the game's update 0x1675C8, the vendor 0x16AB38 and seven more sites): seven digits |
| Going Commando | Challenge mode | Max bolts and Max raritanium 9,999,999 each: the game caps neither, and the HUD's counter prints with "%d" into a layout of one to seven characters (0xCC2678, the length less one held to 0..6) |
| Up Your Arsenal | Challenge mode | Max bolts 99,999,999: the game's own cap, which its update (0x286D08) puts on the bolts every frame (0x287C04) |
| Deadlocked | Challenge mode | Max bolts 99,999,999: the game's own cap (its update 0x2B1F30, from the frame's 0x1CE638 and three more, at 0x2B2400). Max Dread points 99,999,999: nothing caps them, and the game adds rewards to them and tests them signed against its unlocks (the highest 250,000), so the bolts' cap, well clear of a wrap negative |

A switch is On while its field reads above 0 and Off at 0; Off writes 0, On writes the game's first value, 1 (Challenge mode: a file not in challenge mode becomes its first run's, as the game's own challenge-mode start adds one, RaC2's 0xBBB798; a file on its seventh run reads On, and Off makes it 0). Cheats lists the toggles first, then every button (round 5 had the switch between them). A blank row, an INFO row that is not ENABLED with nothing in it, parts a page's parts: Manips' category from the headers' features (and in round 5 Game's position rows from its save-file rows). The values left the Game page, which also left out Die and UYA's and RaC2's Health XP, Health and File time; RaC2's Cheats left out Set respawn point and Store Swingshot.

**Round 6** (the user's hardware notes on round 5, build 54 as before). In every game, Game has no blank row: the Challenge mode switch is where it was, between the position rows and the save-file rows, and Cheats is the toggles, then the buttons (RaC1 has no Challenge mode, so in its place Game has the Goodies menu, RaC1's equivalent by the user's word: its Game is Position slot, Save position, Load position, Goodies menu, Set aside file, Load set-aside file, Force autosave, and its Cheats the four cheats and Max bolts; the Goodies menu is the same LIVE toggle, in DESCRIBE's Cheats group and the client's panels as before, only the console menu's tables moved it; NG+ goodies, a readout, was never in the menu). Deadlocked's two Unlocks buttons, Max all weapon levels and Max all weapon ammo, are on a page of their own, Collectables, as the other games' are. Manips keeps its blank row. Deadlocked's planet picker lists no tournament but DreadZone Station's own row, and its legend has no SKILLS (section 9.5). Nothing in the mailbox changes.

Round 4 moved Planets, Save files and a save category to CONFIG: no game's MAIN look scrolls (UYA's main pause holds nine rows, RaC1's panes and RaC2's bars seven, Deadlocked's list eight), and each of those lists can be longer. The MAIN pages left, the root and Unlocks, fit every game's MAIN look (six rows and five at most). The Planets page stays built in one place (`page_items`' MENU_PAGE_PLANETS case), the native planet pickers' fallback.

**The game's own planet picker** (mailbox version 3, build 54; Deadlocked's Worlds so far, section 9.5). A renderer that can open its game's own planet picker says so in `caps` (bit4 PICKER), and qwark flags the root's Planets row PICKER (row flag bit6). The cross on that row opens the game's picker over the menu, the way the game's own pause opens it, with the game's sounds, its locks shown as the game shows them (but on what the picker loads anyway: Deadlocked's planets, since the pickers' round 2) and every planet qwark can load listed (round 6: Deadlocked's tournaments, which all go to DreadZone Station, left out but for that station's own row). Back from the picker returns to the menu's root, the cursor on Planets; Start or Select closes everything, as anywhere. Picking a planet closes the game's menu the way the picker's own Start closes it, and the renderer hands qwark the planet (`pick_planet`, then `pick_seq`, section 9.4); qwark loads it once the game plays again, exactly as a row of the Planets page loads it (PLANET_LOAD's code, the running game's selected flags, fast loads forced, a locked Deadlocked planet unlocked first). While the picker is up the menu's `hstate` reads 2, PICKER: the menu is not closed, so qwark keeps its stack and page, and the picker's going counts as the menu's close. Everything a renderer changes in the game for its picker acts only on a picker the menu opened: the game's own, from its own pause, is left exactly as it is. Where the renderer cannot open its picker there and then (UYA's map with a Start close pending; Deadlocked's Worlds opens even where the game's own pause greys its WORLDS row, section 9.5), the row posts ACTIVATE as any link does, and qwark opens the Planets page.

**The pickers' round 2** (still mailbox version 3 and build 54). A picker that cannot show every planet qwark loads can send the player to qwark's page of the others: the renderer posts PICKER_OTHERS (event kind 9) on the Planets row instead of ACTIVATE, and qwark opens that game's page of them, a CONFIG page of buttons that load as the Planets page's do (struct game_menu's `picker_others`; page id MENU_PAGE_PLANETS with argument 1). Back from it returns to the root, the cursor on Planets. A game that names no such page gets its whole Planets page. UYA's GALACTIC MAP has no vid comics; its Square, which says VID-COMICS in the Trainer's map, opens Vid-Comics, the base vid comics 1 to 5 (section 9.7).

The controls:

| Control | Row | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Checkbox | TOGGLE: Off, On, and Auto for a toggle that has an auto flag (all but LIVE ones) | the cross and Right go to the next, Left to the one before, wrapping. Auto is on with its auto flag ticked, the client's "A" box; Off clears both. Through FEATURE_SET and FEATURE_SET_AUTO's own code. Round 5, the same control for a switch over a VALUE (Challenge mode: Off or On, as above, through FEATURE_SET) and for Manips' Auto-reset (Off, On, On boot, through MANIP_OPTIONS) |
| Selector | CHOICE: the current option | Left and Right go to the previous or next, wrapping; the cross goes to the next, as the game's Cheats screen does (an ENUM row that opened its options as a page of their own on a MAIN page is gone since the cleanup in build 56) |
| Button | ACTION | the cross runs it, and its value stays blank (round 5, the user's call: the game's own sound for the cross is the cue; round 4 showed "done", and "after closing" for a load). A refusal stands in place of its value for two seconds ("busy", "not in game", "not available", "refused", "failed", "not found", "full"), and in the page's hint as a sentence; so does a refused step of any other row. A button flagged CLOSES (a load: the set-aside file, a planet, a save) closes the menu: qwark asks the renderer to, and runs it once the game is back in ordinary play, or drops it five seconds on with a console notification. A Max button (round 5) writes its value through FEATURE_SET |
| Value | VALUE, a stepper (round 5: on round 1's pages only; no game's menu table lists one, and since the cleanup in build 56 the module builds none: what follows is history) | Left and Right step it; the cross changes the step, 1, 10, 100 up to 1,000,000 but never past the field's range. A renderer without Left and Right shows the number only. Round 3: a VALUE with bounds narrower than its 32-bit field (explicit ones, or an 8- or 16-bit field) is flagged RANGE and carries its value, its bounds and its step as numbers (section 9.4), and one whose range spans 1000 or fewer is flagged SLIDER as well, for a renderer that draws a native slider (Challenge mode's 0..255, not Bolts). Round 4: a SLIDER row's Left and Right move it a tenth of its range, rounded up, so ten presses cross it end to end (Challenge mode: 26, 0, 26 .. 234, 255), and the cross switches between that and a fine step of 1, which its value shows as "[1]" |
| Text | INFO | nothing. Not ENABLED, so every renderer's cursor passes over it, Up and Down alike (round 5: the blank row between a page's parts is one, drawn as an empty row in the grey of a row the cursor skips) |

Every change goes through the code the protocol's ops go through, so `toggle_state`, the auto flags, the manip options and the previous-session record (section 4.1) are exactly what they would be had a client made the change, and a connected client sees it in its next packet. A VALUE row shows what the menu last wrote until the readout agrees, at most a quarter of a second.

qwark sends text as it is written ("Ghost Ratchet", "Off"); a game's renderer cases it the way that game cases its own menus (section 9.6).

**Navigation.** qwark keeps the stack of pages, eight deep. Select on a link opens its page; back goes up a page, the cursor on the row the page was opened from; back on the root leaves the menu, which the renderer does itself. Round 3: whenever the menu closes - Start or Select, the combo, back off the root, a load that closes it, the game closing its pause - the stack goes back to the root, and the next open, from the game's row or the combo, shows the root with the cursor on its first row. qwark resets it when the mailbox's `hstate` goes to closed, and posts the root at once, so it is there before the menu can open again; an opening it did not see the close of (the renderer's `frames` back near 0 with `hstate` open all along) goes back to the root too, a moment late. The values' steps and a load waiting for the close stay. (Round 2 opened the menu again on the page it was closed on.)

**Pages longer than a renderer takes at once** (round 4) are posted a window at a time, a window of as many rows as the renderer takes of that type (`max_rows`, `cfg_rows`: Deadlocked 16, UYA 24, RaC2 32, RaC1 24 on a CONFIG page), with no More and no Previous row and the page's own title: `pflags` says whether the page goes on above the window (MORE_UP) or below it (MORE_DOWN). When the cursor comes to the window's last row it may stand on and the page goes on below, the renderer posts SCROLL_DOWN with that row (SCROLL_UP for its first, the page going on above), and qwark posts the window that continues the page: going down, the cursor's row second from the window's top, going up second from its bottom, as far as the page's ends allow, so the cursor never lands on an edge the page goes on past; the window moves at least a row. PAGE_PREV and PAGE_NEXT (L1 and R1) move the cursor `m - 2` rows up or down and the window with it, on a page that goes on that way. Every game's CONFIG look scrolls its window natively, so only a page longer than the renderer's window slides: a save category of the console's library, and in Deadlocked, UYA and RaC1 nothing else (UYA's 34 planets once, at 24). A page lists 128 rows at most. Round 1 to 3 had a More row last and a Previous row first, and "Skin 2/4" in the title. A renderer with no back control of its own gets a **Back** row at the end of every page but the root, and one row fewer for the page's own.

### 9.2 Opening, closing and switching off

There are two ways in (three since revision 1.34), and no renderer adds a row to any of the game's own lists:

- **The game's own Help row.** Each game's pause menu has a Help entry with the basic controls, which a RaCMAN user has no use for. The renderer takes it over: it keeps its place, says TRAINER in the casing of its list (round 3; it said RACMAN), and opens the menu instead of Help. Back from the menu's root returns to the pause menu with the cursor on it, and Start or Select closes both.
- **The OPEN_MENU combo** (section 5.9), a shortcut from play. It asks the renderer through the mailbox's request byte: 1 to open while the menu is down, 2 to close while it is up. The renderer takes the request on the game's next frame that can act on it and clears the byte. A request it has not taken half a second later is withdrawn - the game was somewhere the menu cannot open, a cutscene, a load, the game's own pause - so the menu never opens later on its own. With the combo at 0, or since revision 1.23 with the COMBO_ENABLE switch off, the Help row still opens the menu.
- **The page combos** (revision 1.34, section 5.9), the same request with the page already posted, so the renderer opens straight on it: one combo per page of the root. The menu opens on a CONFIG page as well as on a MAIN one, since every renderer takes the type of the first page it draws (`mh_core_start`); round 3's rule that a close puts the root back is unchanged, and an open that never comes puts it back too.

`menu.enabled = 0` in config.txt (section 5.10) switches the menu off. No renderer goes into a game, and one already in has its hook words put back - the Help row's too, which is the game's own Help again - at the first moment it is safe: the menu down, the game not paused, no mode change queued, and the game's current menu screen not the menu's. A menu in use stays usable until the player closes it. With the switch at 0 the combo installs nothing and only says so; at 1 again, the renderer goes back in at the next moment the game is in ordinary play.

**The Help row as a switch** (revision 1.23, build 55). `menu.help = 0` (MENU_HELP, section 5.10) leaves the game's Help row the game's in every game while the rest of the renderer goes in and stays in, so the menu is reached through its combo alone; 1, the default, is the Trainer row of build 54. The Help row's words are the last of each renderer's table (`nhelp` of them in `struct mh_desc`: Deadlocked's label call and its `li r4,36`, UYA's six HELP-row words, RaC2's two, RaC1's two; sections 9.5, 9.7, 9.8 and 9.9), and they are the only words the setting moves. The install writes every other hook word and leaves those to the swap, which runs straight after a good install, in the same tick when it is safe, as it always is for the session's own install, so they still go in last. A change of the setting in a running game swaps those words alone, in the table's order going in and in the reverse coming out, without a reload, **only while nothing can be reading the rows they sit in**:

- the menu down (`hstate` 0: not open, and not under the planet picker it opened);
- the game in ordinary play: its mode word 0 with no mode change queued (Deadlocked and UYA gameMode 0 and the pending mode -2; RaC2 gameMode 0 and its pending mode -2; RaC1 its state 0 and no reopen of the pause queued), so no pause menu is up or being built and no move between its screens is under way;
- none of the renderer's screens up (Deadlocked: the game's current screen not 36; the others, the renderer's own word).

That is the install's moment with uninstall's screen test added. Anywhere else the swap waits, looked at with the mailbox every fourth tick, and goes through at the first tick that passes, so a pause menu on screen when the setting changes keeps its rows until it has closed. The renderer reads none of those words (`check.py` holds it to that), so it draws, opens and closes the same with them in or out; from the combo it opens on its root and back at the root leaves the pause, as it always has. Each Help word must hold the game's word or the menu's, as at an install: going in, a word another mod holds stops all of them, nothing is written, the log says which word, and the swap is tried again ten seconds on; coming out, such a word is that mod's and stays. With `menu.help = 0` the install itself refuses for no word at a Help site, which is then the game's or another mod's business. `menu.enabled = 0` takes every word out either way, and a renderer taken over (section 9.3) has its Help row put right at the first safe tick.

### 9.3 The renderer's install

On a console qwark writes a game's renderer in a few seconds into each session (three), at the first tick after that with the game in ordinary play - gameMode 0 and no mode change queued, in Deadlocked and in UYA alike; in RaC1 its state 0 and no reopen of the pause queued - so nothing is written while one of the game's menus is up, and the Help row is the menu's before the player first opens the pause. A press of the menu combo before then installs at once, in the tick that saw it, and its open request goes out right after the install. Before it writes anything it reads every word the renderer replaces (section 9.5 lists them). Each must hold the game's own word or the renderer's:

- **All the game's own**: the data images go in (the mailbox, the renderer's state and its read-only data, section 9.4), then the first page, then the code, then the hook words, in the order the table gives, so nothing can branch into a half-written cave and the Help row can only open a screen that is already the menu's. As build 54 shipped, in the host tests: for Deadlocked twenty-six writes, 19.9 KB (its planet picker's ten words among them); for UYA twenty-three, 23.6 KB; for RaC2 fifteen, 22.6 KB; for RaC1 sixteen, 25.3 KB (its code in four areas).
  Since revision 1.23 the Help row's words go in through the swap of section 9.2, straight after the rest and only as `menu.help` says, so they are still the last.
- **Some already the renderer's**: this module was loaded again into a running game, or an install was cut short. The Help row's words may be either since revision 1.23, and both are fine. The code in the game must be this build's renderer to the byte. If it is, qwark takes the mailbox over as it stands, answers any event left in it unread, writes the hook words still missing, and carries on; if it is not, the install is refused, since rewriting code the game may be running is a crash. Such a game gets its menu back with a restart. A qwark and a renderer always come from the same build, so they always speak the same mailbox version.
- **Anything else** at any of them (at a Help row's word, since revision 1.23, only while `menu.help` is 1) is another mod's: nothing at all is written and the log says which word. qwark tries again every ten seconds, so the menu comes once the other mod is unloaded; a press of the combo tries at once and shows a notification saying why, once a session.

A renderer's caves are never taken out, and its hook words only for `menu.enabled = 0`, as above, and the Help row's alone for `menu.help = 0` (revision 1.23, section 9.2). A process that ends takes them with it, and the next session installs afresh.

qwark carried the renderers inside the module in build 54 (`src/games/menuhelper_bins.c`, built from `src/games/menuhelper/`), the zeros of the mailbox and the state as counts rather than bytes. Since build 55 (revision 1.23) they are in `/dev_hdd0/qwark/ui.pak`, and the install reads the running game's out of it as it writes it (section 9.10): before anything is compared or written the pak must still name this build and carry this module's renderers, and the game's part of it must be whole, or nothing is written, as for a refusal. Its code goes in a scratch buffer at a time, in pieces of 8 KB, still before every hook word, so an install makes a write or two more than build 54 did (Deadlocked twenty-seven, UYA twenty-five, in the host tests).

### 9.4 The mailbox

A block at a fixed address per game, big-endian, laid out the same for every renderer. The renderer's install writes its header; after that each field has one writer.

| Offset | Field | Writer |
|---|---|---|
| 0x00 | u32 magic, `QMNU` | install |
| 0x04 | u16 version, 3 | install |
| 0x06 | u16 size of the whole block | install |
| 0x08 | u8 `row_cap`, the row slots in the block | install |
| 0x09 | u8 `row_size`, 0x40 (round 4; version 2 had 0x80 as well, for rows with a description) | install |
| 0x0A | u8 `max_rows`, the rows the renderer takes at once on a MAIN page, at most `row_cap` | renderer |
| 0x0B | u8 `caps`, below | renderer |
| 0x0C | u8 `hstate`: 0 closed, 1 open, 2 PICKER (version 3: the menu's screen has stopped under the game's own planet picker, which it opened; not a close) | renderer |
| 0x0D | u8 `request`: 0 none, 1 open, 2 close | qwark sets, renderer clears (qwark clears to withdraw) |
| 0x0E | u8 the renderer's game id | install |
| 0x0F | u8 `cfg_rows`, the rows it takes at once on a CONFIG page; 0 for a renderer with no CONFIG page | renderer |
| 0x10 | u32 `frames`: frames the menu has been up since it last opened (round 3: 0 again at each opening, which tells qwark the menu opened) | renderer |
| 0x14 | u32 `ev_seq`: bumped after the event record below | renderer |
| 0x18 | u8 `ev_kind`, below | renderer |
| 0x19 | u8 `ev_row`: the row's index on the page | renderer |
| 0x1A | u16 `ev_id`: the row's id, echoed | renderer |
| 0x1C | u32 `ev_key`: the page key the event was made on | renderer |
| 0x20 | u32 `ev_ack`: the last `ev_seq` qwark has dealt with | qwark |
| 0x24 | u32 `page_seq`: odd while qwark writes the page, even once it is whole | qwark |
| 0x28 | u32 `pick_seq`: bumped after `pick_planet`, a barrier between (version 3, the planet pickers) | renderer |
| 0x2C | s32 `pick_planet`: the planet picked in the game's own picker, qwark's planet id (PLANET_LIST's index) | renderer |
| 0x30 | u32 `page_key`, below | qwark |
| 0x34 | u8 `nrows`, 1 .. `row_cap` | qwark |
| 0x35 | u8 `cursor`: the row the cursor goes to on a new key | qwark |
| 0x36 | u8 `pflags`: bit0 ROOT, back here leaves the menu; bit1 MORE_UP and bit2 MORE_DOWN (round 4), the page goes on above or below this window | qwark |
| 0x37 | u8 `ptype`: 0 MAIN, 1 CONFIG | qwark |
| 0x38 | char `title`[32] | qwark |
| 0x58 | char `hint`[40]: a line about the page, the last answer as a sentence (no renderer shows it since round 4) | qwark |
| 0x80 | rows[`row_cap`], `row_size` bytes each | qwark |

Version 2 (round 2, still revision 1.22 and build 54) added `cfg_rows` and `ptype` in what were two reserved bytes; version 1 never left a console with a PC beside it. Version 3 (round 4, the same build) drops the description: a row is 0x40 bytes and nothing else, which no renderer missed (none showed one after round 3, and the client has its own); and a long page has no MORE or PREV row any more, `pflags` saying where it goes on and the renderer asking for the next window with SCROLL_UP and SCROLL_DOWN (section 9.1). The kinds 7 MORE and 8 PREV stay reserved and are never sent. Also in version 3, for the planet pickers (build 54): `pick_seq` and `pick_planet` at the end of the header qwark polls (which is 0x30 bytes now), the page from `page_key` on 8 bytes further down and its hint 8 bytes shorter; `hstate` 2, PICKER; caps bit4 PICKER; row flag bit6 PICKER. qwark takes each new `pick_seq` once and loads `pick_planet` once the game plays again (section 9.1); the `pick_seq` it finds in the mailbox at an install is not a pick. The pickers' round 2 (the same version and build) adds event kind 9, PICKER_OTHERS.

A row:

| Offset | Field |
|---|---|
| +0x00 | u8 kind: 0 INFO, 1 LINK, 2 TOGGLE, 3 ACTION, 4 CHOICE, 5 VALUE, 6 OPTION, 7 MORE and 8 PREV (retired in round 4, never sent), 9 BACK |
| +0x01 | u8 flags: bit0 ENABLED (the cursor may land on it), bit1 ON (active: a toggle that is on or Auto, an owned unlock, the current option), bit2 STEPS (Left and Right do something on it), bit3 CLOSES (the menu closes before it acts), bit4 RANGE (round 3: a VALUE with bounds, its numbers at +0x1C), bit5 SLIDER (with RANGE: few enough steps from end to end for a bar), bit6 PICKER (version 3: the root's Planets row, for a renderer whose caps say PICKER: the cross opens the game's own planet picker) |
| +0x02 | u16 id, opaque to the renderer and echoed in its events |
| +0x04 | char label[40]; on a RANGE row the label is 23 characters at most and ends inside its first 24 bytes |
| +0x1C | RANGE rows only, the label's last 16 bytes: u32 value, u32 lowest, u32 highest, u32 step (the step Left and Right move it by). `value - lowest` and `highest - lowest`, modulo 2^32, are the distances along a bar whatever the field's sign |
| +0x2C | char value[20] |

Every string is a fixed slot, NUL-padded and not necessarily terminated when full, so a renderer finds row i's label and value at the same place whatever draws them.

`caps`: bit0 STEPS, it posts LEFT and RIGHT; bit1 PAGE_KEYS, it posts PAGE_PREV and PAGE_NEXT; bit2 BACK, it has a back control of its own; bit3 REQUEST, it opens and closes the menu when asked; bit4 PICKER (version 3), it opens the game's own planet picker from the Planets row and posts the pick. Event kinds: 1 ACTIVATE, 2 LEFT, 3 RIGHT, 4 BACK (on a page that is not the root), 5 PAGE_PREV, 6 PAGE_NEXT (L1 and R1, on a page that goes on that way), 7 SCROLL_UP and 8 SCROLL_DOWN (round 4: the cursor at the window's first or last row, the page going on that way; `ev_row` is the cursor's row), 9 PICKER_OTHERS (the pickers' round 2: on the root's Planets row, `ev_row` and `ev_id` that row's, from a renderer whose picker cannot show every planet; qwark opens the game's page of the others, section 9.1, and refuses it on any other row).

**A page** goes in as three writes: `page_seq` made odd, the page from `page_key` to its last row, `page_seq` made even. A renderer copies a page whole and keeps the copy only if `page_seq` read the same even number before and after it; otherwise it tries again on its next frame. It never hands the game a pointer into the mailbox: the games' text widgets keep the pointer they are given and read it at every draw, while qwark writes the mailbox from another thread whenever it likes. It draws from its own copy, which only it changes, on the game's thread.

**The page key** is the page's id in the top sixteen bits, the first row of the window in the next eight, and a count of qwark's moves between pages and windows in the low eight. A renderer that sees a key it has not shown puts the cursor on `cursor`; the same key again is the same page refreshed, and the cursor stays where the player left it.

**An event** is the record and then `ev_seq` one higher, with a barrier between, and one at a time: a press while `ev_seq` differs from `ev_ack` is dropped by the renderer. qwark reads the header every tick while the menu is up and every fourth tick while it is down. It acts on an event only if `ev_key` is the page it last posted and `ev_id` is the id of row `ev_row` on it; anything else is stale and changes nothing. Either way it writes `ev_ack`, and then the next page, which for a refused or stale event is the same page.

**Live values.** qwark builds a page of features, manips or unlocks after every event, ten times a second while the menu is up and once a second while it is down, and writes it only when its bytes changed. A page of links, planets or saves is built only after an event, since nothing on it moves while the game is paused; Save files reads the console's library once per event, not ten times a second. 1.4.1: and once per build, each folder walked once (a category's page walks the library for its name and the category for its saves), where every row walked both again; a row's two seconds of answer keep its page built ten times a second only while they last, and go with the page (until 1.4.1 they kept every page live until that row was built on its own page again). The page is in the mailbox before the menu opens, so a renderer has something to draw on its first frame.

### 9.5 Deadlocked's renderer (NPEA00423)

It shows qwark's pages as screen slot 36 of GuiMenu_Screens, which the game leaves empty, so the game pauses itself for it, draws it in its pause, holds gameplay input off and plays its own sounds:

- **MAIN** pages with GuiSupport's button list, the list Options and Extras use: up to eight rows, `max_rows` 8. A row is "label: value" in the capitals of the game's lists, the current option of a pick-one list marked "*", a row that is not ENABLED grey with no callback. When a page has another number of rows the renderer calls SetMenuTarget again, the call the game uses to place a list, which lays the rows out again and fits the frame round them, so a page of three rows has the frame of three rows.
- **CONFIG** pages (round 3) with the look of the game's Options > GAME SETTINGS (screen 12): its screen panel, template 37, on canvas layer 15, with its plates, separators, frame, title and button legend, in a GuiState thread of the renderer's own run the way GAME SETTINGS runs its own. GAME SETTINGS' eight rows are fixed places holding five value texts and three sliders, so the renderer hides them (elements 0x15..0x29) and shows the page as a CtlList of the game's two-column row (template 28, the Cheats screen's row, in the same font and size as GAME SETTINGS' rows) over their area, made as wide as the Cheats screen's list: eight rows on screen, up to sixteen, `cfg_rows` 16, scrolling. Labels and values are in GAME SETTINGS' capitals, without its colon (round 4, the user's call; round 3 had "GHOST RATCHET:"), an action with nothing to say blank (round 4; it was "..."). The cursor's row is lit (0x80D0D0D0), a row that is not ENABLED grey (0x80404040) and passed over by the cursor, the rest the game's grey (0x80808080): GAME SETTINGS has no colour for an active row, and round 2's gold is gone. A VALUE row flagged MROWF_RANGE and MROWF_SLIDER (section 9.4) is drawn as GAME SETTINGS draws its volumes, in place of the number: the row's outline and swatch rectangles reshaped into an outline 0.3555 by 0.0288 coloured with the row, a dark track 0.3477 by 0.0192 and a fill in the team colours cut at (value - lowest) / (highest - lowest). GAME SETTINGS has no description line, so the hint does not show; an answer shows in its row's value for its two seconds. Sixteen rows hold every list of Deadlocked's whole (the sixteen bot upgrades, the twelve planets, the weapons); a save category longer than that slides sixteen at a time (section 9.1).

Every new page is the game's own way between screens (round 3; round 2 did it only for a page of the other type): GuiMenu_GoToScreen to screen 36 again, whose stop ends the widget shown and whose start begins the new page's. A list closes with MenuMain_Stop and the next opens with ShowMenu inside the same frame, as Main > Options does, the frame gliding to the new rows and the cursor onto its row; the renderer then holds nothing back (`mh_core_hold(0)`), since the same list shows again at once. A CONFIG page's thread is told to end, plays GAME SETTINGS' closing sound and clears its canvas layer on its way out, and the next starts on the second of two GuiStates with GAME SETTINGS' opening sound. A page refreshed in place keeps its screen. GoToScreen pushes nothing, so back goes where it would have gone.

`row_cap` is 16, `row_size` 0x40 (round 4), `caps` all five bits (bit4 PICKER since build 54's planet picker, below).

| Address | Replaces | Becomes |
|---|---|---|
| 0x9E8B58 .. 0x9E8B67 | screen 36's {fcInit, fcReset, fcStart, fcStop}: 0, 0, 0x991D40, 0 | 0, 0, the renderer's start, the renderer's stop |
| 0x368620 | `bl HandleMenuInput` in GuiSupport's UpdateMenus | a call to the renderer's frame, which goes on to HandleMenuInput unless it took the frame's input |
| 0x35E074 | `li r4,1` before GuiMenu_GoToScreen in GuiHud's single-player and co-op Start path | 36 in the frame the renderer pressed Start for an open request, 1 otherwise |
| 0x35DFBC | the nop after GuiHud's call to GB_GetLocalHeroFromSlot, before it tests Start | the open request taken, and Start pressed for the game in that frame |
| 0x3A649C | `bl msg_string` for the label of the pause main menu's fifth row, Help, in MenuMain_StartMain | a call that answers "TRAINER" (round 3; "RACMAN" before) |
| 0x3B2844 | `bl MenuPlanet_HandleSelection` in MenuPlanet_HandleInput: X on Worlds | a call to the renderer's select stub: the menu's pick while the menu opened Worlds, HandleSelection otherwise |
| 0x3B177C | `beq` in MenuPlanet_UpdatePlanets that leaves Ghost Station out unless a byte of the save says otherwise | a branch to a gate: listed while the menu opened Worlds, the game's test otherwise |
| 0x3B1424 | `li r3,15` in MenuPlanet_SetupPlanetTourneyList, the entries it answers | a branch to a gate: 16 while the menu opened Worlds, so its sixteenth entry, Interior, is listed; 15 otherwise |
| 0x3B27C0 | `beq` in HandleInput that ignores Triangle with no mission active | a branch to a gate: Triangle always goes back while the menu opened Worlds; the game's test otherwise |
| 0x3B2898 | the same `beq` for Start and Select | the same gate |
| 0x3B1590 | `stbx r5,r7,r24` in MenuPlanet_UpdatePlanets: a planet entry's bLocked, 1 where its level's status byte has no 0x02 (the pickers' round 2) | a branch to a gate: 0 while the menu opened Worlds, so no planet there has a lock beside it; the game's value otherwise. A tournament's lock (0x3B16C4) stays the game's |
| 0x3B2794 | `beq` in HandleInput to the Skills screen on command 4, Circle (the pickers' round 2) | a branch to a gate: the press ignored while the menu opened Worlds; Skills otherwise |
| 0x3B14F0 | `bne` in MenuPlanet_UpdatePlanets to a tournament's path (an entry's type 1; round 6) | a branch to a gate: tournaments 1 to 4 left out of the list (on to the next entry, nothing inserted) while the menu opened Worlds; tournament 0, DreadZone Station's own row (MenuPlanet_GetTournamentName names it after level 1), and every planet as the game has them |
| 0x3AE500 | `bne` in MenuPlanet_CreateWidgets between its two button legends (no mission active: "\x11 SKILLS \x10 SELECT"; one active: and "\x12 BACK"; round 6) | a branch to a gate: while the menu opened Worlds, "\x10 SELECT \x12 BACK" (msg_string's own texts, called from the gate with the game's r2, into the game's "%s %s" and its sprintf); the game's legend otherwise |
| 0x9E8954 | GuiMenu_Screens[3].fcStop, MenuPlanet_Stop's descriptor 0x994400 (a data word) | the renderer's descriptor: Worlds leaving for anything but screen 36 ends the menu's picker, then the game's stop |
| 0x3A8584 | `li r4,30` in MenuMain_MainCMDHelpCB, the Help row's callback, before GuiMenu_PushScreen | `li r4,36`: the row pushes the menu's screen over the main menu |

The Help callback's descriptor (0x993FD0) is named by that one row and nothing else, so no other list changes; the multiplayer pause menu builds rows of its own with no Help, and keeps every word. The main menu keeps its eight rows in their order. The six Worlds words (build 54; eight since the pickers' round 2, the lock's and Circle's gates after the four; ten since round 6, the tournaments' and the legend's after those) go in after the Start path's and before the Help row's; each acts only while the core's picker byte says the menu opened Worlds, and does exactly the game's own otherwise, so the pause's own WORLDS row opens the game's Worlds as it always has. Probe PL4 holds two of them (X and the stop) and its list mod the four first gates, so the install refuses while either is loaded.

**The planet picker** (build 54, mailbox version 3; probe PL4 ran it on hardware). The root's PLANETS row pushes the pause menu's own Worlds screen (screen 3) over screen 36, GuiMenu_PushScreen(p, 3), as the main menu's WORLDS row does (0x3A8368), with the row's 7 and Worlds' own opening sound; screen 36's stop on the way is not a close. Worlds lists what the game lists - its planets and DreadZone's tournaments, a locked tournament greyed with its requirements (a locked planet too until the pickers' round 2, below) - and, opened from the menu, Ghost Station and Interior too. Round 6 (the user's call: every tournament goes to the same place): opened from the menu it lists no tournament but tournament 0, which is DreadZone Station's own row, so its list is DreadZone Station, the planets, Ghost Station and Interior; and its legend is "\x10 SELECT \x12 BACK", with no SKILLS (Circle does nothing there) and BACK even with no mission active (Triangle always goes back there). The cursor, the scrolling and the side panel are the game's CtlList's and follow the shorter list: every focus the game sets (the current level's or tournament's entry, the first new one, one saved before Skills or Missions) is found by searching the list's entries or clamped to its length, never taken as a fixed place. On it:
- **X** takes the focused entry: a planet hands its level to qwark (co-op's 21..35 as 1..15), locked or not; an unlocked tournament hands DreadZone (1), which holds them, and a locked one gets the game's refusal sound (4) and nothing more (since round 6 the only tournament there is DreadZone Station's own row). Then Worlds closes the way its own Start closes it (the main menu's sound flag, g_fromPlanet, g_fromOther and g_fromMissionScreen cleared, CloseMenu(p, -1); Worlds' stop plays its 6), and qwark loads the planet once the game plays again, unlocking a locked one first (section 5.5). There is no travel dialog and no Missions screen: the planet's arrival puts the player in its first open campaign mission, or on its Missions screen when every one is done.
- **Triangle** goes back to the menu's root, the cursor on PLANETS, even with no mission active (the game's own Worlds then ignores Triangle and Start: the trap after a planet's last mission). **Start** and **Select** close everything, with no mission active too. **Circle** (Skills) does nothing (the pickers' round 2, the user's call; in round 1 it opened the game's Skills screen, back from which Worlds was the game's own).
- **No locks** (the pickers' round 2): every planet is drawn as an unlocked one, with no lock beside it (the game's NEW mark where the save has never visited it) and its own panel of text rather than its requirements, since X loads any of them. Locked tournaments keep their locks, X on one being refused.
- **Where the pause greys its WORLDS row** (MenuMain_StartMain, 0x3A619C..0x3A63B0) - in Interior (15, co-op 35), and in DreadZone (1, 21) until its mission 37 has been completed (the byte at 0xB281B5) - PLANETS opens Worlds all the same (the first version of the picker refused there, to mirror the pause, and opened qwark's Planets page instead). The pause's own WORLDS row is still greyed there. The greying is the pause's rule, not a limit of Worlds: in DreadZone's tutorial the game's X would only close the menu (MenuPlanet_HandleSelection's first act, once the entry is unlocked, is the plain close unless DreadZone's missions 8 and 37 have completions; the pause greys MISSIONS by the same test, and the PS2 prototype did not yet grey WORLDS there), which the menu's X never reaches, since it takes the press before HandleSelection; the trap (no mission active) is passed by the Triangle and Start gates as anywhere.
