The tick thread runs the game's code under the lock; the network threads' uses are DESCRIBE, FEATURE_SET_AUTO and FEATURE_OPTIONS (the feature registry's own copy of the pointer), AUTOSPLIT_DESCRIBE, POS_LIST, PLANET_LIST, MOBY_TABLE and the manip ops, all under the lock since revision 1.26. The holders that kept their own copy, the feature registry, the console menu (and its unlock list) and the manips, drop it under the lock as the module goes, and so do the patch table's entries for the game's toggles, whose definitions live in the state area; the registry keeps what is the core's (the toggle state, the auto flags, the LIVE mask the same game's next record needs). The simulator overwrites an unloaded module's table with 0xA5 bytes, so the unit tests and the smoke run catch any pointer kept past it.

**qwark.log**, every decision with its figures (`grep "game module"`):

```
game module: rac4.sprx is 1 page(s); it needs 128 KB free plus the 128 KB floor, lowest over 3000 ms
game module: loading rac4.sprx: 812 KB free at the lowest over 3000 ms, 1240 KB now; it needs 128 KB and leaves 556 KB over the 128 KB floor, after 2950 ms of waiting
game module: rac4.sprx loaded in 12 ms (id 0x23010500), 1240 KB free before, 1176 KB after
game module: rac4.sprx waits for memory: 212 KB free at the lowest over 3000 ms (660 KB now), it needs 128 KB plus the 128 KB floor; waiting 3010 ms so far
game module: still waiting after 30 s; the console is told once
game module: rac4.sprx unloaded (the game quit): stop 0x00000000, unload 0x00000000
game module: no rac4.sprx on the console; this game has no game features this session
game module: rac4.sprx is from another build (refusal 3, its ID 0x1A2B3C4D, ours 0x5E6F7A8B)
game module: lv2 would not load rac4.sprx: 0x80010004, 180 KB free
```

A waiting line goes at the first look and then at most every ten seconds.

**The simulator** (`qwark-host.exe`) links its games, as before, until its stdin command `modules on`; from the next boot on it stands modules in for them, through the same gate, against its fake VSH memory (`vshmem`), whose free figure a loaded module takes its pages off. `fakemodule <n>` writes a module for game `n` into the fake HDD, `fakemodule <n> other` one of another build, `broken` one that is no module, `refused` one lv2 will not load, `pages <p>` one of `p` pages and `gone` deletes it; `modules off` links them again.

### 9.12 Tools of Destruction: the game's own pause menu (revision 1.29)

`docs/tod-menu.md` has the reverse engineering, the design and the test lists; `src/games/luahelper/lh_menu.h` the layout. ToD has no renderer of qwark's: its menu is the pause menu, an Anark presentation run by Lua, through the Lua helper (section 5.15) and a Lua glue qwark sends it. To `menu.c` it is a renderer like any other, through the same mailbox (`struct menu_native` in place of ui.pak's entry):

- **Install**, a few seconds into play as for every game: the Lua helper on a console (its four words, out of ui.pak since revision 1.29's Lua helper in ui.pak, section 9.10; on RPCS3 LUA_PATCH's patch, looked for), then the mailbox's header, which qwark writes itself: 0x8CD000, 0x880 bytes, 32 row slots of 0x40 (row cap 32), a MAIN page 9 rows at once and a CONFIG page 6 (since the fourth console test: the pause's list shows nine, Game Options six; a longer page comes a window at a time), caps STEPS, BACK, REQUEST and, since the polish, PAGED (0x2D: a page at a time), renderer 5; a header with other caps is written again. The helper's block at 0x8CE000 (0x890 bytes) is zeroed with it. Nothing is installed without the helper (RPCS3 without the patch: the combo says it needs qwark's RPCS3 patch; a console without a usable ui.pak: the menu's no-pak state of section 9.10, said once on the console, and tried again once a client has sent the pak).
- **The Help row** (Tools of Destruction's Inventory row since revision 1.29's finishing touches): `menu.help` is the byte at 0x8CE000, 1 TRAINER in the pause list where Inventory was (the main list's fourth row, and the space combat and arena lists' fifth), 0 the game's Inventory; Options' Help is the game's (until then the Trainer took the Options list's Help row); it may change at any time and shows the next time the list does. `menu.enabled = 0` clears the mailbox's identity, which the helper and the glue take as no menu.
- **The pause's presentation** (the crash fix): the helper does anything in the pause menu's VM only on a frame where `g_pauseMenuState` is 2 or 3, this is `g_pauseMenuSystem`'s menu system, it has its lua_State, runtime, presentation and Lua bridge, its first update ran, it is not stopping, it has updated twice, and nothing is pushed; and then with the presentation pushed and popped around it. A frame kept out says why in the word at 0x8CE024 (`lh_menu.h` LM_WHY_*) and counts in 0x8CE028; qwark logs a wait of 300 frames once. The glue's note at 0x8CE02C (1 its Trainer row in, 2 no pause list found, the Inventory row left the game's (Help until revision 1.29's finishing touches); since the third console test 3 no Cheats screen on its slide, 4 the Trainer row in with the Cheats screen not met yet, 5 the Cheats screen met on its slide; since the fourth, Game Options where these say the Cheats screen) is logged when it changes; after a 2 or a 3 qwark sends the helper's look (`lh_diag.lua`, a request of target 1, once a session for each) and logs what it saw.
- **The pause menu's Lua pool** (the second crash): before anything goes into the pause menu's VM the helper counts the blocks left in the game's Anark Lua pool (`lh_tod.h` LH_ANARK_POOL), which faults when it runs out: 1,024 for a small object and 16 in its largest class for a frame of the glue, 3,072 and 48 for a request (the glue's load), the game's own full collection first if short (never under 128 and 4), and none again for 60 frames after one that left it short. Short after a collection: the frame is kept out (why 0x080), a glue already in takes itself out (word 0x8CE008 3, LUA_STATUS's err says so), and a request of target 1 is answered `NO_ROOM`. The word at 0x8CE030 is the last count (largest class's blocks << 16 | small objects', each to 0xFFFF), 0x8CE034 +1 at every collection that left the pool short (qwark logs it once a pause), 0x8CE038 the frames before the next collection.
- **The glue**: each pause menu's VM asks for it (the word at 0x8CE008 2, WANTED, and the count at 0x8CE00C moved); qwark sends it once per count as a LUA_EXEC-like request of target 1 (its answer is logged, never a client's). On a console it is read out of ui.pak as it is sent (revision 1.29, section 9.10); a pak gone bad since the helper went in sends none, which leaves Inventory the game's in that pause, and logs why. The word goes to 1 once the glue runs, 3 if it stopped on an error or took itself out for a short pool (LUA_STATUS has the message, script "qwark-menu"). A frame that served a request runs no glue frame.
- **What a renderer does** is the helper's (`lh_menu.c`): the page copied under page_seq, events one at a time with the key and the row's id, hstate (open while the Trainer is up) and frames, the request byte taken. A gameplay frame closes the menu. The combo in play is taken by the game's own pause test, so the game opens its pause exactly as for Start, and the glue opens the menu in it.
- **What it looks like** (since the fourth console test): a MAIN page (the root, Unlocks) is a menu of the pause's own list, the one Options and Extras are, a row's label and its value after it; the cross is ACTIVATE (the list's own select), Triangle BACK, and at the root it returns to the list the Trainer was opened from. A CONFIG page (a section, Planets, Unlocks' categories, the save library) is the game's GAME OPTIONS screen (slide 9): its six rows, a row's label at the left and its value at the right (">" for a link), the page's title in its header, the last answer in its description; Up and Down move as on the game's own options, the cross and Right are ACTIVATE on a row that does not step and RIGHT on one that does, Left LEFT, Triangle BACK; the cursor at a window's last row (or first) asks for the next window. Since the polish (MBOX_CAP_PAGED, docs/tod-menu.md section 15) a page longer than six rows goes a page at a time, as the user wants the game's lists to scroll: Down on a window's last row the cursor can stand on asks for the next window, which comes with the cursor on its top row; Up on its first, the window before with the cursor on its bottom row; Up on the first window's top row the last window's last row and Down on the last window's bottom row the first window's top row (the game's lists wrap); a row the cursor cannot stand on is passed over into the next window. Labels, values and titles are in capitals, as the pause writes its own. L1 and R1 do nothing (no PAGE_KEYS). The glue puts its fields and methods on the list and on Game Options only while the Trainer is up, each recorded, and puts every one back as it leaves (docs/tod-menu.md section 12). Since the fifth console test a MAIN page sits in a frame of its own row count (the list's slide for it), a CONFIG page shows only its rows, and a planet, a save file or Load set-aside picked in the pause closes the pause and loads once the game plays, as does a client's PLANET_LOAD while the pause is up (docs/tod-menu.md section 13).
- **The Planets row: the galactic map** (docs/tod-menu.md 16). Where the game can open it, the root's Planets row opens the ship's galactic map, not the Planets page: a CLOSES action (the pause closed by the glue, as for a load), then, once the game plays, `gmap()` sent to the gameplay VM (a request of the core's own, target 0) with the bytes of struct menu_picker's pokes written first (each level the map lists unlocked and shown, the space combat levels visited, so the map lists every level it can and a pick loads the level picked) and put back once the map has gone (a byte the game changed meanwhile is left as the game left it). `g_gameState` 6 is the map up. A pick is the game's own load; Back (the map gone with `g_galMap.selectedLevel` -1) opens the Trainer again with an open request, as the combo does, on the root with the cursor on Planets, the label Planets and the cross the map again (revision 1.29, every planet: no All planets row). **Every planet** (docs/tod-menu.md 17): once the map is up qwark sends the map's own VM its glue (`lh_gmap.lua`, a request of target 2, MAP, served from the map's frame, the helper's fifth hook), sent again while it answers `wait`; it lists Kerwan and Fastoon II too and makes every pick the level picked (the helper's `qw` 10 into `g_galMap.selectedLevel`). A glue that does not go in leaves the game's own map, logged and said on the console, and Back then opens the Planets page. No level's lua_State or no Lua helper: the Planets page, as before; the map not up four seconds after `gmap()`: the bytes back, the Trainer opened on the Planets page; both logged and said on the console.
